| **EA-2** | **No silent inheritance.** Every mechanism carries one of seven labels: `RETAINED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT` · `MODIFIED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT` · `REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT` · `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE` · `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` · `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` · `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` | Task §25 | **Applied to every row of every design table. # PART I — SOURCE STATE, THE FOUNDER'S RECLASSIFICATION, AND WHAT THIS INSTRUMENT IS

## I.1 The re-verification, performed before anything was designed

**[P]** Bound by **EA-3, EA-4, EA-5**. **The task states that the blueprint *"has now been recovered and is available as a source."* That is a statement about the intake, and the intake was inspected before it was relied on.** Six checks, `PV-1 … PV-6`, at **7 October 2026 17:25 UTC** in sandbox **`iuig05qtw2rpvsllytqz0`** — a third distinct instance.

| **PV-5** | **All remote refs, the complete tree of `origin/arena/d871640c-sss`, and every blob in the object store** | **3 refs. Tip `d4e440343b19ba112f588d27e522a745f78612a0` — this project's own round-4 commit. Tree: 8 paths, 7 markdown instruments and the zip. **10 blobs, 0 beginning `%PDF-`*** |
| **PV-6** | Archive identity | **`sha256 504608e6530d3c512d788c05aa48433df38f6ebaf089d25b8ae0aa1f623d3ea9` — byte-identical, 12 entries, its only PDF still the v1.2 visual edition** |

> ### **`THE BLUEPRINT DOCUMENT IS STILL NOT PRESENT. THIS IS THE FIFTH VERIFICATION ROUND, ACROSS THREE SANDBOX INSTANCES AND TWO DECLARED CHANNELS.`**
>
> **`EV-B-01` stands at `E0 content / E1 assertion / P0 provenance`, `RS-9`. Nothing in this instrument is stated as read from the document, and nothing is reconstructed from it (EA-3, EA-4).**

## I.2 Why the task is nonetheless fully performable — and what changes because of the reclassification

**[P]** For five generations the blueprint's absence was a **blocker**: `LDR-U17` required it, `INPUT-05` was it, `EC-01 … EC-26` were a reconciliation against it, and `CF-19` made the pilot circular because the pilot was supposed to produce the inputs of a document the pilot required. **The founder's correction removes the blocker, not by supplying the file but by changing what the file is for.**

| Field | **Determination** |
|---|---|
| **The founder's statement** | *"That blueprint was designed for a different team and an earlier economic/business concept … It must therefore NOT be adopted, copied, patched, or treated as the current Sewak Sathi economic architecture. It is now a historical/reference source only."* |
| **Classification** | **`FD-1 — FOUNDER RECLASSIFICATION OF A SOURCE'S STATUS (E1)`.** It is a statement about the standing of a document, made by the person who holds it. **It is not a legal conclusion, not an entity determination and not an economic fact** |
| **What FD-1 does** | **It converts the current economic architecture's source requirement from *"recover the blueprint"* to *"build from the current corpus"*.** The corpus was already the governing source (EA-1); FD-1 makes it the **only** operative source for current economics |
| **What FD-1 does not do** | **It does not make the document received. It does not validate anything inside it. It does not resolve `CF-22` *(asserted filename vs corpus title)*, which now matters less but is not closed. It does not authorise anything. It does not ratify itself: the corpus's own amendment rule requires **controlled amendment rather than silent replacement** **[appendix.txt:684–685]**, so FD-1 is recorded as `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE RATIFICATION` — a source-list change is an architectural act and is logged as one** |
| **Effect on `LDR-U17`** | **Re-framed, not resolved.** Its source requirement was *"the economic blueprint"*. **FD-1 removes that requirement for current economics and replaces it with this instrument.** `LDR-U17` as a *recovery* item becomes `SUPERSEDED IN PURPOSE BY FD-1 — RECOVERY NO LONGER REQUIRED FOR CURRENT ARCHITECTURE`; the historical document remains unrecovered and remains of interest only to Part II's decomposition. **This is recorded, not silently applied** |
| **Effect on `INPUT-05`** | **Re-classified from `NOT RECOVERED — REQUIRED` to `NOT RECOVERED — HISTORICAL REFERENCE ONLY (FD-1)`. The input is no longer on the critical path** |
| **Effect on `EC-01 … EC-26`** | **Re-purposed.** They were a reconciliation checklist against a document. **They are now the requirements list this instrument must answer** — and Part II §II.5 maps all 26 to the Part that answers each |
| **Effect on `CF-19` *(the pilot circularity)*** | **Broken at its root.** The circularity was: *the pilot needs the blueprint's economics; the blueprint's economics need the pilot's measurements* **[S-F:1898 SR-21]**. **FD-1 removes the first limb: the pilot's economics come from this instrument and from measurement, not from a document. **`CF-19` is recorded as `ANTECEDENT REMOVED BY FD-1 — CONFLICT NO LONGER OPERATIVE`, which is not the same as resolved, because the second limb — that the pilot must still produce every value — is untouched*** |
| **Effect on the pilot gate** | **None. `GATE P0 = 0 of 10`. 22 of 22 Tier-0 items outstanding. **A reclassification of a document's status is not a PASS condition for any sub-gate (Rule DA-29)*** |

**[A] Finding CEA-F1.** **The single most consequential fact in this Part is not the absence of the file but the change in what the file was for.** Five instruments treated the blueprint as the missing keystone of the economic architecture; the founder has now stated that it was cut for a different building. **That does not make the architecture complete — it makes the architecture *buildable*, which is a different and smaller thing, and it is the reason this instrument exists.** The keystone was never the document; it is the set of decisions in Part XX, Part XXIX and Part XXXI.

## I.3 The decomposition basis — what Part II actually reads

**[P]** Bound by **EA-3, EA-4**. **Part II performs the forensic decomposition the task requires, and it performs it honestly: the items decomposed are the corpus's own preserved representations of the historical blueprint, read directly from the corpus in this session.** Each is labelled with where it was read from and at what level.

| Basis | **What it is** | **Level** | **Where read** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B-1 — The v1.1 source text** | The preserved specification text, which contains the waterfall, the definitions, the control table, the role specifications, the payment cycle, the social-impact fund and the bad-month rule | **E2 — internal record of the architecture, and the governing corpus** | **`appendix.txt` and its twin `extracted_text.txt` — read directly this session** |
| **B-2 — v1.3-B's preserved extractions** | The costed economics instrument, which reproduces the source flow verbatim, quotes the definitions, and derives the model from them | **E2** | **`[S-B: §7.1, §7.2, §7.3, §8.1, §8.2, §8.3, §12, §19, §20.2, §21, §26.2, §29, §33, §34, §35, §36, §37, §38, §39]`** |
| **B-3 — The corpus's statements *about* the historical blueprint** | Four passages that describe it, quote it at one remove, or state that it was not attached | **E2 as to what the corpus says; E0 as to the document** | **`[appendix.txt:682–685]` · `[appendix.txt:6364–6369]` · `[appendix.txt:6974]` · `[S-B:40, 46]`** |
| **B-4 — The v1.2 PDF, read in full** | The only PDF in the corpus: 179 pages, 449,593 characters extracted, `/Author = Arena.ai Agent Mode`, created 30 September 2026. **It is a rendering of B-1 and contains no blueprint content** | **E2 — a derivative of the corpus, not an external source** | **EV-B-01 record §3** |
| **What is NOT a basis** | The document itself · any remembered snippet · any inference from its title · any reconstruction from its name · the illustrative figures treated as its content | **Excluded by EA-3, EA-4, EA-10** | — |

> ### **`A LABEL USED THROUGHOUT PART II`**
>
> **`HBP` — HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT, AS REPRESENTED IN THE CORPUS.** Any mechanism tagged `HBP` was read from B-1, B-2 or B-3, **not from the document.** Where a mechanism is tagged `RETAINED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`, the retention is of **the mechanism as the corpus preserves it**, and the tag is shorthand for *"retained from the corpus's own economic architecture, which the historical blueprint also contained."* **This matters, because most of what survives decomposition is not blueprint property at all: it is v1.1 text, which is the current corpus.**

## I.4 Executive definition

**[P]** The first of the thirty-one required outputs.

> ### **THE CURRENT ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE OF SEWAK SATHI SANSTHA IS:**
>
> **A venture-revenue allocation engine, wrapped in a four-flow separation, governed by a sequencing rule rather than a percentage table, operating inside an organisation that has no legal person, no bank account, no constituted authority and no pilot — and therefore, today, an architecture with no money moving through it.**
>
> **Its purpose is not to maximise revenue. It is to make legitimate economic activity *possible without corrupting the mission*: to pay verified work first, to keep help free, to keep recruitment worthless, to keep the four hands of finance separate, to keep the founder's interest visible and bounded, and to keep every rupee traceable to a collected customer receipt.**
>
> **It is designed, and it is not deployable. The gap between those two states is not analysis. It is `SR-01` — the entity — and everything that follows from it.**

| Field | **The definition, decomposed** |
|---|---|
| **What creates value** | **Verified work delivered to a paying customer under an approved venture, in one of seven source categories, through an approved lifecycle with four gates** |
| **What creates revenue** | **Collected customer receipts. Nothing else. Not recruitment, not membership, not fees to participate, not data, not testimony, not endorsement, not access** |
| **What compensates people** | **Priority 1 of the sequencing rule, on verified work, under a formula that does not yet exist and must exist in writing before anyone is recruited on it (`D-1`)** |
| **What funds the organisation** | **Priority 5 — and the corpus's own finding is that this is the weakest link, because the centre is funded last and spent first. Part XII designs the explicit funding map the corpus proposes and does not have** |
| **What maintains reserves** | **Priority 6, five funds, national custody, release by approval matrix — with the corpus's own caveat that *"Reserve release rules — NOT FINAL"*** |
| **What finances social service** | **Priority 7, from surplus only, into a restricted fund barred from salaries, bailouts and political or religious activity — which is why Part XIII must design a second instrument, because service cannot wait for surplus** |
| **What generates founder upside** | **Priority 8, *"as defined by approved structure"* — and no approved structure exists. Part XV designs the architecture of the question** |
| **What keeps it honest** | **Four separated money flows · four separated finance hands · twelve never-monetised categories · seven anti-MLM rules · sixteen gaming vectors with proportionate controls · eight-field records · veto gates over weights** |
| **What stops it today** | **`SR-01` entity structure · `A-N16` bank mandate · `LDR-U09` worker classification · `LDR-U16` founder compensation authority · `LDR-U18` the compensation formula · `A-N15` reserve custody · `A-N14` data fiduciary · and 22 Tier-0 items** |

# PART II — FORENSIC DECOMPOSITION OF THE HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT

## II.0 Method

**[P]** Bound by **EA-1, EA-2, EA-3, EA-4, EA-10**. **The task requires decomposition before design, and requires that no example be assumed automatically retained.** Every item below was evaluated against one test:

> ### **THE RETENTION TEST**
>
> **Does this mechanism survive when it is placed inside the *current* organisation — eleven named roles across four tiers, no legal person, no account, no constituted authority, four separated money flows, an absolute anti-MLM firewall, twelve never-monetised categories, and a mission that may not be subordinated to revenue?**
>
> **If it survives unchanged → `RETAIN`. If it survives only after a stated change → `MODIFY`, with the change, the reason and the dependency named. If it belongs to the old team, the old concept, or an unsupported number → `REJECT`, with the ground named.**

**[P]** **72 items decomposed: 30 retained, 20 modified, 22 rejected.** Every item names where it was actually read from (basis `B-1 … B-4`, §I.3), because nothing was read from the document (EA-3).

## II.1 RETAIN — 30 mechanisms that survive architectural scrutiny

Tested, not assumed — Part X §X.1** | **Part X** |

| **RT-10** | **Residual surplus last, *"after priority obligations and caps as defined by approved structure"*** | **B-1** §14.3; **B-2 [S-B: §8.2]** | **The definition is self-limiting: it points at an approved structure, and none exists. That is a feature, not a gap** | **Part XV** |
| **RT-11** | **Segregation of four hands — receive / approve / record / review-audit — with the six compensating controls for temporary combination** | **B-1 [appendix.txt:6925–6948]** Diagram 20A | **It is the single most important financial control in the corpus and it is stated as approved architecture, not as a proposal. **Its lower limits, dual acknowledgement, city/state review, receipts-and-bank-proof, random audit and prompt role split are retained verbatim*** | **Part XX.3, Part XXII** |
| **RT-12** | **Funds Manager authority limits — the can/cannot list** | **B-1 [appendix.txt:6950–6958]** §20.4 | **It names the three things the money handler may never do: self-approve expenses, change compensation percentages or the priority order or the leader multiplier, release reserve or social-impact funds without approvals** | **Part XX.2** |
| **RT-13** | **Venture approval tiered by *"category, risk, money, data and novelty"*** | **B-1 [S: Ch.17 step 3]**; **B-2 [S-B: §7.3 point 1]** | **Five dimensions is enough to refuse a venture without a debate, and the categories remain `POTENTIAL` until approved one by one **[S: Ch.33]**** | **Part VIII** |
| **RT-14** | **The six scaling conditions** — *"real customer demand, quality delivery, acceptable risk, documented revenue, controlled costs and sufficient trained people"* | **B-1 [appendix.txt:6287–6292]** §13.5; **B-2 [S-B: §7.3 point 2]** | **The sixth condition is the one that prevents growth from hollowing the organisation, and it is a people condition, not a money condition** | **Part VIII.5** |
| **RT-15** | **The four closure conditions** — *"money is reconciled, customers are treated fairly, workers are paid according to approved rules and reasons are documented"* | **B-1** §13.5; **B-2 [S-B: §7.3 point 3]** | **It makes failure a learning event instead of a scandal, and it is the only exit rule the corpus has** | **Part VIII.6, Part XVIII** |
| **RT-16** | **The payment sheet with formula version, approvals and evidence retained** | **B-1 [S: Ch.19 step 13]**; **B-2 [S-B: §26.2 MVC-7]** | **A payment that cannot be shown to be correct destroys trust irreversibly; the formula *version* field is what makes an accrual auditable** | **Part XXI** |
| **RT-17** | **Anti-gaming controls already in the source** — *"collected legitimate customer revenue"*, *"receipts, approval, direct-cost link and anomaly review"*, *"no artificial splitting"*, related-party exclusion, *"verified members/approved participants only"*, publication *"after anomaly review, corrections and appeals"* | **B-1 [S: Ch.25]**; **B-2 [S-B: §36.1]** | **Six controls already exist and four of the sixteen gaming vectors are already covered. Part XIX extends rather than replaces** | **Part XIX** |
| **RT-18** | **Reconciliation to bank/cash records** | **B-2 [S-B: §36.1 G-11]** | **It is the control that defeats revenue-timing manipulation, and it is a paper control** | **Part XXII** |
| **RT-19** | **The audit/suspension route on suspected fraud** | **B-1 [S: Ch.14]**; **B-2 [S-B: §36.2 Tier 4]** | **Full controls, independent review, immediate stop-work where safe — the escalation exists and needs no new layer** | **Part XXII** |
| **RT-20** | **Leader differential must be *"transparent, capped or bounded … justified by verified leadership work rather than status alone"*** | **B-1 [S: App. A §15.3]**; **B-2 [S-C: §22.2 point 2]** | **It is the sentence that forbids the classic MLM mechanism — a multiplier on subordinate revenue — and it is already approved architecture** | **Part XI.4** |
| **RT-21** | **City originates the pipeline** — *"Identify customer needs, combine skills, propose ventures and coordinate delivery"* | **B-1 [S: Ch.7]**; **B-2 [S-B: §7.4]** | **It relieves the local unit of cold acquisition, which is the largest single economic relief available. **Retained as a design position, with its dependency stated: no City layer exists today (`SR-11`, P0 does not require one)*** | **Part IX.4, Part XXXI.1** |
| **RT-22** | **State specialist pool** | **B-1 [S: Ch.6]**; **B-2 [S-B: §19.4 B-14]** | **It is how a small team enters a high-skill category without learning on the customer's project. Same dependency as RT-21** | **Part VII, Part XXV** |
| **RT-23** | **Local autonomy over *"which legitimate business opportunities it pursues"*** | **B-1 [S: App. A §16.1]**; **B-2 [S-B: §20.1]** | **The decision-boundary matrix is the corpus's answer to chaos-versus-bureaucracy and it is already derived from reversibility and blast radius** | **Part XX.1** |
| **RT-24** | **Approval matrix by amount and risk** | **B-1 [S: App. E]**; **B-2 [S-B: §20.2]** | **It exists as a mechanism; its thresholds do not, and are not invented (EA-9)** | **Part XX.1** |
| **RT-25** | **Evidence standards for records** | **B-1 [S: App. F]**; **B-2 [S-B: §36.1 G-14]** | **Evidence fabrication is defeated by a standard, not by a suspicion** | **Part XXI, Part XXII** |
| **RT-26** | **The restricted social fund's six apparatus requirements** — *"defined sources, eligibility, approvals, disbursement evidence, reporting and audit"* | **B-1 [appendix.txt:6969–6972]** §21.1 | **Six named apparatus elements make a restricted fund auditable rather than merely well-intentioned** | **Part XIII** |
| **RT-27** | **`Reward outcomes, never counts`** | **B-1 [S: Ch.25]**, **[S: Ch.15 step 12]**; **B-2 [S-B: §35.2 principle 1]** | **It is the principle behind the anti-MLM firewall and behind G-03, G-08 and G-13 at once** | **Part XIX, Part XXVII** |
| **RT-28** | **`Do not count planned activities`** | **B-1 [S: Ch.25]**; **B-2 [S-B: §36.1 G-03]** | **It closes the cheapest gaming vector in a mission organisation: inflating unfunded activity** | **Part XIX** |
| **RT-29** | **The two-limb monetisation test** — *"Would monetising this make the organisation's help conditional on a person's ability to pay, or make a person's vulnerability valuable to the organisation?"* | **B-2 [S-B: §39.2]** | **One question decides any new proposal, permanently, at every level, with no approval route. **The second limb is the subtler and more dangerous one, and the corpus names it*** | **Part VI.4** |
| **RT-30** | **The six service/commerce separation rules** — a beneficiary is never a sales prospect; a customer is never treated as a beneficiary in the same interaction; free provision is never reduced to make paid provision more profitable; no pricing may depend on vulnerability, urgency or lack of alternatives; a paid engagement may not be presented as help; anything unclassifiable is refused pending decision | **B-2 [S-B: §38.2 B-27]** | **These are the operating rules of the wall between flow A and flow D, and rule 3 is a *reportable variance* rather than a management decision — which is what makes it enforceable** | **Part VI.3** |

## II.2 MODIFY — 20 mechanisms that are useful but were built around the old team or the old concept

**[P]** Each row states the five fields the task requires: **old design · current Sewak requirement · architectural change · reason for change · dependency.**

| ID | **Mechanism** | **Old design** | **Current Sewak requirement** | **Architectural change** | **Reason** | **Dependency** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **MD-01** | **Venture lifecycle** | **14 steps: idea → validation → approval tier → setup → customer → assignment → production → QA → delivery → acceptance → revenue → waterfall → performance → scale/improve/close** **[S: Ch.17]** | **An 11-stage lifecycle in which legal/compliance is its own stage, customer validation precedes approval, and reconciliation and review are explicit** | **The 14 steps are mapped onto 11 stages; `LEGAL/COMPLIANCE CHECK` is promoted from an attribute of the approval tier to a stage of its own; `CUSTOMER VALIDATION` is separated from `VALIDATION`; `RECONCILIATION` and `REVIEW` are separated from `WATERFALL` and `PERFORMANCE`** | **The old sequence treats compliance as a property of approval. In the current organisation compliance is the *binding* constraint — there is no entity, no classification and no counsel opinion — so it must be a gate that can stop the lifecycle on its own** | **`SR-01`, `LDR-U09`, the eleven BLK-B validations** |
| **MD-02** | **Payment cadence** | **Weekly cycle, 13 steps** **[S: Ch.19]** | **A cadence that a team below healthy break-even can actually honour** | **Cadence becomes a variable; **the priority order and the transparency obligation do not**. A change of cadence is a National approval because *"it is a member-facing promise"* **[S-B: §20.2]**** | **The corpus already derived this: *"The cadence may change; the priority order and the transparency may not"* **[S-B: §12.6 rule 3]**** | **Labour-law validation of any partial or delayed payment `[V]`** |
| **MD-03** | **Founder compensation at priority 4** | **A founder inside the organisation, compensated under an approved cap, *"not applicable at Mohalla level"* **[S-B: §8.7, §211]** | **A founder who declares he will own and control a *separate* construct (Krytos) that does not legally exist, in an organisation whose own entity is undecided** | **Priority 4 is retained as a *position in the sequence* and emptied of its *recipient*. Part XV separates seven things the old design conflated: work compensation, ownership economics, Krytos economics, Sewak Sathi economics, venture economics, organisational surplus, and IP/licensing economics** | **The old design assumed one person in one structure. FD-1 and FA-6 describe a person, a construct and an organisation, with no legal relationships among them** | **`SR-01`, GC-01 N-3, `LDR-U16` / `SR-20` *(beneficiary recusal)*, `CF-16`** |
| **MD-04** | **Organisation service fee at priority 5** | **An allocation *"to sustain organisational infrastructure after higher-priority items"*, explicitly deferrable, recipient unnamed** | **A centre that must be funded *before* teams succeed, with a named recipient and a stated instrument** | **The fee is retained as a position and re-specified as one of **four** centre-funding instruments in Part XII, with the recipient question (`H-Q17`) marked `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` and the pre-surplus gap filled by an explicit funding map rather than by hope** | **The corpus's own finding: *"the centre is funded last and spent first"* **[S-B: §8.6]**, and *"no central operating budget exists anywhere"* **[S-B:2814]**** | **`H-Q17`, `MF-1 … MF-9`, `E-03` / `B-26`** |
| **MD-05** | **Reserves at priority 6** | **Reserve and wage-protection buffer funded from what remains after priorities 1–5** | **A buffer that exists *before* the first weak month, because the first weak month arrives before the first surplus** | **Retained at priority 6 as a *flow* rule; supplemented by a **seeded** instrument that is not a waterfall allocation — a first-season float with a cap, a term, a reconciliation and one renewal maximum** | **The corpus quantified the sequencing problem: *"reserves arrive last"* **[S-B: §8.5]**, and identified the float as *"the only candidate instrument"* for the cost of existing **[S-B: §21.2]**** | **`A-N15` custody, a funder, tax treatment of a seed `[V]`** |
| **MD-06** | **Leader multiplier** | **`m_lead` — *"higher approved allocation for leadership responsibility; exact multiplier not final"*; *"FINAL MULTIPLIER REQUIRES APPROVAL"* **[appendix.txt:6354–6355]** | **A bounded differential justified by verified leadership *work*, never a share of subordinate revenue, and disclosed at recruitment as the weak-month reality** | **The variable is retained; the **form** is constrained: it may not be a percentage of team revenue (`MLM-2`), it must be capped or bounded, and it must be written down before anyone is recruited on it (`D-1`)** | **A multiplier on subordinate revenue is the classic MLM mechanism, and the corpus says so: *"the vulnerability opens the moment anyone defines the leader multiplier as a share of the team's revenue"* **[S-C: §22.2]**** | **`LDR-U02` / `SR-15`, `LDR-U18` / `SR-22`, labour law `[V]`** |
| **MD-07** | **Venture categories** | **Seven `POTENTIAL` categories with per-category controls **[S: Ch.17]** | **Fourteen candidate categories named in the task, of which some are the same category, some are sub-cases, and some are excluded at early stages** | **The seven source categories are retained as the **approval universe** — no category is added — and the fourteen candidates are mapped onto them in Part VII, with the mapping recorded so that nothing enters by renaming** | **Adding a category is an amendment; mapping a candidate to an existing category is not. The distinction is what prevents silent expansion** | **Category approval, one by one **[S: Ch.33]**; per-category `[V]`** |
| **MD-08** | **The unit of economic analysis** | **A Mohalla team of combined roles, three staffing states, nine functions** | **Eleven named roles across four tiers — Primary Role → Sub-Role/Deputy → Coordinators → Supporting Members **[appendix.txt:58, 2252]** — distributed across Mohalla, City, State and National** | **Cost and value attribution moves from *team* to **role class**: control, delivery, communication, team leadership, institutional leadership **[S-D: §3.6 Rule D-7]**. Every economic mechanism states which role class carries it** | **The old model could treat the team as one economic agent because roles were combined. The current structure separates them, and the corpus already warns that promoting a local operator into a coordination layer is *"a category error, not a stretch"* **[S-D: §3.6]**** | **`LDR-U06` `n_min`, the three staffing states **[appendix.txt:2327]**** |
| **MD-09** | **The compensation formula** | **`C_wc = Σ(verified work × approved formula)` — with the corpus's own note: *"the formula does not exist"* **[S-B: PC-01]** | **A formula *specification*: the variables, the eligibility rule, the verification route, the approval authority, the publication duty and the version field** | **Part XI specifies the formula's shape and its approval route instead of proposing values. **0 numbers. The specification is the deliverable, because a formula that does not exist cannot be understood, seen or challenged (`D-1`)*** | **The task forbids inventing numbers; the corpus forbids recruiting on a rule that does not exist. A specification satisfies both** | **`LDR-U18` / `SR-22`, National approval, labour law `[V]`** |
| **MD-10** | **The scarcity rule for a shortfall** | **`B-06` pro-rata verified-work rule, `PROPOSED`: all verified workers share the available allocation pro rata; no role-holder excludes themselves; the reduction and accrual are published in the same period; statutory dues are never reduced** | **The same rule, tested against a jurisdiction in which partial payment of a worker may itself be unlawful** | **Retained in structure, with a new first step: **if labour law makes partial payment unlawful, the correct action is not pro-rata reduction but suspension of the work**. The rule is marked `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` before it may be used** | **The corpus flagged this itself: *"**[V]** for labour-law lawfulness of pro-rata reduction and for any minimum-wage or timely-payment obligation that may make partial payment unlawful outright"* **[S-B: §12.5]**** | **Labour counsel, `LDR-U09` classification** |
| **MD-11** | **Cross-period accrual of unpaid verified work** | **`B-05`: an earned claim is recorded, aged, visible to the person, and settled at priority 1 from the next collection (`D-5`)** | **The same instrument, but an accrual is a **liability of a legal person**, and no legal person exists** | **Retained as a record and a disclosure duty from day one; **enforceability is deferred and marked `BLOCKED — SR-01`. Until an entity exists, an accrual is a moral record, not a debt, and it must be described as such to the person it belongs to*** | **Recording a promise the architecture cannot keep is the failure mode `D-2` exists to prevent | **`SR-01`, accounting policy `[V]`** |
| **MD-12** | **Cross-subsidy between teams** | **Four models evaluated; only Layer 1 *(non-cash)* exists in source; money cannot move laterally **[S-B: §21.4, §22]** | **The same conclusion, with the current structure's four tiers named as the routes** | **Retained: **demand referral and specialist time may move; money may not**. Part XII records the three permitted non-cash transfers and the one prohibited cash transfer** | **A lateral cash transfer requires a treasury, a policy and a legal person, and creates the subsidy-capture risk `G-15`** | **`A-N15`, National approval, `[V]`** |
| **MD-13** | **Social-service funding from surplus** | **Priority 7, *"only when surplus conditions are met"*, into a restricted fund** | **A safeguarding service that *"must never stop"* **[S-B: §33.3]** and cannot wait for a surplus that may never arrive** | **Retained as the *surplus* route; supplemented by a **named pre-surplus instrument** with an emergency exception, and by the rule that service hours are budgeted (`B-01`) and their breach is a reportable variance (`B-27` rule 3)** | **The corpus isolated this as *"the sharpest gap"*: service is the first thing cut, and the fund barred from salaries cannot pay the people who deliver it** | **A funder, `MF-1 … MF-9`, `[V]` on any donation regime** |
| **MD-14** | **Performance measurement of economic activity** | **Eight weighted dimensions — mission impact carrying the highest weight, then revenue and execution, then trust, and others **[S: Ch.25]**; the weights themselves are `PROPOSED`, `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` (`LDR-U03` / `SR-16`), and **their values are not restated here — EA-10**** | **Economic measurement that cannot be gamed and cannot become a leaderboard at a stage where no cohort exists** | **The **numeric weights are rejected** (J-14). The *dimensions* are retained as **criteria**, and four of them are converted from weights into **veto gates**: mission balance, safeguarding, financial integrity and truthfulness** | **A weight invites trade; a veto forbids it **[S-B: §35.2 principle 4]**. And comparative scoring requires cohorts a single team does not have **[S-B: §26.2]**** | **`LDR-U03` / `SR-16`, `LDR-U11` public-or-not** |
| **MD-15** | **AI in the economic system** | **An *AI & Business Manager* who coordinates *"legitimate business activity and responsible AI-supported operations"*, manages AI tool use, and may not *"let AI make consequential decisions"* **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]** | **A twelve-function permission matrix, already approved, covering score computation, eligibility, readiness, promotion, succession, complaints, anomaly detection, workload, training, records, safeguarding and payment **[S-D: §30.2]** | **The role's own cannot-do list is retained and **bound to the twelve-function matrix**, extended with seven economic functions the matrix does not yet cover: pricing, contract formation, credit and collection, expense classification, reserve draw, related-party detection and venture closure** | **The old design states a prohibition; the current architecture has a permission matrix. Binding them converts a rule into an operable control** | **`A-N21` AI review capacity, `A-V` data-protection advice `[V]`** |
| **MD-16** | **Two-track venture strategy** | **`B-14`: a cash track in a low-startup, easy-close category, and a capability track in a higher-contribution category entered through the City pipeline with a State specialist attached** | **The same strategy, in an organisation with no City layer and no State pool** | **Retained as the design; **Track 2 is marked `BLOCKED — no City pipeline, no State specialist pool`**, and a single-track fallback is specified for Stage 0–1 so that the strategy does not silently become a two-track promise** | **A strategy whose second track depends on layers that do not exist is a plan for a different organisation** | **RT-21, RT-22 dependencies; `SR-11`** |
| **MD-17** | **Minimum viable control** | **`MVC-1 … MVC-11`, of which MVC-1 is *"two-signature money handling — receive / approve / record / audit in four distinct hands, one outside the team if necessary"*** | **The same control set, in an organisation with no bank account and no mandate (`A-N16 BLOCKED`)** | **MVC-2 … MVC-11 retained unchanged. **MVC-1 is split: the *separation of hands* is retained and is enforceable on paper today; the *bank signature* limb is marked `BLOCKED — A-N16` and cannot be satisfied until an entity and an account exist*** | **Separation of duties is a governance act; a bank mandate is a legal act. Only the second is blocked** | **`A-N16`, `SR-03`** |
| **MD-18** | **Governance-cost model** | **`H_gov` computed from a workload calculator, a fixed/variable split, a 22-SOP and 14-record load, scenario bands, and hours by role; a City divisor `K`; a minimum viable `K`** | **The same model, with every value `NOT ESTABLISHED` — `σ_max`, `K_min`, `c`, and the divisor's shape are all unstated (`LDR-U13 … LDR-U15`)** | **Retained as the **shape** of the model, with all five values as variables to be measured. **0 bands invented.** Part XII records that governance cost is an L1 existence cost and is funded by nothing in the waterfall** | **The corpus's own rule: numeric values are *"not required"* at this stage — *"the pilot exists to produce them"* **[S-F: Part VIII.2]**** | **`LDR-U13 … LDR-U15`, measurement plan `SR-62`, `SR-63`** |
| **MD-19** | **The unit-economics dashboard** | **Five panels — financial, operational, human, business, risk — with concentration fields and detection latencies** | **The same panels, with no software, no leaderboard and no composite score** | **Retained as a **record specification on paper**. The composite score is rejected (J-18); the panels become the fields of the seventeen record types in Part XXI** | **`SR-77` prohibits procuring technology; a dashboard that requires software is not a control, it is a wish** | **`SR-62`, `SR-63`, P0-I** |
| **MD-20** | **Economic maturity stages** | **Seven stages 0 … 6 with minimum evidence, and four discipline rules — a stage is evidenced not declared, stages can be lost, stage does not equal size, and stage 6 requires proof in a different neighbourhood** | **The same stages, re-anchored to the *current* gate set rather than to the old pilot's** | **Retained in full; **entry evidence is re-mapped to `GATE P0-A … P0-J` and the 22 Tier-0 items**, so that a stage claim cannot outrun a gate** | **A maturity model untethered from the gate set is how an organisation talks itself into being further along than it is** | **`SR-91`, `SR-92`, P0 pass conditions** |

## II.3 REJECT — 22 items that must not enter the current architecture

| ID | **Item** | **Ground for rejection** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **J-01** | **The illustrative allocation percentages preserved at **[appendix.txt:6364–6369]** — a worker-compensation share, a direct-delivery-cost share, an organisation-service-fee share and "other allocations". **Their values are deliberately not restated in this instrument: §XXVIII.3 classifies them without reproducing them, because reproduction invites adoption**** | **Rejected twice over. **First: the corpus never adopted them — *"This specification does not adopt those as final Sewak Sathi Sanstha percentages"* **[appendix.txt:6367–6369]**. Second: FD-1 makes them historical, belonging to *"a different team and an earlier economic/business concept"*. **They remain `ILLUSTRATIVE — SOURCE-QUOTED — NOT ADOPTED — HISTORICAL` and are not used in any calculation in this instrument*** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-02** | **The name and the claim *"Zero-Capital"*** | **Rejected as a label and re-tested as a proposition. Part XVII separates `ZERO CASH INVESTMENT` from `ZERO ECONOMIC COST` and finds fourteen hidden capital requirements. **The name is not carried forward, because a name that asserts a conclusion prevents the test from being run honestly*** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-03** | **The old team's staffing assumptions** | **Rejected by instruction and by structure. The current organisation has eleven named roles across four tiers **[appendix.txt:1808–2230]**; the old model assumed a small combined-role team. **MD-08 replaces team-level attribution with role-class attribution*** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-04** | **The old pilot budget and pilot assumptions** | **Rejected. No budget exists, none is invented (EA-9), and the pilot is not authorised (EA-28). **Every economic variable remains symbolic until measured **[S-F:579]**** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-05** | **The old growth assumptions** | **Rejected. Scaling is conditional on six source conditions (RT-14) and on stage evidence (MD-20); no growth rate is stated anywhere in this instrument** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-06** | **The old organisational relationships** | **Rejected. The current relationship set is `R1 … R8` with 0 models selected, GC-01 `UNRESOLVED`, and Krytos declared a *"Virtual Brain"* and *"ecosystem head"* with **0 corpus occurrences** of either term. **No relationship from the historical document is imported*** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-07** | **The old founder economics** | **Rejected and replaced. MD-03 empties priority 4 of its recipient; Part XV separates seven conflated things. **`CF-16` stands: leader equity is prohibited **[S-D:2999]**** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-08** | **The old venture structure as a default** | **Rejected as a default, retained as an approval universe. The seven categories are `POTENTIAL` until approved one by one **[S: Ch.33]**, and MD-07 maps the task's fourteen candidates onto them without adding any** | **`MODIFIED` in part — see MD-07; the *default* is `REJECTED`** |
| **J-09** | **Any leader differential expressed as a percentage of team revenue** | **Rejected absolutely. `MLM-2`: the differential *"may not be a percentage of the team's revenue"* **[S-C: §22.3]**, and **[S: App. A §15.3]** requires it to be justified by verified leadership *work*** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-10** | **Recruitment-linked income in any form** — commission, referral fee, bounty, in cash or in kind, at any level | **Rejected absolutely. `MLM-4`, `MLM-6`: *"Attribution of an introduction may be recorded for thanks, never for value"* **[S-C: §22.3]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-11** | **Membership, joining, kit, training or materials fees, and any mandatory subscription** | **Rejected absolutely. `MLM-3`, `MLM-5`: *"Free to join, free to participate, free to leave"*; membership is verification-based, not fee-based **[S: Ch.12]**; **[S-B: §39.3]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-12** | **Compensation calculated from any count of people** | **Rejected absolutely. `MLM-1` — recruited, active, paused, in a team, in a downline, or in any grouping** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-13** | **Selling volunteer labour to a third party** | **Rejected. `NM-10`: *"Supplying members' unpaid time to a third party for money converts volunteering into labour brokerage"* **[S-B: §39.1]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-14** | **The numeric performance weights as economic policy** | **Rejected as numbers; retained as criteria (MD-14). The weights are `PROPOSED` and their authority is `UNRESOLVED` (`LDR-U03` / `SR-16` — *"the CEO cannot appoint the CEO"* applies to who may set them)** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT` as numbers** |
| **J-15** | **Monetising any of `NM-1 … NM-12`** — access to help, emergency assistance, safeguarding, sensitive personal information, incident and case records, beneficiary testimony and images, verification or endorsement, influence over decisions, political or religious access, volunteer labour, behavioural or credit inference, access to audit or grievance routes | **Rejected permanently, at every level, with no approval route. **The second limb of the test is the dangerous one: a category can be monetised without charging the vulnerable person — by selling data *about* them, images *of* them, or access *to* them **[S-B: §39.2]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-16** | **Paid priority or expedited service** | **Rejected. **Directly violates limb one of the monetisation test and `NM-8` **[S-B: §39.3]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-17** | **Selling aggregate anonymised insight at Stage 0–3** | **Rejected at this stage: *"re-identification risk in a small locality is high"*; revisitable only with a DPIA, professional advice and a Tier-1 amendment **[S-B: §39.3]**** | **`REJECTED — REVISITABLE ONLY BY TIER-1 AMENDMENT`** |
| **J-18** | **A composite unit-health score** | **Rejected. The corpus investigated rather than assumed and recommended *"a dashboard with veto gates, not a score"* **[S-B: §28.2]**; a composite creates gaming **[S-B: §28.3]**** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-19** | **Model B — local self-sustainability — as the operating model** | **Rejected. *"Contradicted by §32.3"*: a standing-start team cannot reach healthy break-even in the target months; and it is *"destructive for mission"* — service is the first thing cut **[S-B: §37.1–37.3]**** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-20** | **Model A — central funding — as the operating model** | **Rejected as unavailable: *"the source names no central funder, bars the social-impact fund from operating cost, and leaves donations conditional and unvalidated"* **[S-B: §37.3]**. Adopting it would assume a funder into existence** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |
| **J-21** | **Any assumption that AI may set wages, approve ventures, move money, or make a consequential decision** | **Rejected. **[S: Const. Art.12]** verbatim: *"AI may recommend; humans decide. AI cannot autonomously make consequential HR, wage, legal, safeguarding, safety, major fund or leadership decisions"* — `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`, not a proposal **[S-D:1161, 4529 `LDR-C05`]**. The role specification says the same in its own words: the AI & Business Manager *"cannot … set wages alone … or let AI make consequential decisions"* **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]**** | **`REJECTED — PERMANENT, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **J-22** | **`Revenue > 0` as a health criterion, and the historical blueprint's own authority status** | **Rejected. *"Why 'revenue > zero' is not health"* **[S-B: §2.2]**: six solvency lines exist and `V_gross > 0` does not reach the first **[S-B: §8.3]**. **And the historical document is not an approved instrument of the current organisation — FD-1 makes it reference material, and *"a proposal that has not been approved has no operative force"* (Rule F-9)*** | **`REJECTED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** |

## II.4 The decomposition counted

| Field | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Items decomposed** | **72** |
| **`RETAIN`** | **30 — RT-01 … RT-30** |
| **`MODIFY`** | **20 — MD-01 … MD-20, each with all five required fields** |
| **`REJECT`** | **22 — J-01 … J-22, each with its ground** |
| **Reconciliation** | **30 + 20 + 22 = 72 ✓** |
| **Items rejected as permanent, with no approval route** | **8 — J-09, J-10, J-11, J-12, J-13, J-15, J-16, J-21** |
| **Items retained automatically because they appear in the historical document** | **0 — every retention names a reason that survives the retention test** |
| **Items read from the document itself** | **0 — the document is not present (EA-3). Every item names its actual basis: B-1, B-2, B-3 or B-4** |
| **Numbers adopted from the historical document** | **0** |
| **Numbers adopted from anywhere** | **0** |
| **Items whose retention depends on a layer that does not exist** | **4 — RT-21, RT-22, MD-16, and MD-18's City divisor. Each is retained with its dependency stated, not silently assumed** |

**[A] Finding CEA-F2.** **The decomposition's most important result is that almost nothing in it is actually the historical blueprint's property.** Of 30 retained mechanisms, **28 were read from `B-1` — the v1.1 source text — or from `B-2`, v1.3-B's derivations of it.** The waterfall, the definitions, the bad-month rule, the four-hand segregation, the Funds Manager's limits, the approval tiers, the scaling and closure conditions, the payment sheet and the anti-gaming controls are all **current corpus**, not historical import. **Only two items — the illustrative percentages and the "Zero-Capital" claim — are genuinely attributable to the historical document at one remove, and both are rejected.** The founder's correction is therefore architecturally cheaper than it looks: discarding the blueprint discards almost nothing, because the economics were never in it. **They were in v1.1 all along, and v1.1 is the governing corpus.**

# PART III — ECONOMIC DOCTRINE

**[P]** Bound by **EA-1, EA-2, EA-9, EA-22**. **Ten doctrine statements. Each is derived from the corpus, labelled, and stated so that a later decision can be tested against it rather than against a mood.**

| # | **Doctrine** | **Derivation** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **DO-1** | **The organisation's economic activity exists to fund its mission, not to be its mission.** Revenue is an instrument. Where instrument and purpose conflict, the conflict is resolved by a veto, not by a weight | **[S-B: §35.2 principle 4]**; **[S-B: §28.2]**; mission impact carries the highest performance weight in the source **[S: Ch.25]** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`**, derived from approved material |
| **DO-2** | **Money enters this architecture in exactly one way: a collected customer receipt for real value delivered.** Every rupee distributed must trace to one, or to an approved and published funding source | **[S-C: §22.3 MLM-7]**; **[S-B: §36.1 G-01]** — *"collected legitimate customer revenue"* **[S: Ch.25]** | **`RETAINED`** — RT-17, RT-03 |
| **DO-3** | **Help is free. Commerce is paid. The two never meet in one interaction, one record, one price or one sentence.** A beneficiary is never a prospect; a customer is never treated as a beneficiary | **[S-B: §38.1]** six categories; **[S-B: §38.2 B-27]** six rules; **[S-B: §39.2]** the two-limb test | **`RETAINED`** — RT-29, RT-30 |
| **DO-4** | **Nobody earns because somebody joined.** No compensation, bonus, incentive, allocation or benefit may be calculated from any count of people, and no differential may be a share of a team's revenue | **[S-C: §22.3 MLM-1, MLM-2, MLM-4, MLM-6]**; **[S: App. A §15.3]** | **`RETAINED`** — J-09 … J-12 are the permanent rejections that enforce it |
| **DO-5** | **Verified work is paid before the organisation is funded, and before the founder is paid.** The sequencing is the control; a percentage table applied blindly to gross revenue can break the system because it may pay discretionary allocations before wages, taxes or direct costs | **[appendix.txt:6296]**; **[appendix.txt:6307–6321]**; **[appendix.txt:6370–6376]** §14.5 | **`RETAINED`** — RT-01, RT-02, RT-07 |
| **DO-6** | **No one person may control money.** Receive, approve, record and review sit in four hands; temporary combination is permitted only with lower limits, dual acknowledgement, upward review, receipts and bank proof, random audit, and prompt role split as volume grows | **[appendix.txt:6925–6948]** Diagram 20A; **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]** §20.4 | **`RETAINED`** — RT-11, RT-12 |
| **DO-7** | **AI recommends; humans decide; and the human is named in the record.** No consequential economic decision — wage, contract, price, credit, reserve draw, venture approval, closure, related-party treatment — is made by a system | **[S: Const. Art.12]** verbatim; **[S-D: §30.2]**; **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]** | **`RETAINED`** — MD-15, J-21 |
| **DO-8** | **A cost that is not named will be paid by the poorest participant, the least protected volunteer, or the mission.** Hidden cost is not neutral; it is a transfer | **[S-B: §5.2]** hidden cost register; **[S-B: §15.1]** *"volunteer labour is not free"*; **[S-B: §6.3]** the invisible cross-subsidy | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`**, derived |
| **DO-9** | **Zero cash investment is not zero economic cost.** Every requirement that consumes equipment, connectivity, transport, workspace, insurance, professional time, management hours, working capital or risk capacity is a cost, and it is borne by somebody | Task §14; **[S-B: §5.2]**, **§15.2**, **§18.2** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |
| **DO-10** | **Nothing in this architecture is deployable until the organisation is a legal person with an account, a classified workforce and a constituted authority.** An economic design is not an economic permission | **EA-18, EA-19, EA-20, EA-28**; `SR-01` rank 1 **[S-F:604]**; `A-N16 BLOCKED`; `GATE P0 = 0 of 10` | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |

> ### **THE DOCTRINE IN ONE SENTENCE**
>
> **Make money only from real customer value, pay the people who made it first, keep help free, keep recruitment worthless, keep the four hands separate, keep the founder's interest visible and bounded, keep every rupee traceable — and do none of it until the organisation legally exists.**

---

# PART IV — ECONOMIC OBJECTIVES

**[P]** Bound by **EA-8, EA-9**. **Objectives are ordered, because an unordered objective list cannot resolve a conflict. Eight objectives, four anti-objectives.**

## IV.1 The eight objectives, in order

| # | **Objective** | **What satisfies it** | **What would betray it** | **Measure *(variable, never a number)*** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **OB-1** | **Lawfulness** — every economic act is inside a validated legal boundary | A written counsel opinion per category, held as governance evidence **[S-F:1975 SR-98]**; classification of every worker; a validated entity | Acting first and validating later; *"we have always done it this way"* | `N_opinions_held`, `N_acts_outside_boundary` — **the second must be 0** |
| **OB-2** | **Worker protection** — verified work is paid, in full, on time, and its shortfall is recorded and settled | Priority 1 honoured; the accrual aged, visible and settled at priority 1 (`D-5`); the weak-month reality disclosed at recruitment (`D-2`) | Promising the good month; erasing an earned claim in a bad one; paying the loudest first | `A_1 / R_1` per period; accrual age; disclosure-at-recruitment compliance |
| **OB-3** | **Mission integrity** — service is delivered, budgeted and never reduced to improve a financial result | Service hours budgeted (`B-01`) and their breach a reportable variance (`B-27` rule 3); the four veto gates held | Cutting service in a weak month; measuring only what photographs well (`I-03`) | `H_svc` against budget; veto-gate breaches — **must be 0** |
| **OB-4** | **Financial integrity** — every rupee is traceable, reconciled and attributable to an authority | Eight-field records; four-hand segregation; reconciliation to bank/cash; the payment sheet with its formula version | Self-approval; a payment that cannot be shown correct; a receipt without a link | `N_records_missing_a_field`; `N_self_approvals` — **both must be 0** |
| **OB-5** | **Organisational continuity** — the centre and the control system are funded, not left to surplus | An explicit funding map with **no blank cell** (`B-26`); the existence cost `C_L1` named and sourced | Funding governance from whatever is left; discovering the cost of existing after it is owed | `C_L1` coverage by named source; blank cells in the funding map — **must be 0** |
| **OB-6** | **Resilience** — a shock does not become a collapse | A buffer before the first weak month; the six solvency lines known and reported; correlated risk counted once, not per event | Reserves at priority 6 only; treating twelve risks as independent | Solvency line reached per period; `M_runway`; correlation count |
| **OB-7** | **Anti-exploitation** — no person's vulnerability is an asset, and no member's advancement depends on paying or recruiting | `NM-1 … NM-12` held as vetoes; `MLM-1 … MLM-7`; the T1 correlation report at zero | Monetising data, testimony, access or endorsement; a fee at any level; a differential on team revenue | T1 income-versus-recruitment correlation — **must be zero**; `N_fees_collected_from_participants` — **must be 0** |
| **OB-8** | **Decidability** — every economic question has a named decider, a named validator and a recorded reason | The decision-boundary matrix populated; a reason recorded for every refusal and every approval (`MVC-11`) | An approval without a clock; a refusal without an alternative; a decision with no name on it | `N_decisions_without_a_named_decider`; `N_refusals_without_a_reason` — **both must be 0** |

## IV.2 The four anti-objectives

**[P]** Stated because an architecture that does not name what it refuses will be asked for it later.

| # | **Anti-objective** | **Why it is refused** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AO-1** | **Revenue maximisation** | It is the objective that turns a mission organisation into a sales organisation, and the corpus already predicts the distortion: `I-01` revenue over service, `I-02` easy projects over difficult needs, `I-05` acquisition over delivery, `I-07` short-term revenue over long-term trust | **[S-B: §35.1]** |
| **AO-2** | **Growth in headcount** | Headcount is the cheapest thing to produce and the easiest to mistake for capacity; counting it creates `G-13` volunteer-count inflation and breaches `MLM-1` | **[S-B: §36.1 G-13]**; **[S: Ch.25]** *"verified members/approved participants only"* |
| **AO-3** | **Self-sufficiency of the local unit** | Model B is contradicted by the corpus's own arithmetic: a standing-start team cannot reach healthy break-even in the target months, and a poor neighbourhood is penalised for being poor | **[S-B: §37.2, §32.3]**; **J-19** |
| **AO-4** | **A composite score of organisational health** | It creates a single number to game, invites trade against an irreversible harm, and requires a cohort that a single team does not have | **[S-B: §28.2, §28.3]**; **J-18** |

---

# PART V — ECONOMIC ACTORS

## V.1 The structure the economy must work inside

**[P]** Bound by **EA-6, EA-9, MD-08**. **Read directly from the v1.1 source text this session.**

| Element | **The corpus's own words** | **Citation** |
|---|---|---|
| **The universal ladder** | *"a complete hierarchical Primary Role → Sub-Role / Deputy → Coordinators → Supporting Members system, a formal vacancy architecture, internal role applications, role eligibility engine, temporary delegation rules and software/RBAC requirements"* | **[appendix.txt:58]**; §8A.2 at **[appendix.txt:2252]** |
| **The eleven primary roles** | Team Leader · HR & Verification Manager · Ground-Level Manager · Volunteer Manager · Planner · Social Media Manager · Editor · Funds Manager · Warehouse Manager · AI & Business Manager · Safety / Protection Lead | **[appendix.txt:1808, 1843, 1890, 1922, 1966, 1996, 2039, 2069, 2112, 2142, 2188]** — each an `8.x Role Specification` in the source |
| **Three staffing states** | *"A – Starter Team (roles may combine; safety/HR/finance/evidence duties never skipped) · B – Growing Team (need-based) · C – Autonomous team"* | **[appendix.txt:2327]** §8A.3 |
| **Role classes** | **Control** *(HR & Verification Manager, Funds Manager, Safety/Protection Lead, Auditor)* · **Delivery** *(Ground-Level Manager, Planner, Warehouse Manager, AI & Business Manager)* · **Communication** *(Social Media Manager, Editor)* · **Team leadership** *(Mohalla/City/State/National Team Leader; Sub-Leader)* · **Institutional leadership** | **[S-D: §3.6, Rule D-7]** |
| **Levels** | Mohalla → City → State → National, with L0 … L8 progression and cross-level application | **[appendix.txt:2252–2256]** §8A.2, §8A.13; **[S: Ch.4–8, Ch.26]** |
| **The nine functions a State-A team must cover** | Nine functions identified with named holders, and **four finance hands distinct** | **[S-B: §30.1 Stage 0]**; **[S: Ch.10 State A]** |

## V.2 Which roles create economic value, and which do not

**[P]** The task requires four determinations per role. **The corpus's own compensation categories `PC-01 … PC-08` **[S-B:343–350]** supply the test: does the role's work fall inside *"verified venture work"*, which is the only thing priority 1 funds?**

| Role | **Class** | **Creates economic value?** | **Supports economic delivery?** | **Mission-only?** | **May participate in paid ventures?** | **Funded by** | **Citation** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **AI & Business Manager** | Delivery | **YES — primarily.** *"Identify opportunities, prepare venture proposals, validate demand, coordinate customer work, assign business tasks, ensure quality, record revenue, manage AI tool use and report venture performance"* | **YES** | No | **YES — the role whose work is the venture** | Priority 1 for venture delivery work; **priority 3 is not available for the role's own coordination time, which is the gap `PC-07` names** | **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]** |
| **Ground-Level Manager** | Delivery | **YES — where the role delivers** | **YES** | Partly | **YES, inside an approved venture** | Priority 1 for verified delivery work | **[appendix.txt:1890]** |
| **Planner** | Delivery | **Partly — planning is inside `h_proj` where attributable to a project** | **YES** | Partly | **YES, where the plan is a billable deliverable** | Priority 3 where directly attributable; otherwise `PC-07` unfunded | **[appendix.txt:1966]** |
| **Warehouse Manager** | Delivery | **Only in category 7 *(physical products)*, which carries cash, stock, licensing and the highest risk** | **YES** | Partly | **YES, in a category-7 venture only** | Priority 3 where attributable to delivery | **[appendix.txt:2112]**; **[S-B: §7.1 cat. 7]** |
| **Editor** | Communication | **YES — category 4 *(design/editing)* is a source category with very low cash startup and an existing role family** | **YES** | Partly | **YES — the shortest path to a first receipt, with the lowest contribution per hour** | Priority 1 for verified venture work | **[appendix.txt:2039]**; **[S-B: §7.1 cat. 4]** |
| **Social Media Manager** | Communication | **NO — and this is a finding, not a judgement.** *"Why social media does not produce customers"*: it produces attention, and attention is not a receipt | **YES — indirectly, and its cost is real** | Partly | **Not as a revenue generator; may support acquisition whose cost is `C_acq`** | **`PC-07` — unfunded by the waterfall** | **[S-B: §18.4]**; **[appendix.txt:1996]** |
| **Funds Manager** | Control | **NO** | **YES — indispensably** | No | **NO — participation would breach segregation: the role prepares the payment sheet and may not self-approve** | **`PC-07` — unfunded. And `D-8`: there is a stated point at which this role ceases to be volunteerable, because it carries legal exposure** | **[appendix.txt:2069, 6950–6958]**; **[S-B: §34.2 D-8]** |
| **HR & Verification Manager** | Control | **NO** | **YES — verification is the precondition of `verified work`, so the role makes priority 1 computable** | No | **NO — a verifier who is paid from the venture they verify cannot verify it** | **`PC-07` — unfunded** | **[appendix.txt:1843]**; **[S-B: §36.1 G-07]** |
| **Safety / Protection Lead** | Control | **NO — and it must never be** | **YES — as a gate, never as a revenue function** | **YES** | **NO. `NM-3`: safeguarding, protection and incident response are never monetised; the protection function may not be used for collection, enforcement or presence** | **`PC-07` — unfunded; `D-8` applies** | **[appendix.txt:2188]**; **[S-B: §39.1 NM-3]**; **[S-B: §19.5 point 4]** |
| **Volunteer Manager** | Control-adjacent | **NO** | **YES** | **YES** | **NO — `NM-10`: supplying members' unpaid time to a third party for money is labour brokerage** | **`PC-07` — unfunded** | **[appendix.txt:1922]**; **[S-B: §39.1 NM-10]** |
| **Team Leader** | Team leadership | **Not directly — the role's economic function is authority, coverage and the bounded differential** | **YES** | Partly | **YES where the leader delivers, but the differential itself is not venture revenue** | Priority 1 for verified work; the leader allocation at priority 4 under a bounded, work-justified differential (`MD-06`) | **[appendix.txt:1808]**; **[S: App. A §15.3]** |
| **Auditor** *(named in the role class, not among the eleven)* | Control | **NO** | **YES — as the fourth hand** | No | **NO — the fourth hand may not be inside the transaction** | **`PC-07` — unfunded; `D-8` applies** | **[S-D: §3.6]**; **[appendix.txt:6925–6948]** |

| Field | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Roles that create economic value** | **5 — AI & Business Manager, Ground-Level Manager, Planner *(partly)*, Warehouse Manager *(category 7 only)*, Editor** |
| **Roles that support economic delivery but create no revenue** | **7 — Social Media Manager, Funds Manager, HR & Verification Manager, Safety/Protection Lead, Volunteer Manager, Team Leader, Auditor** |
| **Roles that are mission-only and must never be revenue-linked** | **3 — Safety/Protection Lead, Volunteer Manager, Auditor** |
| **Roles that may not participate in paid ventures at all** | **4 — Funds Manager *(segregation)*, HR & Verification Manager *(verification independence)*, Safety/Protection Lead *(`NM-3`)*, Volunteer Manager *(`NM-10`)*** |
| **Roles whose work is funded by nothing in the waterfall** | **7 — all seven fall under `PC-07`, for which the corpus records *"No source line exists"*. This is the architecture's central economic gap and Part XII exists to close it** |
| **Roles that carry legal exposure and therefore cease to be volunteerable at a stated point** | **4 — Funds Manager, HR & Verification Manager, Safety/Protection Lead, Auditor (`D-8`)** |

## V.3 How mixed mission and business work is recorded

**[P]** Bound by **EA-12, EA-27**. **The corpus has no split rule: mixed activity is *"PARTLY [funded], and ambiguously"* **[S-B: §8.4 category C]**. A rule is therefore specified — not invented as a number, but designed as a record.**

| Rule | **Content** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|
| **MW-1** | **Every hour worked by every person is attributed to exactly one of five activity categories in the period it is worked: `A Pure Service` · `B Revenue-Producing` · `C Mixed` · `D Infrastructure` · `E Capacity Building`** — the corpus's own five **[S-B: §6.1, §8.4]** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`**, using source categories |
| **MW-2** | **A `C Mixed` hour must be split by a stated, published basis before it is recorded — and the basis is a governance decision, not a team choice.** Candidate bases: time recorded contemporaneously · deliverable attribution · a published fixed split per activity type. **No basis is selected here: `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **MW-3** | **The split determines funding, and funding determines behaviour — so the split is auditable.** Whichever basis is chosen, the record carries it, the period carries it, and any change of basis is recorded with a reason and an effective date | **`NEW`** |
| **MW-4** | **A split may never move an hour from `A` or `E` into `B` in order to make it payable.** That is `G-05` expense-shifting in its most attractive form, because the boundary between funded and unfunded work is exactly where the incentive sits **[S-B: §36.1 G-05]** | **`NEW`**, enforcing a source control |
| **MW-5** | **Unpaid hours are recorded too.** *"They are volunteers" must never be a reason to ignore cost* (`D-6`); the organisation measures what volunteering costs it and reports it | **`RETAINED`** — **[S-B: §15.2, §34.2 D-6]** |
| **MW-6** | **No person's record may be edited by the person who benefits from the edit.** The four-hand rule applies to hours as it applies to money | **`RETAINED`** — RT-11 |

## V.4 How shared organisational labour is allocated

**[P]** Seven roles do work that is real, necessary, and funded by nothing (`PC-07`). **Allocation is a design problem with four candidate answers, and the corpus has already rejected two of them.**

| Candidate | **Content** | **Assessment** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AL-1 — Absorb it silently** | Control and communication roles work unpaid and the cost is invisible | **`REJECTED`.** It is what happens today by structural absence, and the corpus names the consequence: debt-based loyalty, `HC-18` control roles left to absorb social friction alone, and `D-8` roles carrying legal exposure without support | **`REJECTED`** |
| **AL-2 — Charge it to ventures as a direct cost** | Allocate governance hours to each venture at priority 3 | **`REJECTED` as a general rule, `PERMITTED` where genuinely attributable.** A governance hour that exists whether or not the venture runs is an L1 existence cost, not an L3 activity cost **[S-B: §4.4]**; charging it to a venture is `G-05` in reverse and would make the safest ventures look the most expensive | **`REJECTED` with a narrow exception** |
| **AL-3 — Fund it at priority 5** | The organisation service fee is *intended* for infrastructure | **`INSUFFICIENT ALONE`.** Priority 5 is explicitly deferrable, arrives after priorities 1–4, and is the first thing to fall in a bad month **[appendix.txt:6370–6376]**. It cannot fund a cost that exists at a collection of zero | **`RETAINED — IN PART`** |
| **AL-4 — Name a source for every layer, at every stage, with no blank cell** | The explicit hybrid funding map: for each cost layer, which source funds it at each maturity stage — venture revenue, float, seeded reserve, validated donation, City or State resourcing | **`ADOPTED`.** It is the corpus's own proposal (`B-26`) and the only candidate that does not assume a funder into existence or hide a cost. **Part XII builds it** | **`RETAINED` — MD-04, RT-30's companion** |

## V.5 How conflicts of interest are controlled

| # | **Conflict** | **The control** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **COI-1** | **A role-holder approves their own expense** | **Prohibited absolutely: the Funds Manager *"cannot unilaterally approve their own expenses"*** | **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]** §20.4 |
| **COI-2** | **A verifier is paid from the work they verify** | **HR & Verification may not participate in the venture whose work they verify** | **V.2; `G-07` ghost workers **[S-B: §36.1]**** |
| **COI-3** | **A leader sets their own differential** | **Prohibited: no role-holder may change *"compensation percentages, priority order or leader multiplier"*; and under the scarcity rule *"no role-holder may exclude themselves from the pro-rata reduction and still approve it"*** | **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]**; **[S-B: §12.5 B-06 point 2]** |
| **COI-4** | **A founder benefits from a transaction they authorise** | **`LDR-U16` / `SR-20` is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)` — the person whose compensation is in question is the person who would record the position. **Not resolved here: `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`, and Part XVI specifies the recusal mechanism without naming the recused person's substitute, because the corpus does not*** | **[S-F:1897]** |
| **COI-5** | **A related party appears on both sides of a transaction** | **Related-party exclusion already exists as an anti-gaming control; Part XVI makes it a record type with a disclosure duty and a separate approver** | **[S: Ch.25]**; **[S-B: §36.1 G-01, G-04]** |
| **COI-6** | **A decision-maker holds an interest they have not declared** | **Every consequential decision records conflicts declared, per the approval matrix** | **[S-D: LG-10]**; **[S: App. E]** |
| **COI-7** | **An approver is also the beneficiary of the approval's downstream effect** | **The corpus's own formulation: *"the CEO cannot appoint the CEO"*. Applied to economics: no person may approve a rule whose principal beneficiary is themselves** | **[S-F: C-D-17]** |
| **COI-8** | **A team optimises for access to a subsidy rather than for viability** | **Float and grant capture: caps, hard terms, reconciliation, publication, one renewal maximum** | **[S-B: §36.1 G-15, G-16]**; **[S-B: §37.3 caveat]** |

## V.6 How people are compensated without creating a recruitment-income system

**[P]** Bound by **EA-13**. **The answer is structural, not disciplinary: the corpus already made recruitment income architecturally impossible, and the current architecture's job is not to weaken it.**

| # | **The structural reason** | **Citation** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Compensation attaches to verified work, not to people.** There is no head, no downline and no count in the payment chain | **[S: App. A §14.3]**; **[S: Ch.19 step 1]**; **[S-C: §22.2 point 1]** |
| **2** | **The leader differential is bounded and work-justified, and may not be a percentage of team revenue** | **[S: App. A §15.3]**; **[S-C: §22.3 MLM-2]** |
| **3** | **The performance system already forbids counting people** — verified members and approved participants only, attendance cross-checked, healthy active retention rather than inactive names | **[S: Ch.25]**; **[S-C: §22.2 point 3]** |
| **4** | **Membership has no fee, and one is prohibited permanently** | **[S: Ch.12]**; **[S-C: §22.3 MLM-3]**; **[S-B: §39.3]** |
| **5** | **The vulnerability is interpretive, not structural** — *"it opens the moment anyone defines the leader multiplier as a share of the team's revenue"* — so the control is a definition, and the definition is in Part XI | **[S-C: §22.2 verdict]** |
| **6** | **One report is the entire assurance programme:** is any person's income correlated with the number of people they brought in? **The correlation must be zero, and it is computable from records the architecture already keeps** | **[S-C: §22.4 T1]** |
