# PART VI — ECONOMIC BOUNDARIES AND THE FOUR MONEY FLOWS

## VI.1 The four flows, separated

**[P]** Bound by **EA-12, EA-18, EA-27**. **The task requires that money not be collapsed into one pool. Four flows are defined, each with its own source, purpose, governance, record and prohibition.**

| Flow | **Name** | **What enters it** | **Its purpose** | **Who governs it** | **Its record** | **What may never enter or leave it** | **State today** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **CUSTOMER / COMMERCIAL REVENUE** | **Collected customer receipts for real value delivered under an approved venture** | **To fund the work that produced it, in the priority order of Part X** | Approval matrix by amount and risk **[S: App. E]**; the venture's approval tier | **The venture revenue account — *"designated account/ledger where customer revenue for an approved venture is recorded before allocation"*** **[S: App. A §14.3]** | **Never a membership fee, a participation fee, a recruitment payment, a donation with a commercial condition, or money from a beneficiary relationship** | **`BLOCKED — A-N16`. No account, no mandate, no entity. The ledger specification exists; the account does not** |
| **B** | **WORKER / MEMBER COMPENSATION** | **Allocations from flow A at priority 1, and the leader allocation at priority 4** | **To pay verified work, in full, on time, with any shortfall recorded and settled** | The compensation formula *(which does not yet exist)*; the four-hand segregation; National approval for any change to percentages, priority order or multiplier | **The payment sheet with formula version, approvals and evidence retained** **[S: Ch.19 step 13]** | **Never a payment calculated from a count of people; never a share of a team's revenue as a differential; never a payment to a person for recruiting** | **`BLOCKED — no formula, no account, no classification`** |
| **C** | **ORGANISATIONAL OPERATING FUNDS** | **Priority 5 from flow A · the explicit funding map's other named sources *(float, seeded reserve, validated donation, City/State resourcing)*** | **To fund the existence cost `C_L1`, the capability cost `C_L2` and the control system, which the waterfall does not fund** | **The funding map, with no blank cell** *(`B-26`)*; the recipient of priority 5 is `H-Q17` — **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | A funding-map row per cost layer per stage; an operating-cost record with receipt, approval and link | **Never a social-service allocation used as operating cost — the social fund is barred from *"ordinary operating salaries"* **[S: Ch.20]**; never a venture bailout** | **`UNFUNDED — no source named anywhere in the corpus`. This is the architecture's largest open economic gap** |
| **D** | **SOCIAL-SERVICE FUNDS** | **Priority 7 from flow A, *"only when surplus conditions are met"* · a restricted mission fund with defined sources** | **To deliver the mission: the four categories that are always free, and the restricted programme list** | **Programme approval tier by *"amount, risk, beneficiary and geography"* **[S: Ch.20]**; restricted ledger; the six apparatus requirements** | **Disbursement evidence, reporting and audit, per the fund's own definition** | **Never ordinary operating salaries, never venture bailouts, never political or religious activity** **[appendix.txt:6969–6979]**; **never a payment for access to help** | **`CONDITIONAL — cannot arise before surplus, and no surplus can arise before flow A exists`** |

## VI.2 The five further pools, evaluated separately

**[P]** The task requires that reserves, emergency/wage protection, founder compensation, reinvestment and residual surplus each have a defined purpose and governance. **They are not flows; they are pools that a flow may feed, and each has its own rule.**

| Pool | **Purpose** | **Fed from** | **Governance** | **What it may not do** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Venture reserve / wage-protection buffer** | *"Continuity, future costs and worker wage protection"* — to pay verified workers in a weak month | **Flow A at priority 6** | **National custody; release by approval matrix; *"Reserve release rules — NOT FINAL"* **[S: Ch.33]**; `A-N15` custody `UNRESOLVED`** | **Not a venture bailout — *"SOP-10 flags 'venture bailout' as abuse"*; not a lateral transfer to another team** | **`POSITION CORRECT, FUNDING SEQUENCE WRONG` — MD-05** |
| **First-season float** *(seeded)* | To cover the existence cost `C_L1` before any revenue arrives | **Not from flow A — it does not exist yet. From a named external source** | **A cap, a term, a reconciliation, one renewal maximum; request locally, sponsor sign-off at City, renewal at State, cap and term at National; tax and accounting `[V]`** | **Not a salary substitute; not an open-ended subsidy; not a resource to be captured (`G-15`)** | **`PROPOSED — DOES NOT EXIST IN SOURCE`. The only candidate instrument for `C_L1`** |
| **Founder compensation** | Payment for actual leadership work, under a cap | **Flow A at priority 4** | **`LDR-U16` / `SR-20` `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)`; a recorded position even if nil** | **Not equity, not profit share, not an ownership stake — leader equity is prohibited **[S-D:2999]**; not applicable at Mohalla level** | **`BLOCKED — authority unresolved`** |
| **Reinvestment** | Capability, equipment, training, and the second venture track | **Flow A, after priorities 1–6, before or alongside priority 7** | **A named decision, an approval tier, and a stated purpose; **the corpus has no reinvestment line, so this pool is `NEW` and its position in the sequence is determined in Part X*** | **Not a reserve by another name; not a route around priority 7's surplus condition** | **`NEW — POSITION DETERMINED AT PART X.6`** |
| **Residual surplus / founder upside** | *"Remaining surplus after priority obligations and caps as defined by approved structure"* | **Flow A at priority 8** | **GC-01 N-3 `LIVE`; `CF-06` against *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"*; `CF-16` against the leader-equity prohibition** | **Not distributable while the entity form is undecided; not distributable to a person who approved the distribution** | **`BLOCKED — no approved structure exists, and the definition says so`** |

## VI.3 What may never cross between flows

| # | **Prohibited crossing** | **Why** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **X-1** | **D → C.** Social-service funds may not pay organisational operating cost | *"Not ordinary operating salaries"* | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[appendix.txt:6969–6979]** |
| **X-2** | **D → A.** Social funds may not subsidise a venture, and a venture may not be bailed out from any restricted fund | *"Not venture bailouts"*; *"SOP-10 flags 'venture bailout' as abuse"* | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S-B: §21.2]** |
| **X-3** | **A → D outside priority 7.** Commercial revenue may not be moved into the mission fund except through the waterfall's surplus condition | The condition is what makes the fund restricted rather than discretionary | **[appendix.txt:6360–6361]** |
| **X-4** | **Any flow → a person, for recruiting a person** | `MLM-1`, `MLM-4`, `MLM-6` | **[S-C: §22.3]** |
| **X-5** | **A beneficiary relationship → flow A.** No revenue may be collected from a person because they received help | *"A beneficiary is never a sales prospect"*; and no venture's collection may depend on a relationship the team cannot ethically leverage | **[S-B: §38.2 B-27 rule 1]**; **[S-B: §19.5 point 3]** |
| **X-6** | **A lateral cash transfer between teams** | Money cannot move laterally; only demand referral and specialist time may | **[S-B: §21.4]**; **MD-12** |
| **X-7** | **The protection function → any flow.** Safeguarding, protection and incident response may never be a revenue source, a collection instrument or a presence for enforcement | `NM-3`; and the corpus forbids any venture that requires the protection function for collection, enforcement or presence | **[S-B: §39.1]**; **[S-B: §19.5 point 4]**; **v1.3-A A-08 DO-NOT list** |
| **X-8** | **Personal money → flow C.** No member funds operations personally, ever | *"Personal spending creates debt-based loyalty"*; `D-3`; `HC-03` is the most common breach | **[S-B: §12.6 rule 1, §34.2 D-3]** |

## VI.4 What must be free

**[P]** Adopted from the corpus without amendment, because the task requires the mission not to become subordinate to money and the corpus has already drawn the line.

| Category | **Position** | **Reasoning preserved** |
|---|---|---|
| **Free community service** — education and learning circles, tutoring, digital literacy, cleaning, plantation, awareness, donation distribution | **`ALWAYS FREE`** | **It is the mission. Charging would convert a service into a product and exclude the poorest** |
| **Emergency assistance** — urgent coordination, disaster support, immediate protective response | **`ALWAYS FREE, AND NEVER CONDITIONAL`** | ***"A price at the moment of emergency is coercion"*** |
| **Safeguarding response** — protecting a child or vulnerable adult, referral, reporting | **`ALWAYS FREE, AND MANDATORY`** | **It is a veto gate. *"A financial consideration may never enter the decision"*** |
| **Educational support** — study materials, learning circles, adult and digital literacy | **`FREE AT THE POINT OF USE`** | **With the tension named: education is *also* a venture category. Resolved by separation — same content, different payer, separately accounted, and the free provision must not shrink** |
| **Subsidised service** — a real cost where the recipient pays part | **`PERMITTED ONLY BY EXPLICIT, APPROVED, PUBLISHED RULE`** | ***"Sliding scales are legitimate in principle and dangerous in practice"*** |
| **Business customer services** — venture work for a paying customer | **`PAID — AND THIS IS CORRECT`** | ***"The customer is not a beneficiary. Charging a business for a website is not contamination; it is the venture"*** |

## VI.5 What must never be monetised

**[P]** `NM-1 … NM-12`, adopted as **vetoes**, not weights (EA-23). **Permanent, at every level, with no approval route.**

| ID | **Category** | **Ground** | **ID** | **Category** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **NM-1** | Vulnerable people's access to help | Selling access converts need into a market and excludes the poorest | **NM-7** | Verification, membership status or organisational endorsement | Selling verification destroys its evidentiary value and creates a two-tier membership |
| **NM-2** | Emergency assistance | A price at the moment of emergency is coercion, and it incentivises waiting for a payable emergency | **NM-8** | Influence over decisions — priority assessment, venture approval, resource allocation, ranking position | Corruption by another name; the segregation rules exist to prevent it |
| **NM-3** | Safeguarding, protection and incident response | The protection function's legitimacy depends on being beyond commerce | **NM-9** | Political or religious access | Already prohibited; the social fund is *"not political or religious activity"* |
| **NM-4** | Sensitive personal information — conduct, incident, safeguarding, beneficiary identity | Data is not an asset to be sold | **NM-10** | Volunteer labour, as a sellable resource | Supplying members' unpaid time for money is labour brokerage |
| **NM-5** | Incident and case records | Sealed for protection, not for product | **NM-11** | Data-derived behavioural or credit inference about members or beneficiaries | Profiling vulnerable people for third-party value is the highest-harm use of the estate |
| **NM-6** | Beneficiary testimony, images and stories | *"We helped them" is never "we may sell their story"* | **NM-12** | Access to the organisation's own audit, grievance or integrity routes | ***"A complaint channel that costs money is not a channel"*** |

> ### **THE TEST THAT DECIDES ANY NEW PROPOSAL**
>
> *"Would monetising this make the organisation's help conditional on a person's ability to pay, or make a person's vulnerability valuable to the organisation?"*
>
> **If yes to either → prohibited, permanently, at every level, with no approval route.** **[S-B: §39.2]**, adopted verbatim. **The second limb is the subtler and more dangerous one: a category can be monetised without charging the vulnerable person — by selling data *about* them, images *of* them, or access *to* them.**

## VI.6 The economic boundary of the organisation

**[P]** Bound by **EA-19, EA-20**. **Four candidates exist, and the boundary between them is not yet a legal boundary.**

| Actor | **Economic boundary today** | **What would have to be true for money to cross** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sewak Sathi Sanstha** | **A `PROPOSED` public name, not a legal person. It cannot receive, hold, pay, contract, employ or be taxed** | **`SR-01` decided, an entity constituted, an account opened under a mandate, workers classified** | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **Krytos** | **Declared a *"Virtual Brain (VB)"* and the *"ecosystem head"*; **0 corpus occurrences of either term**; no constitutive document; no legal structuring work completed** | **A decision whether to constitute it at all, and if so as what — which is GC-01 and `SR-01`, not an economic question** | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL STRUCTURE VALIDATION`** |
| **Vasu Gupta, personally** | **A natural person who declares he will own and control Krytos. He can instruct counsel, hold property, and pay from his own funds; none of that is organisational money** | **Any flow between him and the organisation is a related-party transaction and takes Part XVI's route** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` + `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **A venture** | **Not a person. An approved activity inside the organisation, with its own revenue account *(as a ledger)*, its own direct costs and its own closure rule** | **A venture never becomes a separate economic actor without an entity decision; treating it as one is how an unregistered business acquires an unregistered owner** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |

> ### **`NO TRANSFER BETWEEN THESE FOUR ACTORS IS DECLARED LEGALLY PERMISSIBLE ANYWHERE IN THIS INSTRUMENT.`**
>
> **Part XV designs the architecture of the question. Part XVI designs the control that would apply if a transfer were ever permitted. Neither authorises a transfer (EA-19, EA-20).**

# PART VII — VENTURE TAXONOMY

## VII.1 The seven source categories — the approval universe

**[P]** Bound by **EA-2, MD-07**. **Reproduced with the source's own controls. *"No category is added. None is assumed viable anywhere"* **[S-B: §7.1]**. All seven remain `POTENTIAL` until approved one by one **[S: Ch.33]**.**

| # | **Category** | **Controls stated in the source** | **Startup requirement** | **Skill** | **Sales difficulty** | **Position at Stage 0–2** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Software / websites / apps** | *"Technical quality, scope, contracts, data/security review"* | **Low cash; high skill; portfolio needed** | Development, QA, scoping | **High — crowded market, trust barrier** | **Capability track only; `h_proj` risk** |
| **2** | **AI automation** | *"Disclose limitations; no unlawful data processing or autonomous consequential decisions"* | **Low cash; high skill + high governance** | AI operations, data handling, disclosure discipline | **High** | **Capability track only; review load rises (`HC-11`)** |
| **3** | **Digital services / analytics** | *"Customer data legality, confidentiality and source controls"* | **Low cash; medium–high skill** | Analysis, data privacy, reporting | **Medium–high — needs a demonstrable artefact** | **Capability track; the highest contribution per hour of the low-cash set** |
| **4** | **Design / editing** | *"IP ownership, consent, fact-check and content rights"* | **Very low cash; medium skill; the Editor family already exists** | Copy, video, graphic, proofreading, content quality review | **Medium — high volume needed** | **Cash track candidate; lowest contribution per hour** |
| **5** | **Education / training** | *"Trainer competence and safeguarding for minors"* | **Low cash; medium skill; certification and safeguarding gate it** | Subject mastery, teaching, **certified safeguarding** | **Medium — real local demand** | **`BLOCKED` at Stage 0–2 without certified safeguarding (`SR-31`, MVC-4)** |
| **6** | **Local business technology** | *"Do not access customer accounts without permission"* | **Low cash; medium skill** | SME digital support, setup, maintenance | **Low–medium — the customer is next door; retainer potential** | **The best cash-track candidate: easiest close, recurring** |
| **7** | **Physical products / services** | *"Health, safety, licensing, tax and liability review"* | **Cash, stock, storage, licensing — highest of all** | Procurement, quality, logistics, compliance | **Medium** | **`EXCLUDED` at Stage 0–2 — *"highest risk; not for Stage 0–2"*** |

## VII.2 The fourteen candidate categories, mapped — nothing enters by renaming

**[P]** The task names fourteen candidates to evaluate. **Each is mapped onto the seven, or excluded, with its ground. No eighth category is created (MD-07).**

| Candidate | **Maps to** | **Determination** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AI / software development** | **1, and 2 where automation is the deliverable** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category; the split matters because 2 carries a higher governance load |
| **Websites / apps** | **1** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category |
| **Automation** | **2** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category, with *"disclose limitations; no unlawful data processing or autonomous consequential decisions"* |
| **Digital services** | **3** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category |
| **Design** | **4** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category; the Editor family already exists in the role structure |
| **Editing** | **4** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category |
| **Marketing** | **3 or 4, depending on the deliverable** | **`EVALUABLE — WITH A CONSTRAINT`.** The role that would deliver it, Social Media Manager, does not produce customers **[S-B: §18.4]**, and marketing to beneficiaries is prohibited (`B-27` rule 1). **Marketing as a *service to a business customer* is legitimate; marketing as an *acquisition channel for the organisation* is not a revenue line** | **[S-B: §18.4]**; **RT-30** |
| **Analytics** | **3** | **`EVALUABLE`** | Existing category, with the data-legality control |
| **Consulting** | **3 where it is a defined deliverable; otherwise `UNCLASSIFIED`** | **`EVALUABLE — NARROWLY`.** Open-ended advice has no acceptance criteria, and step 4 of the lifecycle requires *"scope, price, timeline, acceptance criteria, data/IP/confidentiality terms"*. **A consulting engagement without acceptance criteria cannot be verified, and unverified work is not payable** | **[S: Ch.17 step 4]**; **RT-03** |
| **Education** | **5** | **`BLOCKED` at Stage 0–2** | Certified safeguarding is a `VALIDATION REQUIRED` Tier-0 item (`SR-31`) |
| **Local business solutions** | **6** | **`EVALUABLE — THE PREFERRED CASH TRACK`** | Easiest close, recurring, and the customer is adjacent |
| **Digital products** | **1 or 4, and only where a customer buys a defined artefact** | **`EVALUABLE — WITH A CONSTRAINT`.** A product sold repeatedly is still a sale per customer; **it may not become a passive income stream derived from members, because that would put a count of people back into the payment chain (`MLM-1`)** | **[S-C: §22.3]** |
| **Physical products** | **7** | **`EXCLUDED` at Stage 0–2** | Cash, stock, storage, licensing — the highest requirement of all |
| **Other legitimate ventures** | **No category** | **`NOT ADMITTED`.** The universe is closed at seven: adding a category is an amendment, not a mapping. *"Approve categories one by one using risk/value framework"*** | **[S: Ch.33]**; **MD-07** |

| Field | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Candidates mapped to an existing category** | **13 of 14** |
| **Candidates not admitted** | **1 — "other legitimate ventures"** |
| **New categories created** | **0** |
| **Candidates evaluable now** | **9** |
| **Candidates blocked at Stage 0–2** | **2 — education *(safeguarding)*, physical products *(cash, stock, licensing)*** |
| **Candidates carrying an added constraint** | **3 — marketing, consulting, digital products** |
| **Categories approved** | **0 — all seven remain `POTENTIAL`** |

## VII.3 The twelve evaluation criteria and the four gates

**[P]** Retained from the corpus **(RT-13)**, because they are already derived from source and already ordered cheapest-test-first.

| Gate | **Test** | **Fails if** | **Who decides** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G1 — LAWFUL** | Legality, ethics, data, permissions, licensing, safeguarding | Any unresolved legal question; any regulated activity without certification; any category-7 licensing gap | **Human. `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` where a legal question is open — and G1 is absolute: *"A highly profitable venture that fails G1 is refused, and the refusal is recorded"*** |
| **G2 — CAPABLE** | Verified skill exists or is obtainable from the State pool **before** commitment | The team must learn on the customer's project | **Human — the AI & Business Manager proposes; the approval tier decides** |
| **G3 — ECONOMICAL** | Contribution per delivery hour above the approved floor; break-even volume reachable inside capacity; collection rate and collection window survivable | Contribution per hour below floor, or break-even volume above capacity | **Human, on measured variables. **The floor is `[M]` — a value to be approved, never invented here (EA-9)*** |
| **G4 — MISSION-COMPATIBLE** | Does not distort the service programme; does not create the incentives `I-01 … I-08`; social value assessed honestly | It crowds out service hours below budget, or it depends on a relationship the team cannot ethically leverage | **Human — and a veto, not a weight (EA-23)** |

**[P] The twelve criteria**, each a variable with a source anchor and no value: local demand · local skills · local competition · available technology · customer ability to pay · delivery distance · startup cost · repeatability · margin per project and per delivery hour · social value · risk · team capacity after governance and service hours are subtracted **[S-B: §19.2]**. **The scoring scale is `[M]`, left to approval, *"so that no numeric weighting is invented here"*.**

## VII.4 The two-track strategy, and what blocks it

| Track | **Purpose** | **Category** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Track 1 — cash track** | Reach bare survival, then healthy break-even; chosen for speed to first collection, accepting low contribution | **Typically 6 or 4** | **`AVAILABLE IN DESIGN` — blocked in fact by `A-N16`, `SR-01` and G1** |
| **Track 2 — capability track** | Enter a higher-contribution category with the skill borrowed rather than learned on the customer | **Typically 3, 1 or 2** | **`BLOCKED — no City pipeline and no State specialist pool exist`. MD-16** |
| **The three rules** | Track 2 may not start before Track 1 has produced `n` consecutive collected periods `[M]`; Track 1 may not be abandoned while it funds the existence cost; **neither track may consume service hours below budget** | — | **Retained verbatim** |
| **Single-track fallback** | At Stage 0–1, one track in category 6 or 4, with Track 2 deferred and the deferral recorded | — | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`**, required so that a two-track strategy does not become a two-track promise |

# PART VIII — VENTURE LIFECYCLE

## VIII.1 The eleven stages, mapped from the source's fourteen steps

**[P]** Bound by **MD-01**. **The mapping is stated so that nothing is lost and nothing is smuggled in.**

| Stage | **Name** | **Source steps mapped in** | **Entry evidence** | **Exit evidence** | **Who decides** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **IDEA** | *idea* | A stated opportunity, in the approved-category universe | A one-page proposal | **Any member may propose** |
| **2** | **SCREENING** | *validation* (first half) | The twelve criteria scored on the approved scale `[M]` | A screening record with reasons | **AI & Business Manager screens; the local team may decide** *(reversible, local)* |
| **3** | **ECONOMIC VALIDATION** | *validation* (second half) | Demand evidence, price evidence, contribution per delivery hour, collection window, break-even volume inside capacity | **G3 passed or refused with a reason** | **Human, on measured variables** |
| **4** | **LEGAL / COMPLIANCE CHECK** | **promoted from an attribute of *approval tier* to a stage of its own** | The category's own controls; data legality; safeguarding; licensing; worker classification; tax treatment | **G1 passed, or refused. `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` where any question is open — and no stage-5 work may begin on an open G1** | **Human, with counsel where required. **This is the stage that does not exist in the old sequence and that the current organisation cannot skip*** |
| **5** | **PILOT** | *setup*, partly | A scope, price, timeline, acceptance criteria, and data/IP/confidentiality terms **[S: Ch.17 step 4]** | One full delivery cycle on paper, with the records of Part XXI | **Approval tier** |
| **6** | **CUSTOMER VALIDATION** | *customer*, *assignment*, *production*, *QA*, *delivery*, *acceptance* | A real customer, real delivery, real acceptance | **Acceptance recorded; quality evidence retained; *"no artificial splitting"*** | **The customer accepts; the organisation records** |
| **7** | **APPROVAL** | *approval tier* | All four gates passed | An approval record with the tier, the amount, the risk, the data class and the novelty | **Approval matrix by amount and risk **[S: App. E]** — `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` where the matrix's authority is unresolved** |
| **8** | **DELIVERY** | *production*, *QA*, *delivery* | The approved scope | Delivery and acceptance records | **AI & Business Manager manages approved customer delivery** |
| **9** | **RECONCILIATION** | *revenue*, *waterfall* | **A collected receipt** | Revenue recorded in the venture revenue account; the waterfall run in order; the payment sheet produced with its formula version | **Four hands, separately** |
| **10** | **REVIEW** | *performance* | The reconciled period | An exception register; a concentration check; an anomaly review; a lesson recorded | **Human review; AI may surface signals but *"may not conclude that gaming occurred"*** |
| **11** | **SCALE / MODIFY / CLOSE** | *scale/improve/close* | The six scaling conditions, or the closure conditions | A recorded decision with a reason | **Approval tier; closure at National where a reserve or float is outstanding** |

| Field | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Source steps mapped** | **14 of 14 — none dropped** |
| **Stages** | **11** |
| **Stages promoted to their own gate** | **1 — stage 4, legal/compliance** |
| **Stages whose authority is unresolved** | **2 — stage 7 and stage 11, both `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **Stages that may be performed on paper today** | **1, 2, 3, 5 *(design only)*, 10 *(design only)* — and none of them authorises money to move** |
| **Stages blocked by `A-N16`** | **6, 7, 8, 9, 11 — every stage at which money moves** |

## VIII.2 Human approval gates

| Gate | **Between stages** | **The question it answers** | **May AI decide it?** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HG-1** | 2 → 3 | Is this worth validating economically? | **No — AI may screen and recommend** |
| **HG-2** | 3 → 4 | Did it pass G3? | **No** |
| **HG-3** | 4 → 5 | **Is it lawful, and is every legal question closed or explicitly accepted as open with a stated consequence?** | **No — and AI may not make legal conclusions at all **[S: Const. Art.12]**** |
| **HG-4** | 6 → 7 | Did a real customer accept real delivery? | **No — acceptance is the customer's act and the record is a human's** |
| **HG-5** | 7 → 8 | Is the venture approved at the right tier? | **No — *"cannot … approve ventures"* **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]**** |
| **HG-6** | 9 | **Is the waterfall run in the right order, with the right amounts, by the right hands?** | **No — AI may *"compute per the approved formula version"*; it may not *"approve or alter pay"* **[S-D: §30.2]**** |
| **HG-7** | 10 → 11 | Scale, modify or close? | **No** |

## VIII.3 What AI may and may not do in the economic system

**[P]** The corpus's twelve-function matrix **[S-D: §30.2]** is adopted, and extended with the seven economic functions it does not yet cover (MD-15). **Every row names the human who decides.**

| Function | **AI may** | **AI may not** | **Human who decides** | **Source** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Score computation** | Aggregate, normalise within cohort, flag anomalies, attach a confidence rating | Finalise a score; determine a gate outcome | Performance function + Audit | **[S-D: §30.2]** |
| **Payment** | Compute per the approved formula version | **Approve or alter pay** | Finance, per the segregation rule | **[S-D: §30.2]**; **[appendix.txt:6925–6948]** |
| **Anomaly and gaming detection** | Surface detection signals | **Conclude that gaming occurred** | Audit | **[S-D: §30.2]** |
| **Records and memory** | Draft decision records from structured inputs; index; summarise lessons | **Write the reason — *"the reason is the human's"*** | The decision-maker | **[S-D: §30.2]** |
| **Safeguarding** | Route a report immediately to a human | Assess risk to a child or vulnerable adult; close a case | Designated safeguarding role `[V]` | **[S-D: §30.2]** |
| **Pricing** *(new)* | Estimate a price band from comparable recorded engagements; flag a price below the approved floor | **Set a price; approve a discount; waive a floor** | AI & Business Manager proposes; the approval tier decides | **MD-15; `NEW`** |
| **Contract formation** *(new)* | Draft from an approved template; check that required terms are present | **Form a binding contract; vary a term; accept a customer's term** | A human with contracting authority — **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`, because no such authority is constituted** | **MD-15; `NEW`** |
| **Credit and collection** *(new)* | Age receivables; flag concentration; predict a collection window from recorded history | **Extend credit; write off a debt; agree a payment plan; contact a debtor** | Funds Manager records; the approval tier decides | **MD-15; `NEW`** |
| **Expense classification** *(new)* | Propose a category and a direct-cost link | **Classify finally; approve an expense; reclassify across ventures** | Person B *(approve)* and Person C *(record)*, separately | **MD-15; `G-05`** |
| **Reserve or float draw** *(new)* | Compute the request; check the cap and the term | **Release funds** | **National, by approval matrix — *"cannot release reserve or social-impact funds without required approvals"*** | **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]** |
| **Related-party detection** *(new)* | Flag a counterparty matching a declared interest | **Clear a transaction; conclude that a conflict exists** | A person outside the transaction | **MD-15; Part XVI** |
| **Venture closure** *(new)* | Assemble the four closure conditions' evidence | **Close a venture; decide that workers are paid; decide that customers are treated fairly** | Approval tier; National where a reserve is outstanding | **RT-15** |

> ### **`AI MAY RECOMMEND; HUMANS DECIDE. AI CANNOT AUTONOMOUSLY MAKE CONSEQUENTIAL HR, WAGE, LEGAL, SAFEGUARDING, SAFETY, MAJOR FUND OR LEADERSHIP DECISIONS.`**
>
> **[S: Const. Art.12], verbatim, `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` **[S-D:1161, 4529 `LDR-C05`]**. The role specification says the same in its own words: the AI & Business Manager *"cannot launch unapproved high-risk ventures, fake revenue, set wages alone, misuse customer data, promise illegal services, or let AI make consequential decisions"* **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]**.**

## VIII.4 Scaling and closure

| Rule | **Content** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|
| **SC-1** | **A venture scales only on all six conditions together** — real customer demand, quality delivery, acceptable risk, documented revenue, controlled costs, **and sufficient trained people** | **`RETAINED`** — RT-14, **[appendix.txt:6287–6292]** §13.5 |
| **SC-2** | **The sixth condition is a people condition and cannot be bought.** Scaling without trained people hollows the organisation; growth is authorised on demand, not on leadership supply | **`RETAINED`** — **[S-D: L-22]** |
| **SC-3** | **A stage is evidenced, not declared; stages can be lost; stage does not equal size** | **`RETAINED`** — MD-20, **[S-B: §30.2]** |
| **CL-1** | **Failure becomes a learning event only on four conditions** — money reconciled, customers treated fairly, workers paid according to approved rules, reasons documented | **`RETAINED`** — RT-15 |
| **CL-2** | **A venture that repeatedly cannot pay workers lawfully and sustainably *"must be restructured, paused or closed"*** | **`RETAINED`** — **[appendix.txt:6370–6376]** §14.5, verbatim |
| **CL-3** | **"Repeatedly" must be defined before it is used, and the definition is a governance decision** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** — **[S-B: §23.4]** |
| **CL-4** | **Closure is a decision with a named decider and a recorded reason, never a lapse** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |

# PART IX — REVENUE ARCHITECTURE

## IX.1 What revenue is, and what is not revenue

**[P]** Bound by **DO-2, EA-13**. **The distinction matters because three of the four things that can put money into the organisation are not revenue, and treating them as revenue is how a mission organisation loses its mission.**

| Source of money | **Is it revenue?** | **Its nature** | **Its conditions** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A collected customer receipt for real value delivered under an approved venture** | **`YES — THE ONLY ONE`** | Flow A | Approved category · G1 … G4 passed · acceptance recorded · reconciled | **`BLOCKED — A-N16, SR-01`** |
| **A seeded first-season float** | **`NO — it is capital, not revenue`** | A liability or a restricted instrument with a cap, a term and a reconciliation | A named source; one renewal maximum; `G-15` capture controls | **`DOES NOT EXIST IN SOURCE` — proposed** |
| **A validated donation** | **`NO — it is funding`** | Flow C or D depending on restriction | *"Donations may be possible **after validation**"*; donor intent must be honoured; receipt, restriction, storage, allocation and report | **`CONDITIONAL AND UNVALIDATED [V]`** |
| **City or State resourcing** | **`NO — it is internal allocation`** | Materials and specialist time, not cash | *"Donated/purchased materials with approved evidence and priorities"* | **`NON-CASH ONLY — money cannot move laterally`** |
| **Cross-subsidy from a stronger team** | **`NO — and cash may not move at all`** | Layer 1 non-cash only: demand referral and specialist time | No lateral cash mechanism exists | **`PERMITTED NON-CASH · PROHIBITED CASH`** |
| **A membership, joining, kit, training or materials fee** | **`NO — AND PROHIBITED`** | — | `MLM-3`, `MLM-5` | **`PROHIBITED PERMANENTLY`** |
| **A recruitment commission, referral fee or bounty** | **`NO — AND PROHIBITED`** | — | `MLM-4`, `MLM-6` | **`PROHIBITED PERMANENTLY`** |
| **Money from a beneficiary, or from a person because they received help** | **`NO — AND PROHIBITED`** | — | `B-27` rule 1; `X-5` | **`PROHIBITED PERMANENTLY`** |
| **Money for data, testimony, images, endorsement, verification, access or priority** | **`NO — AND PROHIBITED`** | — | `NM-4 … NM-8`, `NM-11`, `NM-12`; paid priority prohibited | **`PROHIBITED PERMANENTLY`** |

> ### **`REVENUE HAS EXACTLY ONE SOURCE. EVERYTHING ELSE IS FUNDING, CAPITAL, ALLOCATION — OR PROHIBITED.`**

## IX.2 The anti-MLM firewall

**[P]** Bound by **EA-13, EA-23**. **The corpus's seven rules are adopted as the floor, verbatim in substance, and extended with four economic controls that the corpus does not yet have.**

| # | **Rule** | **Origin** |
|---|---|---|
| **MLM-1** | **No compensation, bonus, incentive, allocation or benefit may be calculated from any count of people** — recruited, active, paused, in a team, in a downline, or in any grouping | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-2** | **No compensation may be derived from another person's contribution** except through an approved, capped, transparent leader differential justified by verified leadership **work** — **and the differential may not be a percentage of the team's revenue** | **[S-C: §22.3]**; **[S: App. A §15.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-3** | **No membership fee, joining fee, kit purchase, training purchase, materials purchase or mandatory subscription** may be required at any level. Free to join, free to participate, free to leave | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-4** | **No recruitment commission, referral fee or bounty** of any kind, in cash or in kind, at any level | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-5** | **No mandatory purchase** of any product or service as a condition of participation, advancement, role or recognition | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-6** | **No referral chain.** An introducer receives no benefit, preferential treatment or record advantage. *"Attribution of an introduction may be recorded for thanks, never for value"* | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-7** | **Economic value must come from real service + real work + real products + real customer value** — every rupee distributed must trace to a collected customer receipt through the waterfall, or to an approved and published funding source | **[S-C: §22.3]**, adopted |
| **MLM-8** *(new)* | **No venture may be approved whose business model depends on the number of participants rather than on customer demand.** The test is stated as a question at G3: *if membership doubled and customers did not, would this venture's revenue rise?* If yes → refused | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |
| **MLM-9** *(new)* | **No compensation element may be calculated on a base that includes another person's compensation.** This closes the indirect route by which a differential becomes a share of a team: a percentage of *revenue* is forbidden by MLM-2, and a percentage of *compensation* would achieve the same result | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |
| **MLM-10** *(new)* | **No product may be sold to a member on terms not available to a non-member customer, and no product may be sold to members as the venture's principal market.** A venture whose customers are its own members is a fee with extra steps | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |
| **MLM-11** *(new)* | **Recognition, ranking, endorsement and role may never be sold, traded, or conditioned on any economic contribution.** This extends `NM-7` and `NM-8` from the organisation's external offers to its internal economy | **`NEW`**, extending **[S-B: §39.1]** |

## IX.3 The four detection tests, and the one that matters

| Test | **Question** | **Data** | **Required result** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** | **Is any person's income correlated with the number of people they brought in?** | Compensation records against recruitment attribution | **`ZERO CORRELATION`. *"T1 is the decisive one and it is computable from records the architecture already keeps … A single correlation report, run annually, is the entire anti-MLM assurance programme"*** **[S-C: §22.4]** |
| **T2** | Does any team's headcount growth outrun its growth in customer revenue and outcome-verified service? | Metrics; the source's own anti-gaming controls | **No divergence** |
| **T3** | Does any communication describe earning in terms of building a team rather than doing work? | Communications review; prohibited appeals | **None** |
| **T4** | **Does anyone pay anything to participate?** | Zero-tolerance audit | **`ZERO`. Any receipt of money from a participant for participation is a veto-gate breach** |

## IX.4 Where customers actually come from

**[P]** Bound by **RT-21, MD-16**. **The corpus has already done this analysis and its conclusions are unflattering and correct.**

| Finding | **Content** | **Consequence for the architecture** |
|---|---|---|
| **Social media does not produce customers** | It produces attention; attention is not a receipt | **The Social Media Manager is not a revenue role, and its cost is real and unfunded (`PC-07`)** |
| **The only cheap customers are repeat customers** | Acquisition cost is mostly invisible; a first customer in a neighbourhood costs trust, time and delivery quality | **`r_repeat` is a first-class criterion, and category 6's retainer potential is why it is the preferred cash track** |
| **A Mohalla team is not required to originate its own demand** | *"Identify customer needs, combine skills, propose ventures and coordinate delivery"* is a **City** responsibility | **Acquisition cost should be modelled at City level and allocated, not duplicated in every team** |
| **The reverse also holds** | If the City has no pipeline capability, every team must cold-acquire with no brand, no portfolio, no budget and no sales training | **`BLOCKED` today — no City layer exists. This, not local effort, separates a viable team from a non-viable one** |
| **Collection is the hard part** | Normal SME behaviour is late payment; invoiced is not collected | **The receipts-versus-invoiced basis is an unresolved accounting policy `[V]` and it decides whether workers are paid in a delayed month** |

## IX.5 Revenue recognition, concentration and reconciliation

| Rule | **Content** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|
| **RR-1** | **The basis of revenue recognition — receipts or invoiced — is not decided, and it is not a technicality.** The source's own definition leaves it open: *"Total customer receipts or invoiced revenue before deductions, **depending on final accounting policy**"* | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — accounting and tax |
| **RR-2** | **Until the basis is decided, both are recorded separately and neither is used alone.** A period reports `V_invoiced` and `V_collected` and the gap between them, so that the choice cannot be made invisibly later | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** |
| **RR-3** | **Revenue is recognised per venture, in the venture revenue account, before allocation** | **`RETAINED`** — RT-06 |
| **RR-4** | **Concentration is measured, reported and limited.** A single customer's share of a venture's revenue is a standing field; the limit is `[M]`, to be approved, never invented | **`RETAINED`** in structure — **[S-B: §27.5, §36.1 G-10]**; **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** as to the limit |
| **RR-5** | **Every period is reconciled to bank or cash records** — the control that defeats revenue-timing manipulation | **`RETAINED`** — RT-18 |
| **RR-6** | **No revenue is recorded without a link to a customer, an acceptance and a delivery record.** Unpaid work is never recorded as revenue | **`RETAINED`** — RT-17; **`G-01`** |

# PART X — MONEY-FLOW ARCHITECTURE: THE WATERFALL FROM FIRST PRINCIPLES

## X.1 The foundational principle, tested rather than preserved

**[P]** The task requires that *"THE PRIORITY ORDER IS THE CONTROL; PERCENTAGES ARE A PLANNING TOOL"* be tested against the current architecture, not automatically preserved. **The test:**

| # | **Test** | **Result** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Does the current architecture still have revenue that must be divided?** | **Yes — one source, flow A, and eight claims on it** |
| **2** | **Does the current architecture still face weak months?** | **Yes — and more severely: a standing-start team has no float, no reserve and no centre funding** |
| **3** | **Would a percentage table break it?** | **Yes, and the source explains exactly how: *"A percentage table applied blindly to gross revenue can break the system because it may pay discretionary allocations before wages, taxes or direct costs"*** |
| **4** | **Is there a better control available?** | **No. A percentage table requires a stable denominator; a cap requires a decision-maker that does not exist; a budget requires a funder that is not named. **Sequencing is the only control that behaves identically at a small collection and at a large one, and the only one that needs no number to operate*** |
| **5** | **Does it conflict with anything current?** | **No — but it is incomplete in three ways, addressed at §X.5, §X.6 and §X.7** |

> ### **`THE PRINCIPLE IS RETAINED.`**
>
> **Formally defined, as the task requires:**

| Element | **Definition** |
|---|---|
| **Why it exists** | **To decide, in advance and without discretion, which claims on a limited collection are paid and which fall to zero. It converts a negotiation into an arithmetic** |
| **What it controls** | **The **order** of allocation from the venture revenue account. Not the amounts, not the eligibility, not the accounting basis, not the cadence** |
| **When it applies** | **Every period in which a collection occurs, and every partial collection. It does not apply at a collection of zero, where there is nothing to sequence — and at a collection of zero the operative control is runway, not priority** |
| **Who can override it** | **Nobody at team, City or State level. *"Waterfall priority order"* is outside delegated authority at every level: it is *"constitutional; v1.3-A Tier-1 candidate (A-13 T1.x)"*** **[S-B: §20.2]** |
| **Do overrides require human approval?** | **An override requires a Tier-1 amendment: National approval **and** professional validation **and** the founder's formal amendment. **No AI, no officer, no team and no emergency may override it. Where an emergency appears to require it, the correct act is a reserve or float draw under its own authority, not a reordering*** |
| **How overrides are recorded** | **As an amendment, with the reason, the effective date, the validation relied on and the decision-maker named — never as an accounting adjustment** |
| **How legal/statutory priority interacts with it** | **Statutory obligations are not merely priority 2. **Priorities 1 and 2 form one indivisible tier in a weak period**, because the source's own bad-month rule protects *"verified worker compensation **and mandatory dues**"* together **[appendix.txt:6370–6376]**, and because the deduction component of a wage cannot be separated from the wage. **§X.5** |

## X.2 The source order, and the task's candidate order, compared

**[P]** The source order is `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`. The task's ten-layer candidate is a design proposal. **They are set side by side and the differences are named — and no reordering is performed by this instrument.**

| Position | **The source order** *(approved)* | **The task's candidate order** | **Difference** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Worker compensation** | Revenue received | Not a difference — "revenue received" is the input, not a claim |
| **2** | **Statutory dues where applicable** | **Statutory obligations** | **The candidate puts statutory first** |
| **3** | **Direct business / delivery costs** | Direct delivery costs | Aligned |
| **4** | **Worker compensation** *(the candidate's position 4)* | — | **The candidate puts worker compensation third, after statutory and direct costs** |
| **5** | **Founder compensation / approved cap** | Operating costs | **The candidate puts founder compensation eighth, after reserves** |
| **6** | **Organisation service fee** | Reserves | Aligned in substance |
| **7** | **Reserves / wage-protection buffer** | Social mission allocation | Aligned in substance |
| **8** | **Social-impact contribution when conditions are met** | Founder compensation | **See position 5** |
| **9** | **Residual surplus / founder upside** | **Reinvestment** | **The source has no reinvestment line** |
| **10** | — | Residual surplus | Aligned with the source's position 8–9 |

**[P] Three real differences, and each is handled differently:**

| # | **Difference** | **Determination** |
|---|---|---|
| **D-A** | **Statutory before or after worker compensation** | **`NEITHER — THEY ARE ONE TIER.`** Derived from the source's own words, not imposed: the bad-month rule protects *"verified worker compensation **and mandatory dues**"* jointly, and the scarcity rule states *"Statutory dues are never reduced by this rule"*. **A linear diagram of eight positions obscures a joint obligation. §X.5 defines the joint tier** |
| **D-B** | **Founder compensation at position 4 or position 8** | **`AN ORDERING QUESTION — NOT RESOLVED HERE.`** The source's position 4 places the founder ahead of the organisation's own funding and ahead of reserves. **The incentive analysis is stated at §X.6 and it is adverse. But the priority order is constitutional: changing it is a Tier-1 amendment requiring National approval, professional validation and the founder's formal amendment. **This instrument does not reorder it. It records the question, the analysis and the route → `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` + `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION (TIER-1)` + `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`*** |
| **D-C** | **Reinvestment has no position in the source order** | **`A GAP — NOT FILLED BY INVENTION.`** Reinvestment is a real claim, and the source has no line for it. **§X.7 specifies three candidate positions and their consequences, and assigns the choice to a Tier-1 amendment. No position is selected** |

## X.3 The waterfall derived against the nine determinants

**[P]** The task requires the ordering to be determined against nine named factors. **Each factor is applied to the source order, and each produces a finding rather than a change.**

| Determinant | **What it requires** | **Effect on the source order** | **Result** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Legal constraints** | Legally compulsory payments cannot be deferred by an internal rule | **Priorities 1 and 2 are both compulsory in substance** | **`JOINT TIER` — §X.5. `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` as to which specific obligations are non-deferrable in the asserted jurisdiction** |
| **2 — Worker rights** | A worker who delivered verified work has the strongest claim in the system, and in many regimes a legally protected one | **Confirms priority 1's position** | **`RETAINED`. And the corpus refuses to dilute it: *"It needs a companion scarcity rule and a cross-period accrual, not a dilution"*** |
| **3 — Tax** | Tax is computed on a base, at a rate, by a regime that depends on the entity form and the worker classification — neither of which exists | **Priority 2's *amount* cannot be computed today** | **`POSITION RETAINED; AMOUNT `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`**. The definition already carries the dependency: *"applicable to the legal structure and worker classification"*** |
| **4 — Cash flow** | Direct costs are usually incurred **before** collection | **Priority 3 is an allocation rule for costs already incurred, not a funding rule for costs about to be incurred** | **`RETAINED WITH A FINDING` — the working-capital gap is real and belongs to Part XVII, not to the waterfall. Reordering would not fix it** |
| **5 — Business survival** | A venture that cannot pay its own direct costs destroys value while it grows | **Confirms priority 3 above the discretionary set** | **`RETAINED`** |
| **6 — Mission protection** | The mission must not be the first thing cut | **Priority 7 funds the mission last, from commerce** | **`RETAINED IN THE COMMERCE WATERFALL — AND SUPPLEMENTED OUTSIDE IT.`** Moving priority 7 up would take money from workers, which is not mission protection. **Part XIII designs the separate pre-surplus instrument** |
| **7 — Governance** | The control system must be funded, or every other control is fictional | **Priority 5 funds it, deferrably, after the founder** | **`RETAINED WITH A FINDING` — see D-B. The gap is closed by the funding map (Part XII), not by reordering** |
| **8 — Founder economics** | The founder's claim must be visible, bounded, and behind the claims of people who cannot protect themselves | **Position 4 is ahead of the organisation and ahead of reserves** | **`QUESTION RECORDED — D-B, §X.6`. Not reordered** |
| **9 — Organisational sustainability** | Reserves must exist before the shock, not after it | **Priority 6 arrives after four prior claims** | **`RETAINED WITH AN INSTRUMENT` — the seeded float of MD-05 exists precisely because priority 6 cannot fund the first weak month** |

## X.4 The computable sequence, and the deferrable set

**[P]** Retained from the corpus's own derivation, because it is already correct and already carries no numbers.

```
S_0 = V_collected                      (the collected receipt, not the invoice — RR-2)
for i = 1 … 8:
    A_i = min( R_i , S_(i-1) )         never pay more than is owed; never more than remains
    S_i = S_(i-1) − A_i                the residual passes down
```

| Set | **Members** | **Rule** |
|---|---|---|
| **Indivisible tier** | **Priorities 1 and 2** | **Neither falls to zero while any money remains. *"The waterfall should not divert money to lower-priority pools while verified worker compensation and mandatory dues remain unpaid"*** **[appendix.txt:6370–6376]** |
| **Non-deferrable** | **Priority 3** | **Direct costs already incurred are owed; a venture that cannot pay them *"must be restructured, paused or closed"*** |
| **Deferrable** | **Priorities 5, 7 and 8** | ***"Deferrable organisational fees and social contributions fall before verified worker compensation is sacrificed, subject to law and final structure"*** |
| **Discretionary in the short run, fatal to skip in the long run** | **Priority 6** | **The corpus's own characterisation of reserves** |
| **Position not determined** | **Reinvestment** | **§X.7** |
| **Position questioned** | **Priority 4** | **§X.6, D-B** |

## X.5 The joint first tier, defined

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`, derived from the source's own bad-month sentence rather than imposed on it.

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **JT-1** | **Priorities 1 and 2 are paid together, in the same period, from the same collection, before any other claim.** Where the collection is insufficient for both, the shortfall is a **single** shortfall and is recorded as such |
| **JT-2** | **The deduction component of a worker's entitlement is not a separate claim.** Withholding is part of discharging priority 1, not a competing priority — **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`**, because whether it is lawful to pay a net amount while an undischarged withholding liability sits in the account is a question for counsel and a CA, not for an architecture |
| **JT-3** | **No scarcity rule may reduce a statutory obligation.** *"Statutory dues are never reduced by this rule"* |
| **JT-4** | **A shortfall in the joint tier is an escalation event, not an accounting entry.** It triggers Part XVIII's failure chain `F-07`, and it is reported upward in the same period |
| **JT-5** | **The joint tier is the only tier that may not be reordered by anyone, at any level, for any reason, including an emergency.** The emergency route is a reserve or float draw under its own authority |

## X.6 The founder-position question, stated and not answered

**[P]** Bound by **EA-17, EA-19, EA-20**. **The analysis is adverse to the source's position 4, and it is recorded rather than acted on.**

| Consideration | **Finding** |
|---|---|
| **What position 4 means** | The founder is paid before the organisation's own infrastructure *(position 5)* and before the reserve that protects workers in the next weak month *(position 6)* |
| **The incentive it creates** | A claim that ranks ahead of the control system's funding creates a structural interest in underfunding the control system — the same distortion `I-06` and `I-07` describe in other forms |
| **The counter-argument, stated fairly** | The source limits it: founder compensation is *"capped, and not applicable at Mohalla level"* **[S-B: §8.7]**, so at the only level that will operate for some time, position 4 pays nothing. **The ordering question is therefore latent, not live, until a City or National layer exists** |
| **What is already prohibited regardless of position** | **Leader equity, profit share or ownership stakes are prohibited** **[S-D:2999]**; the authority to set founder compensation is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)` **[S-F:1897]**; and `CF-16` records the conflict as one *"that cannot be managed"* |
| **Determination** | **`ORDERING QUESTION RECORDED — NOT RESOLVED.`** Changing the priority order is a **Tier-1 amendment** requiring National approval, professional validation and the founder's formal amendment **[S-B: §20.2]**. **This instrument has none of the three and performs no reordering** |
| **Label** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** *(tax and corporate treatment of founder compensation ahead of organisational funding)* **· `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION (TIER-1 AMENDMENT)` · `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** |

## X.7 Reinvestment, and the three candidate positions

| Candidate | **Position** | **Consequence if adopted** | **Consequence if rejected** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **RI-1** | **Between reserves and social contribution** *(after 6, before 7)* | Capability is built before the mission is funded from surplus — defensible, because a venture that cannot improve cannot grow, and growth is what eventually funds the mission | Service waits longer |
| **RI-2** | **After the social contribution, before residual surplus** *(after 7, before 8)* | The mission keeps its position and reinvestment competes only with the founder's upside — the most mission-faithful ordering | Capability investment arrives last and is the first thing skipped in a modest surplus |
| **RI-3** | **Inside priority 3 as a capitalised direct cost** | Requires no amendment at all: an approved capability purchase attributable to a venture is already a direct cost | It only works for venture-attributable capability; shared capability *(the L2 layer)* has no home, and `G-05` expense-shifting becomes easier |
| **Determination** | **`NO POSITION SELECTED.`** The choice is a Tier-1 amendment to an `APPROVED` order, and this instrument does not hold the authority. **RI-3 is noted as the only candidate that requires no amendment, and is therefore the only one available before `SR-01` is decided — but availability is not adoption** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION (TIER-1)` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** *(capitalisation and tax treatment)* | — |

## X.8 The six solvency lines

**[P]** Retained from the corpus's derivation, because they are the model's most important single result and they contain no invented numbers.

| Line | **Condition** | **Meaning** |
|---|---|---|
| **S-1 Worker solvency** | `V_collected ≥ R_1` | Verified workers can be paid in full |
| **S-2 Legal solvency** | `V_collected ≥ R_1 + R_2` | Workers **and** statutory dues can be paid — **the joint tier of §X.5** |
| **S-3 Delivery solvency** | `V_collected ≥ R_1 + R_2 + R_3` | The work can actually be paid for |
| **S-4 Institutional solvency** | `V_collected ≥ R_1 … R_5` | The organisation itself is funded |
| **S-5 Resilience** | `V_collected ≥ R_1 … R_6` | A buffer is being built |
| **S-6 Mission surplus** | `V_collected ≥ R_1 … R_7` | Surplus conditions are met and the mission is funded from surplus |

> ### **`"REVENUE > 0" DOES NOT REACH S-1. A TEAM CAN SIT ABOVE ONE LINE AND BELOW THE NEXT, AND WHICH LINE IT IS ABOVE IS THE ONLY HONEST ANSWER TO "HOW ARE WE DOING?"`**
>
> **The reporting rule that follows: every period reports the highest solvency line reached. Not a score, not a grade, not a percentage — a line (J-18, J-22, MD-14).**

# PART XI — WORKER COMPENSATION ARCHITECTURE

## XI.1 The principle, under test

**[P]** Bound by **RT-02, RT-03, MD-09, MD-10, MD-11**. **The corpus tested the worker-first principle and refused to weaken it. That verdict is adopted.**

| Question | **Answer** | **Source** |
|---|---|---|
| Can worker-first be preserved in a **full** month? | **Yes — trivially** | **[S-B: §12.7]** |
| In a **weak** month? | **Yes for venture workers — but the existence cost goes unpaid and is invisible** | **[S-B: §12.7]** |
| In a **zero** month? | **The principle has no operation.** There is nothing to sequence; survival depends on runway | **[S-B: §12.7]** |
| When payment is **delayed**? | **At risk — and the risk is created by an unresolved accounting policy `[V]`: receipts versus invoiced** | **[S-B: §12.7]**; **RR-1** |
| When a customer **defaults**? | **No. Verified work goes unpaid — *"this is the structural problem"*** | **[S-B: §12.7]** |
| Should the principle be weakened? | **No.** *"It is the architecture's best feature. It needs a companion scarcity rule and a cross-period accrual, not a dilution"* | **[S-B: §12.7]** |
| **The honest structural finding** | ***"Worker-first is a rule about the division of a surplus. A new team has no surplus. The economic-survival question is therefore not 'can we protect workers' share?' but 'can we create a share to protect?'"*** | **[S-B: §12.7]** |

## XI.2 Eligibility

| Rule | **Content** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|
| **EL-1** | **Only approved tasks, projects and venture work are eligible** | **`RETAINED`** — **[S: Ch.19 step 1]** |
| **EL-2** | **Work is eligible when it is *verified*, and verification is performed by a person who is not paid from the work** | **`RETAINED` + `NEW` control** — RT-03, **V.2 COI-2** |
| **EL-3** | **Unpaid work is never recorded as payable work, and payable work is never recorded as unpaid** | **`NEW`** — defeats `G-05` and the "unpaid work recorded as revenue" vector |
| **EL-4** | **Service, governance, infrastructure and training work are not eligible under priority 1** — because priority 1 is compensation *"for verified work"* under a venture. **This is a structural exclusion, not a judgement of worth, and it is the reason `PC-06`, `PC-07` and `PC-08` are unfunded** | **`RETAINED — AS A FINDING`** — **[S-B: §8.4]** |
| **EL-5** | **A person may not be paid from a venture whose approval, verification, recording or audit they also perform** | **`NEW`**, from the four-hand rule |

## XI.3 The formula specification — no values

**[P]** Bound by **EA-9, MD-09**. **The formula does not exist. This is its specification, which is the deliverable, because a rule that does not exist cannot be understood, seen or challenged (`D-1`).**

| Element | **Specification** | **Who determines it** |
|---|---|---|
| **The base** | Verified work value in the period, per person, per venture | **Records — computed, never estimated** |
| **The unit of work** | A task, a deliverable or an hour — **and the choice among them is a decision, because it determines whether long careful work or fast visible work is rewarded** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **The rate mechanism** | A published rate per unit, per role class, per category — **0 rates stated here** | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + National approval + `[V]`** |
| **The leader differential** | Bounded or capped, justified by verified leadership **work**, never a percentage of team revenue *(MLM-2)*, never computed on a base that includes another person's compensation *(MLM-9)* | **`LDR-U02` / `SR-15` — `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`; *"FINAL MULTIPLIER REQUIRES APPROVAL"*** |
| **The eligibility rule** | EL-1 … EL-5 | **This instrument** |
| **The verification route** | Supervisor or customer verification of completed work, recorded | **`RETAINED`** — **[S: Ch.18, Ch.25]** |
| **The publication duty** | The formula is published to members **before** anyone is recruited on it, and every change is published with its effective date | **`D-1`; `NEW` as an economic rule** |
| **The version field** | Every payment sheet carries the formula **version** that produced it | **`RETAINED`** — RT-16 |
| **The cadence** | A variable. **The priority order and the transparency obligation are not variables** | **MD-02; National approval — *"it is a member-facing promise"*** |
| **The shortfall rule** | §XI.5 | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` before use** |
| **The accrual rule** | §XI.6 | **`BLOCKED — SR-01` as to enforceability** |

## XI.4 The leader differential, constrained

| Constraint | **Content** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **LD-1** | **Bounded or capped — never open-ended** | **[S: App. A §15.3]** |
| **LD-2** | **Justified by verified leadership *work*, not by status** | **[S: App. A §15.3]** |
| **LD-3** | **Never a percentage of the team's revenue** | **MLM-2 — and this is the exact point at which the architecture becomes an MLM** |
| **LD-4** | **Never computed on a base that includes another person's compensation** | **MLM-9** |
| **LD-5** | **Not settable by the person it pays** | **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]**; **COI-3** |
| **LD-6** | **Reduced pro rata alongside everyone else in a shortfall, and the role-holder may not exclude themselves and still approve it** | **[S-B: §12.5 B-06 point 2]** |
| **LD-7** | **Disclosed at recruitment as the weak-month reality, not the good-month possibility** | **`D-2`** |
| **LD-8** | **Not applicable at Mohalla level** | **`RETAINED`** — **[S-B: §8.7, §211]** |

## XI.5 The shortfall rule

**[P]** Bound by **MD-10**. **Retained in structure, gated on labour law.**

| Step | **Rule** |
|---|---|
| **SS-0** *(new, first)* | **Before any pro-rata reduction is applied, confirm that partial payment is lawful.** If a minimum-wage or timely-payment obligation makes partial payment unlawful outright, **the correct action is not reduction but suspension of the work** — and the suspension is recorded, published and escalated in the same period |
| **SS-1** | All verified workers in the period share the available allocation **pro rata to verified work value** — not the earliest, not the loudest, not the most senior |
| **SS-2** | **No role-holder may exclude themselves from the reduction and still approve it.** The Leader and the Funds Manager take the same proportional reduction |
| **SS-3** | The reduction, its cause and the accrued balance are **published to the team in the same period** |
| **SS-4** | The accrued balance is carried per §XI.6 and settled at priority 1 from the next collection |
| **SS-5** | **The rule is triggered by arithmetic, not by discretion** — it applies automatically whenever the collection is below the joint tier, and its application is recorded |
| **SS-6** | **Statutory dues are never reduced by this rule** |
| **Label** | **`MODIFIED FROM HISTORICAL BLUEPRINT`** *(the source proposal is `B-06`)* **+ `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** *(SS-0 is new and is the gate on the whole rule)* |

## XI.6 The accrual

| Rule | **Content** | **State** |
|---|---|---|
| **AC-1** | An earned claim that cannot be paid is **recorded, aged, visible to the person it belongs to, and settled at priority 1 from the next collection** | **`RETAINED`** — `D-5`, `B-05` |
| **AC-2** | The accrual record carries the formula version that produced it, so that a later change of formula cannot rewrite history | **`NEW`**, from RT-16 |
| **AC-3** | **Until a legal person exists, an accrual is a moral record and not a debt — and it must be described to the person as exactly that.** Recording a promise the architecture cannot keep is the failure `D-2` exists to prevent | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`**; **`BLOCKED — SR-01`** as to enforceability |
| **AC-4** | An accrual may not be extinguished by a bad month, by a departure, by a role change or by silence | **`RETAINED`** — **[S-B: §12.4]** |
| **AC-5** | Accrual ageing is a standing detection field: an accrual that never settles is a signal, not a statistic | **`NEW`**, from **[S-B: §27.5]** |

## XI.7 The three absolute rules and the dignity floor

**[P]** Adopted verbatim in substance from the corpus.

| # | **Absolute rule** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **A member is never expected to fund operations personally.** Personal spending creates debt-based loyalty | **[S-B: §12.6]**; `D-3`; `HC-03` |
| **2** | **Compensation is never recruited on before it is collectable.** What is said at recruitment must be what the waterfall can deliver in the **weak** month | **[S-B: §12.6]**; `D-2` |
| **3** | **The cadence may change; the priority order and the transparency may not** | **[S-B: §12.6]**; MD-02 |

| # | **Dignity requirement** | **Instrument** |
|---|---|---|
| **D-1** | The compensation formula exists in writing **before** the first person is recruited on it | §XI.3 |
| **D-2** | Payment expectations disclosed at recruitment are the weak-month reality | Rule 2 above |
| **D-3** | No member funds operations personally, ever | Rule 1 above; **X-8** |
| **D-4** | **Unpaid work is optional, bounded, disclosed and never a condition of anything** | §XI.8 |
| **D-5** | Accrued unpaid compensation is recorded, aged, visible and settled at priority 1 | §XI.6 |
| **D-6** | The organisation measures what volunteering costs it, and reports it | §V.3 MW-5 |
| **D-7** | Control roles are supported, backed and rotated — not left to absorb social friction alone | **V.2; Part XX** |
| **D-8** | **There is a stated point at which a role ceases to be volunteerable** — the four segregated finance hands and the safety role carry legal exposure | **V.2; `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` + `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** |
| **D-9** | A person can see a path from volunteering to earning that does not depend on their neighbourhood being wealthy | **Part VII two-track; RT-21 City pipeline** |
| **D-10** | The dignity of the person helped is never traded for revenue or evidence | **Part VI.4, VI.5** |

## XI.8 The volunteer boundary

| Rule | **Content** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|
| **VB-1** | **Unpaid work is optional, bounded, disclosed, and never a condition of membership, advancement, role or recognition** | **`RETAINED`** — `D-4`, `MLM-5` |
| **VB-2** | **A volunteer may decline any commercial assignment without consequence, and the decline is not recorded as a performance fact** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`** — required by task §21 |
| **VB-3** | **Volunteer labour is never supplied to a third party for money** | **`RETAINED`** — `NM-10` |
| **VB-4** | **The cost of volunteering is measured and reported, because *"they are volunteers" must never be a reason to ignore cost*** | **`RETAINED`** — `D-6`, **[S-B: §15.2]** |
| **VB-5** | **The paid/volunteer boundary is published per role class, and the four `D-8` roles are the first to cross it** | **`RETAINED`** — **[S-B: §16.3]**, `B-09` |
| **VB-6** | **Converting a volunteer role into a paid role is a decision with a named decider and a funding source, never an accommodation** | **`NEW`**; **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |

## XI.9 What compensation is not

**Recognition is not compensation. Ranking is not compensation. Endorsement is not compensation. A role is not compensation. Access is not compensation. Credit for an introduction is not compensation.** *"Attribution of an introduction may be recorded for thanks, never for value"* **[S-C: §22.3 MLM-6]** — and the same rule governs every non-cash good the organisation can produce, because a non-cash good that can be traded for work is a currency, and an uncontrolled currency is where a recruitment economy restarts.
