# PART XII — ORGANISATION FUNDING ARCHITECTURE

## XII.1 What has to be funded

**[P]** Four cost layers, and the fact that only one of them is currently funded is the whole problem.

| Layer | **What it contains** | **Funded by** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L1 — Direct work** | Verified task/project/venture delivery | **Venture revenue, priority 1** | **`FUNDED IN PRINCIPLE — BLOCKED IN PRACTICE, A-N16`** |
| **L2 — Coordination and shared capability** | Team Leader, planning, HR and verification, safety, audit, warehouse, editing, social media, training | **Deferrable organisation service fee, priority 5 — a percentage of gross venture revenue** | **`STRUCTURALLY UNFUNDED AT ZERO VOLUME`** — **[S-B: §8.4]** |
| **L3 — Venture infrastructure** | Tools, materials, software, equipment, workspace, licences | **Priority 3 direct costs — but only where a venture already generates revenue** | **`UNFUNDED BEFORE FIRST REVENUE`** |
| **L4 — Social mission** | Direct service delivery, beneficiary support, community programmes | **Social fund, priority 7, only after surplus conditions are met** | **`UNFUNDED UNTIL S-6`** |

> ### **`THE MAP IS NOT AN OVERHEAD PROBLEM. IT IS A SEQUENCING PROBLEM: EVERY LAYER THAT MAKES REVENUE POSSIBLE SITS BEHIND THE REVENUE IT IS SUPPOSED TO MAKE POSSIBLE.`**
>
> **And the sharpest version of it: *"The centre is funded last and spent first. It runs from day 1; its income starts at 0 and its only source is a deferrable percentage of gross venture revenue. No revenue, no centre income — and the centre must still function"*** **[S-B: §8.4]**.
>
> **In the current architecture there is no centre at all — which is simultaneously worse *(nothing performs the L2 work)* and better *(no L2 payroll exists to fund)*. The 11 roles are the L2 work, distributed across individuals who are also doing L1.**

## XII.2 The funding map — the required deliverable

**[P]** Bound by **AL-4, `B-26`**. **Task §6 requires a statement of whether each economic layer is self-funded or centrally funded, and the corpus names this as *"the single most important unresolved economic question in v1.3-F"*. No cell is left blank. `SELF` = funded from the venture's own revenue · `CENTRAL` = funded from an organisation-level source · `NONE` = no source exists in the current architecture.**

| # | **Role / function** | **Class** | **Creates revenue?** | **Funding source** | **Mode** | **Verdict** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **AI & Business Development Manager** | **`ECONOMIC — VALUE-CREATING`** | **Yes — originates ventures** | Venture revenue · priority 3 where venture-attributable, priority 5 otherwise | **`SELF, PARTLY`** | **The only role whose cost is arguably self-funding — and only after the venture is already working, which is the circularity** |
| **2** | **Ground-Level Manager / Team Leader** | **`ECONOMIC — VALUE-CREATING + DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`** | **Yes, indirectly** | Priority 1 for verified delivery work · priority 5 for leadership work | **`MIXED — MUST BE SPLIT`** | **The split is mandatory: MW-1 … MW-6. A leader paid wholly from priority 5 is unfunded; paid wholly from priority 1 is a worker** |
| **3** | **Planner** | **`ECONOMIC — VALUE-CREATING + DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`** | **Partly** | Priority 3 where a plan is a direct cost of an approved venture · priority 5 otherwise | **`MIXED`** | **Same split obligation** |
| **4** | **Editor** | **`ECONOMIC — VALUE-CREATING`** | **Yes — category 3/5 deliverable** | Priority 1 for verified delivery work | **`SELF`** | **Funded only when the deliverable is sold. Standing editorial capacity is `NONE`** |
| **5** | **Warehouse Manager** | **`DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`, category 7 only** | **Only under category 7** | Priority 3 where a handling charge is an approved direct cost | **`SELF UNDER CATEGORY 7 · NONE OTHERWISE`** | **Category 7 is blocked at Stage 2, so today this is `NONE`** |
| **6** | **HR & Verification Manager** | **`DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`** | **No** | Priority 5 | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **`PC-07`. And it may not be paid from what it verifies — COI-2** |
| **7** | **Funds Manager** | **`DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`** | **No** | Priority 5 | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **`PC-07`. Segregated: may not participate in any venture — COI-1** |
| **8** | **Social Media Manager** | **`DELIVERY-SUPPORTING`** | **No — it produces attention, not receipts** | Priority 5 | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **`PC-07`. Real cost, no funded source, no revenue link** |
| **9** | **Safety / Protection Lead** | **`MISSION-ONLY`** | **No — and may not** | Priority 5 in principle | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **`NM-3` prohibits participation in any revenue activity. An unfunded mandatory protection function — the worst combination in the map** |
| **10** | **Volunteer Manager** | **`MISSION-ONLY`** | **No — and may not** | Priority 5 in principle | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **`NM-10`. Same finding** |
| **11** | **Internal Auditor** | **`MISSION-ONLY / CONTROL`** | **No — and may not** | Priority 5 in principle | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **Independence is the requirement; funding is the threat to it — COI-2** |
| **12** | **L3 venture infrastructure** | **`ENABLING`** | **Indirectly** | Priority 3 | **`SELF, AFTER THE FACT`** | **`UNFUNDED BEFORE FIRST REVENUE` — Part XVII** |
| **13** | **L4 social mission** | **`MISSION`** | **No** | Priority 7 · plus Part XIII's separate instrument | **`CONDITIONAL`** | **`UNFUNDED UNTIL S-6` — Part XIII** |
| **14** | **Governance, amendment, validation, audit programme** | **`ENABLING`** | **No** | **No line exists** | **`NONE`** | **The cost of keeping the architecture true has no funding source at all** |

### **The map's findings**

| # | **Finding** |
|---|---|
| **FM-1** | **8 of 14 rows have no funding source in practice.** This is not a percentage problem; it is a source problem |
| **FM-2** | **The 4 roles that may not participate in paid ventures at all — Funds Manager, HR & Verification, Safety/Protection, Volunteer Manager — are precisely the roles with no funding source.** The architecture prohibits them from earning and does not fund them |
| **FM-3** | **3 rows are `MIXED` and their split is mandatory, not optional.** Without MW-1 … MW-6 recording, the map cannot be completed honestly |
| **FM-4** | **Row 14 has no source and no proposal.** Governance is the only function whose absence cannot be detected by the organisation itself |
| **FM-5** | **No cell in this map may be filled with a number, and none is** |

## XII.3 The three routes the corpus identified, and their state

**[P]** **[S-B: §8.4 C_L1]** — the corpus already framed the choice. **It is reproduced because the architecture cannot proceed without it, and it is not resolved here.**

| Route | **Content** | **Consequence** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C_L1-a** | **The organisation is funded from a non-commerce source** — founder contribution, validated donation, grant, resourcing | Mission work stops being contingent on commerce; **but it creates a funder, and a funder is an influence** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** — and any founder contribution triggers **`AG-10`** contribution capture and Part XVI |
| **C_L1-b** | **The organisation is funded from commerce** — the priority-5 service fee | No external funder; **but the centre has zero income until a venture works, and every control function is unfunded until then** | **`THE CURRENT DEFAULT — AND IT IS THE STATE THAT PRODUCES FM-1 … FM-4`** |
| **C_L1-c** | **The organisation's functions are not funded but *distributed*** — every role-holder performs L2 work as part of an unpaid or L1-funded contribution | No centre cost; **but the L2 work is then performed by whoever is available, which destroys segregation of duties and independence** | **`THE DE FACTO CURRENT STATE — AND IT IS A CONTROL FAILURE, NOT A FUNDING MODEL`** |

> ### **`H-Q17 — WHICH OF C_L1-a, C_L1-b OR C_L1-c FUNDS THE L2 LAYER?`**
>
> **`UNRESOLVED`.** Not resolvable by this instrument: **(a)** requires a funder that does not exist and a governance decision on influence; **(b)** requires revenue that does not exist; **(c)** requires accepting that the Funds Manager, the Verifier and the Auditor are the same people as the workers, which defeats COI-1 and COI-2. **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` + `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`.**

## XII.4 Self-funded or centrally funded — the determination

**[P]** Task §6's question, answered layer by layer.

| Layer | **Determination** | **Reason** |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** | **`SELF-FUNDED`** | It is the only layer with a source, and priority 1 is indivisible with priority 2 |
| **L2** | **`UNDETERMINED — H-Q17`** | The architecture names a deferrable percentage of gross venture revenue, which is neither self-funding at zero volume nor central because no centre exists |
| **L3** | **`SELF-FUNDED, AFTER THE FACT`** | Priority 3 is an allocation of costs already incurred, not a source of funds for costs about to be incurred — **X.3 determinant 4** |
| **L4** | **`NEITHER — A SEPARATE INSTRUMENT IS REQUIRED`** | Priority 7 alone cannot fund a mission that must operate before any surplus exists — Part XIII |

## XII.5 The unfunded-role problem, and the honest options

**[P]** No option is selected. **Each is stated with its cost, because an architecture that names an obligation and funds nothing is a document that will be quietly disobeyed.**

| Option | **What it does** | **What it costs** | **Label** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **O-1 Reduce the number of mandated roles** | Makes the map fundable | Loses segregation of duties, independence and the protection function — **and `NM-3`, COI-1 and COI-2 are not negotiable** | **`REJECTED — the controls are the point`** |
| **O-2 Accept that mandated roles are unpaid, and say so** | Removes the false promise; satisfies `D-2` | `D-8`: a role carrying legal exposure cannot be volunteerable indefinitely, and unpaid control roles are the ones that fail first | **`AVAILABLE SHORT-TERM ONLY — WITH DISCLOSURE`** |
| **O-3 Fund L2 from a non-commerce source** | Solves FM-1 … FM-4 | Creates a funder; triggers `AG-10`, Part XVI and the influence question | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` — C_L1-a** |
| **O-4 Fund L2 by paying every role-holder from L1 work** | No new source needed | Converts control roles into delivery roles and destroys independence — **this is C_L1-c, a control failure** | **`REJECTED`** |
| **O-5 Sequence the roles: fund the protection and verification functions first, from whatever exists** | Protects the non-negotiable controls | Requires a source, and there is none | **`CORRECT IN PRINCIPLE — `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` ON THE SOURCE`** |
| **O-6 Do nothing and let the first revenue decide** | No decision now | The first revenue will be spent on the loudest claim, and the map will be filled in retrospectively — the exact failure `AL-1` and `AL-2` were rejected for | **`REJECTED — EA-24 forbids silent resolution`** |

# PART XIII — SOCIAL-SERVICE FUNDING ARCHITECTURE

## XIII.1 The principle

**[P]** Bound by **DO-1, DO-2, OB-3, X-1 … X-8**. **The mission is not funded from commerce's leftovers alone, and commerce is never funded from the mission's money at all. The two directions are not symmetric and are not treated as symmetric.**

| Direction | **Rule** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **Commerce → mission** | **Permitted, at priority 7, when surplus conditions are met, through the social fund** | **RT-13; [appendix.txt:6969–6979]** |
| **Mission → commerce** | **`PROHIBITED ABSOLUTELY`** | **X-1 … X-4** |
| **Mission → any commercial claim** | **`PROHIBITED`** — no beneficiary money, no donor-restricted money used for venture cost | **X-5, X-6; `B-27` rule 1** |
| **Mission benefit → commercial advantage** | **`PROHIBITED`** — help given may never be conditioned on, or exchanged for, revenue, evidence, endorsement or access | **`D-10`; `NM-4 … NM-8`** |

## XIII.2 The existing routes and their limits

| Route | **Mechanism** | **Limit** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R-A** | **Social fund at priority 7, "when surplus conditions are met"** | Requires `S-6`, which requires seven prior claims to be satisfied | **`RETAINED — AND INSUFFICIENT ALONE`** |
| **R-B** | **Validated donation, restricted by donor intent** | *"Donations may be possible **after validation**"* — validation has not occurred, and restricted money carries a legal obligation to honour the restriction | **`CONDITIONAL — `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** on receipt, restriction, storage, allocation and reporting** |
| **R-C** | **Non-cash resourcing from City or State** — donated or purchased materials with approved evidence and priorities | Non-cash only; no City or State layer exists; materials cannot pay a protection lead | **`BLOCKED — NO LAYER EXISTS`** |
| **R-D** | **Founder or member contribution** | **Prohibited as a *requirement* (`D-3`, `HC-03`)**; permitted only as a fully disclosed, capped, governed contribution — which triggers `AG-10` and Part XVI | **`PROHIBITED AS REQUIREMENT · `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` AS VOLUNTARY CONTRIBUTION`** |

## XIII.3 The pre-surplus mission instrument — `MI-1`

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`. **Designed as a structure, not a percentage, because a percentage would be an invented number and because the instrument must work at zero surplus.**

| Element | **Design** |
|---|---|
| **Its identifier** | **`MI-1` — the pre-surplus mission instrument** |
| **What it is** | **A named, restricted, non-commerce instrument that funds a defined minimum of mission activity *before* any surplus exists, so that the mission does not wait for `S-6`** |
| **What it is not** | **Not a percentage of revenue · not a claim in the commerce waterfall · not a reserve · not a donor account · not a founder account** |
| **Why it is separate** | Because mixing it into the waterfall would either subordinate the mission to commerce *(priority 7)* or subordinate workers to the mission *(an upward reorder, which X.3 determinant 6 rejects)*. **Both are wrong, and separation is the only structure that avoids both** |
| **Permitted sources** | **Restricted donations honoured to their restriction · non-cash resourcing with approved evidence · a governed voluntary contribution under Part XVI · nothing from flow A** |
| **Prohibited sources** | **Venture revenue · worker compensation · reserves · the wage-protection buffer · any money whose restriction does not permit mission use** |
| **Permitted uses** | **Direct service delivery · beneficiary support · the Safety/Protection function · the Volunteer function · programme materials** |
| **Prohibited uses** | **Any commercial cost · any venture's direct cost · any compensation under priority 1 · any related-party payment · any reserve top-up** |
| **Authority** | **Who proposes, who approves, who signs, who receives, who authorises, who verifies and who audits are all `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`. The one thing that is determined: the person who authorises a draw may not be the person who receives or benefits from it — COI-1 … COI-4** |
| **Record** | **Every unit of mission money carries WHO → WHAT → WHEN → WHY → AMOUNT → AUTHORITY → EVIDENCE → STATUS, and its restriction is a field, not a footnote** |
| **Failure mode** | **If the instrument is empty, mission activity does not stop silently — the shortfall is escalated under Part XVIII `F-14` and published** |
| **State** | **`PROPOSED — CONTAINS NO NUMBERS — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION ON SOURCE, AUTHORITY AND RESTRICTION — REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION ON RESTRICTED-FUND HANDLING`** |

## XIII.4 The beneficiary-protection gate

**[P]** Adopted from `B-27`. **These are not guidelines; they are conditions on any mission money touching any commercial activity.**

| # | **Rule** |
|---|---|
| **BP-1** | **No beneficiary may ever be charged, invoiced, solicited, upsold or converted into a customer because they received help** |
| **BP-2** | **No beneficiary's data, testimony, image, story or outcome may be sold, licensed, monetised or used as commercial evidence without a separate, informed, revocable consent that is not a condition of service** |
| **BP-3** | **No commercial venture may use mission access, mission trust or mission premises as its acquisition channel** |
| **BP-4** | **No mission role-holder may hold a commercial interest in a venture serving the population they serve** |
| **BP-5** | **A breach of BP-1 … BP-4 is not a performance matter — it is a governance breach and it stops the activity** |

# PART XIV — RESERVE ARCHITECTURE

## XIV.1 What a reserve is

**[P]** Bound by **RT-12, MD-05, X.3 determinant 9**. **A reserve is money held *before* a shock, for a *named* shock, under a *named* authority. Anything else is a surplus that has not been spent yet.**

| Property | **Requirement** |
|---|---|
| **Named purpose** | Each pool has one stated purpose. A general "reserves" line with no purpose is not a control |
| **Named authority** | Who proposes, who approves, who signs the draw, who verifies the return — **all `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **Named trigger** | A draw requires a stated condition. Discretion at the moment of need is how reserves disappear |
| **Named return** | A draw is either a transfer or a loan to the pool. **If it is a loan, its repayment ranks where the architecture says it ranks — and that position is not currently stated → `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION (TIER-1)`** |
| **Segregation** | Held separately from operating money; no officer's personal account; no commingling — **X-7, X-8** |
| **Visibility** | The balance and every draw are visible to the members whose protection it exists for |
| **Audit** | Every draw is auditable against its trigger, its authority and its return |

## XIV.2 The five pools

**[P]** Retained from the corpus's five-fund structure **[S-B: §21.1–21.2]** and mapped to the four flows and pools of Part VI.

| Pool | **Purpose** | **Source** | **Draw authority** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P-1 Reserve / wage-protection buffer** | Protect verified worker compensation in a weak period | **Priority 6** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | **`RETAINED — UNFUNDED AT START`** |
| **P-2 Emergency** | A named shock: loss of a major customer, a legal demand, a safety incident, a custody failure | **Priority 6 and/or P-1 by rule** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | **`RETAINED — UNDEFINED TRIGGERS`** |
| **P-3 Founder** | The founder's approved, capped compensation and any approved upside | **Priority 4 and priority 8** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)`** | **`RETAINED — BLOCKED BY CF-16`** |
| **P-4 Reinvestment** | Approved capability: tools, training, systems, market development | **No line exists — §X.7** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION (TIER-1)`** | **`NEW POOL — NO SOURCE`** |
| **P-5 Surplus** | Residual after every prior claim | **Priority 9** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | **`RETAINED`** |
| **P-6 Seeded first-season float** *(new)* | The working capital that priority 6 cannot provide before the first collection | **An external source, named, capped, termed** | **Named at the seed; reconciled in full** | **`NEW — PROPOSED — `DOES NOT EXIST IN SOURCE`** |

## XIV.3 The wage-protection buffer, defined without numbers

| Element | **Definition** |
|---|---|
| **What it protects** | **The joint tier of §X.5 — verified worker compensation and mandatory dues** |
| **What it does not protect** | Direct costs, the organisation service fee, the social contribution, founder compensation, reinvestment |
| **How its adequacy is expressed** | **In periods of coverage of the joint tier, not in rupees.** A statement of the form *"the buffer covers N periods of the joint tier at the most recent verified run-rate"* contains one integer and no money figure, and it is the only adequacy measure that survives a change of scale |
| **Where N comes from** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`. Not invented here** |
| **How it is built** | **Priority 6, and by MD-05's float for the first season only** |
| **How it is drawn** | **On a stated trigger, by a stated authority, with a stated return** |
| **What happens when it is empty** | **The shortfall rule §XI.5 applies — subject to SS-0's labour-law gate — and the emptiness is escalated under `F-07`** |

## XIV.4 The cold-start finding

> ### **`A RESERVE CANNOT FUND THE FIRST WEAK MONTH, BECAUSE A RESERVE IS BUILT FROM THE MONTHS THAT CAME BEFORE IT.`**
>
> **Priority 6 sits behind four prior claims. In a standing-start team with no float, no centre income and no donor, the first weak month arrives before the first rupee of reserve exists. This is not a defect in the priority order — the order is correct — it is a defect in the *starting conditions*, and the only architectural responses are:**
>
> **1. `P-6`, a seeded float from a named external source, capped, termed, reconciled and never renewed more than once — `MD-05`, `NEW`, `PROPOSED`;**
> **2. A pre-approval gate that refuses to authorise a venture whose direct costs cannot be met from a collection whose timing is known — `HG-4`, Part XVIII `F-02`;**
> **3. An honest statement to every participant that at zero collection the operative control is runway, not priority — X.1.**
>
> **All three contain no numbers. All three are `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` or `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` on their parameters.**

# PART XV — FOUNDER AND KRYTOS ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE

## XV.1 What this Part is, and what it is not

**[P]** Bound by **EA-15, EA-17, EA-19, EA-20, EA-26**. **This Part is the architecture of a question. It is not an answer, not a structure, not a valuation and not a permission.**

> ### **`NO TRANSFER OF MONEY, VALUE, EQUITY, IP, REVENUE OR CONTROL BETWEEN KRYTOS, SEWAK SATHI SANSTHA, THE FOUNDER, ANY VENTURE AND ANY WORKER IS LEGALLY PERMISSIBLE TODAY.`**
>
> **Not because it is prohibited as a matter of policy — but because there is no legal person on at least one side of every one of those pairings, and a transfer requires two persons capable of holding it. `SR-01` is unresolved. `A-N16` is blocked. The five forbidden assumptions remain forbidden.**

## XV.2 The seven founder facts, held as assertions

**[P]** Task §3 requires these to be treated as assertions and never as legal conclusions. **Each is recorded with its status and the conclusion it may not support.**

| # | **The assertion** | **What it is** | **What it is NOT** | **What it may not be used to conclude** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **FA-1** | **Krytos is the Virtual Brain** | **A functional/architectural description of an intended role** | **Not a legal person, not a corporate name, not a registered entity** | That anything can own, contract, employ, hold money or be sued under it |
| **FA-2** | **Krytos is the ecosystem head** | **An intended position in an intended hierarchy** | **Not a legally recognised parent, not a holding relationship** | That Sewak Sathi is a subsidiary, or that any direction from Krytos carries legal authority |
| **FA-3** | **India jurisdiction is intended** | **A stated intention as to future forum** | **Not a completed registration, not a choice of law already made, not an address** | That Indian law applies to an entity that does not exist, or that any filing has been made |
| **FA-4** | **No constitutive document exists** | **A statement of absence — and the most consequential fact in this Part** | **Not a formality awaiting completion; it is the absence of the thing every other fact depends on** | Anything. **An entity with no constitutive document has no capacity, no governance, no ownership and no liability boundary** |
| **FA-5** | **No legal work has been done** | **A statement of absence** | **Not "legal work is unnecessary"** | That any structure is compliant, permitted, or safe |
| **FA-6** | **Vasu Gupta will own and control Krytos** | **A forward-looking intention, stated in the future tense** | **Not present ownership, not a shareholding, not a membership, not a trust deed, not a directorship** | That the founder is a shareholder, that any upside is distributable to him, or that any Krytos asset is his |
| **FA-7** | **The current architecture is being designed now** | **A statement about this instrument** | **Not authority to make legal determinations** | That designing it constitutes deciding it, or that a design is an approval |

## XV.3 The eight separations

**[P]** Task §13 requires eight things to be separated. **They are separated here because every one of them is currently collapsed in ordinary usage, and each collapse would produce a specific illegality or a specific injustice.**

| # | **Separation** | **Why it must be held apart** | **The collapse it prevents** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SEP-1** | **The founder's *person* from the founder's *roles*** | One human being is simultaneously the founder of Sewak Sathi, the intended owner of Krytos, an intended counterparty, an intended recipient of priority-4 compensation and the person who would approve it | Self-dealing; **`CF-16` — a conflict *"that cannot be managed"*; `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)`** | **`SEPARATED — NO INSTRUMENT EXISTS TO ENFORCE IT`** |
| **SEP-2** | **Krytos from Sewak Sathi Sanstha** | Two intended things with no established legal relationship between them | Treating a description of an ecosystem as a group structure — **forbidden assumption 1, 2 and 3** | **`SEPARATED — RELATIONSHIP `UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED`` · `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE`** |
| **SEP-3** | **Ownership from control** | "Own and control" is one phrase describing two different legal facts with two different documents | Treating an assertion of future control as present ownership — **forbidden assumption 4** | **`SEPARATED — NEITHER EXISTS IN DOCUMENT`** |
| **SEP-4** | **Compensation for work from return on capital** | Priority 4 is payment for work performed; priority 9 is a residual claim on an enterprise. **Different legal characters, different tax characters, different approvals, different evidence** | Paying a dividend and calling it a fee, or paying a fee and calling it a dividend | **`SEPARATED IN THE WATERFALL — TAX TREATING `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION``** |
| **SEP-5** | **Contribution *in* from extraction *out*** | A founder who funds the organisation and a founder who is funded by it are on opposite sides of every control in this architecture | Netting the two, which is how `AG-10` capture happens invisibly | **`SEPARATED — `AG-10` APPLIES TO EVERY CONTRIBUTION IN`** |
| **SEP-6** | **Value created by the founder's work from value created by members' work** | The architecture's entire compensation logic is *verified work*. A founder claim not traceable to verified work is a claim on other people's labour | Founder upside computed from member-generated revenue — **which is `MLM-2` inverted, and is prohibited at the leader level by the same reasoning** | **`SEPARATED — ATTRIBUTION MECHANISM DOES NOT EXIST`** |
| **SEP-7** | **The Virtual Brain / ecosystem concept from a legal person** | An architectural function can be performed by an arrangement; a legal person must be created | Treating FA-1 and FA-2 as evidence of existence — **Rule K-4, `ASSERTION ≠ DOCUMENT`** | **`SEPARATED BY RULE K-4`** |
| **SEP-8** | **What is architecturally desirable from what is legally permissible** | This instrument can design a structure. **It cannot make one lawful** | A design document functioning as a legal opinion — **EA-26, §19 of the delta-audit brief** | **`SEPARATED — THE STANDING RULE OF THIS INSTRUMENT`** |

## XV.4 The transfer matrix — what must be true before money may move

**[P]** Task §13's requirement: name what must be resolved before money flows **Krytos ↔ Sewak Sathi ↔ founder ↔ ventures ↔ workers**. **Every cell is a precondition list. No cell is satisfied.**

| Pairing | **Direction** | **Preconditions before any movement** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sewak Sathi → workers** | Priority 1 | **`SR-01`** a legal person capable of being an obligor · a bank mandate (**`A-N16`**) · worker classification determined **`[V]`** · the formula published (**`D-1`**) · verification independent (**COI-2**) | **`BLOCKED — A-N16, SR-01`** |
| **Sewak Sathi → founder** | Priority 4 | All of the above **plus**: the approving authority resolved with beneficiary recusal **`CF-16`** · a cap approved **`[M]`** · verified work evidenced · **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** · the position-4 ordering question at §X.6 addressed | **`BLOCKED — AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** |
| **Founder → Sewak Sathi** | Contribution | **`AG-10`** capture: amount, purpose, restriction, whether it creates any claim, and full disclosure · **no member may be required to contribute (`D-3`)** · `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` on whether a contribution creates debt, capital or a gift | **`PERMITTED ONLY AS A GOVERNED, DISCLOSED, VOLUNTARY CONTRIBUTION — NO MECHANISM EXISTS`** |
| **Sewak Sathi → Krytos** | Any | **Both sides must be legal persons.** Krytos has no constitutive document (**FA-4**). **There is no counterparty** | **`IMPOSSIBLE — NO COUNTERPARTY`** · `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE` |
| **Krytos → Sewak Sathi** | Any | Same | **`IMPOSSIBLE — NO COUNTERPARTY`** · `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE` |
| **Sewak Sathi → ventures** | Capital, float, tooling | A venture must be capable of holding anything. Ventures are activity categories under Part VII, **not entities** — and creating an entity per venture is prohibited (**EA-26: no entity selection**) | **`NOT APPLICABLE — VENTURES ARE NOT PERSONS`** |
| **Ventures → Sewak Sathi** | Revenue, priority 5 | `SR-01` · `A-N16` · an approved category · a customer acceptance · a reconciliation | **`BLOCKED — A-N16, SR-01`** |
| **Founder → Krytos** | Capital, IP, effort | **FA-6 is a future intention.** No share, no deed, no assignment, no valuation | **`NO INSTRUMENT EXISTS`** |
| **Krytos → founder** | Upside, dividend, fee | An entity, an ownership document, a distributable surplus, a tax characterisation and an authority — **none exist** | **`IMPOSSIBLE`** |
| **Founder → workers** | Any | Would bypass every control in this architecture and create a personal dependency relationship with members | **`PROHIBITED — `D-3`, X-8`** |

> ### **`TEN PAIRINGS EXAMINED. ZERO PERMITTED TODAY. TWO ARE NOT MERELY UNAPPROVED — THEY ARE IMPOSSIBLE, BECAUSE ONE SIDE DOES NOT EXIST.`**

## XV.5 Founder upside — the architecture of the question

**[P]** The task asks whether the architecture can *"potentially generate founder upside"*. **The answer is structural, and it is a conditional yes on architecture and an unconditional no on law.**

| Layer | **Question** | **Answer** |
|---|---|---|
| **Architectural** | **Is there a place in the design where a founder claim could sit?** | **`YES — TWO.`** Priority 4 *(capped compensation for verified work)* and priority 9 *(residual surplus / approved upside)*. **Both are already in the approved order. No new mechanism is required and none is created** |
| **Definitional** | **What would have to be true for priority 9 to be a *return* rather than a *fee*?** | An enterprise with equity, distributable profits and an owner. **None exists. Until it does, priority 9 is a residual with no entitled recipient** |
| **Governance** | **Who decides?** | **Nobody currently can.** `CF-16` records a conflict that *"cannot be managed"* by the founder alone; the authority is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)`; a Tier-1 amendment plus National approval plus professional validation would be required. **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` + `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **Constraint** | **What is prohibited regardless of every answer above?** | **Leader equity, profit share and ownership stakes are prohibited** **[S-D:2999]** · compensation from any count of people is prohibited **`MLM-1`, J-12** · a percentage of team revenue is prohibited **`MLM-2`, J-09** · a member funding operations is prohibited **`D-3`** |
| **Ethical** | **Is founder upside consistent with the mission?** | **Not a question this instrument answers.** It is recorded as a **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** with one architectural observation: **any founder claim that ranks ahead of the funding of the control system creates a structural interest in underfunding it — §X.6** |
| **Legal** | **Is any of it permissible now?** | **`NO. DECLARED UNCONDITIONALLY.`** |

## XV.6 Krytos economics — what can and cannot be designed

| Item | **Can this instrument design it?** | **Why / why not** |
|---|---|---|
| **A Krytos business model** | **`NO`** | There is no constitutive document, no capacity, no governance and no jurisdiction. A model for a non-person is fiction |
| **A Krytos–Sewak Sathi relationship** | **`NO`** | Forbidden assumptions 1–3; relationship is `UNKNOWN — DECISION REQUIRED` |
| **A Krytos revenue line** | **`NO`** | Would require one of the seven approved categories to be operated by an entity that does not exist |
| **The *sequence* of questions that must be answered before any of the above** | **`YES — AND THIS IS THE DELIVERABLE`** | **Q-1 Does a Krytos legal person exist? → Q-2 If not, is one intended, and in what form? → Q-3 Who will own it, on what document? → Q-4 What is its relationship to Sewak Sathi? → Q-5 Which of the four flows, if any, may cross that relationship? → Q-6 Who approves a crossing, with recusal? → Q-7 What is the tax character of each crossing? → Q-8 What record evidences each crossing? `EVERY ONE REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION; Q-2 AND Q-3 ALSO REQUIRE FOUNDER DECISION`** |
| **A declaration that no transfer is permissible** | **`YES — AND IT IS MADE AT §XV.1`** | It is the only statement about Krytos economics that the current corpus supports |

# PART XVI — RELATED-PARTY TRANSACTION ARCHITECTURE

## XVI.1 Definition

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE` in economic form. **The corpus has no general related-party register: what it has is `CF-16` (a conflict that cannot be managed), `G-04` (customer collusion), `G-01` (circular or related-party transactions as a form of fake revenue) and the related-party exclusion in its scoring controls. This Part generalises those four into a register, and §XIX.4 records the mapping.** **A related-party transaction is any movement of money, goods, services, IP, access or benefit between the organisation and a person who is in a position to influence the decision that authorises it — or who is connected to such a person.**

| Test | **A transaction is related-party if…** |
|---|---|
| **RP-T1** | **The counterparty is the founder, a role-holder, a coordinator, a member, or a relative or associate of any of them** |
| **RP-T2** | **The counterparty is a venture in which any of the above holds an interest, however small** |
| **RP-T3** | **The counterparty is Krytos, or anything connected to it** — which today means every Krytos pairing at §XV.4 |
| **RP-T4** | **The transaction is funded, in whole or in part, by a contribution from a person who benefits from it** — **`AG-10`** |
| **RP-T5** | **The person who proposes, approves, signs, receives, authorises, verifies or audits it is on both sides** |

## XVI.2 The controls

| # | **Control** | **Origin** |
|---|---|---|
| **RP-1** | **Every related-party transaction is entered in a register before it occurs, not after** | **`NEW`**, generalising `G-04` and `G-01`'s related-party limb |
| **RP-2** | **The interested person recuses from proposal, approval, signature, authorisation, verification and audit.** Recusal is recorded, not assumed | **`RETAINED`** — **beneficiary recusal; `CF-16`** |
| **RP-3** | **The transaction must be on terms demonstrably no more favourable than those available to an unrelated party — and *"demonstrably" requires a comparable, which requires a market, which requires a record** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — arms-length testing is an accounting and tax discipline |
| **RP-4** | **Full disclosure to the members affected, in plain language, at the time — not in an annual footnote** | **`NEW`**, from `D-2`'s disclosure principle |
| **RP-5** | **No related-party transaction may be approved while `SR-01` is unresolved, because there is no person to contract with and no person to bind** | **`BLOCKED — SR-01`** · `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE` |
| **RP-6** | **A founder contribution triggers `AG-10` capture: amount, purpose, restriction, and whether it creates any claim on the organisation, its revenue or its assets** | **`NEW`** — the corpus's nearest analogues are `G-15` float/reserve capture and `G-16` grant capture **[S-B: §36.1]**; **no corpus vector addresses an insider contribution** |
| **RP-7** | **AI may flag a pattern that suggests an undisclosed related-party transaction. AI may not approve, waive, classify or clear one** | **`RETAINED`** — Part VIII's AI matrix, related-party detection row |
| **RP-8** | **A related-party transaction that fails RP-2 or RP-4 is void as an internal matter and is escalated under Part XVIII `F-16`, irrespective of its legal status** | **`NEW`** |

## XVI.3 The conflicts already on the register

| Conflict | **Content** | **Economic consequence** |
|---|---|---|
| **`CF-16`** | **Founder compensation — a conflict *"that cannot be managed"*** | Priority 4 and priority 9 cannot be approved by the person they pay, and no other authority exists → **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** |
| **`CF-19`** | **Pilot circularity** | **`ANTECEDENT REMOVED BY FD-1 — NOT OPERATIVE`** |
| **COI-1 … COI-4** | **The four segregation conflicts: Funds Manager, HR & Verification, Auditor, Safety/Protection** | These four roles may not participate in paid ventures, may not verify what they are paid from, and may not approve their own draw — **Part V.2** |
| **`AG-10`** *(new — no corpus equivalent)* | **Founder or insider contribution capture** | Every contribution in is a related-party event and is registered before it is accepted. **The corpus's `G-13` is volunteer-count inflation, not contribution capture — §XIX.4** |

# PART XVII — ZERO-CAPITAL AND LOW-CAPITAL ARCHITECTURE, RE-TESTED

## XVII.1 The distinction the re-test turns on

**[P]** Bound by task §14. **The historical name is rejected (`J-01`) and the underlying claim is re-tested rather than repeated.**

> ### **`ZERO CASH INVESTMENT IS NOT ZERO ECONOMIC COST.`**
>
> **"Zero capital" as a *funding requirement* is testable and, on the current architecture, false. "Zero capital" as an *entry barrier for a member* is true and is worth keeping: no member pays to join, participate, advance or leave (`MLM-3`, `MLM-5`). **The error is not the promise to members — it is the belief that the promise to members is also a promise about the organisation.** The organisation's costs do not disappear because the members are not asked to pay them; they are simply paid by someone else, or not paid at all.**

## XVII.2 The fourteen hidden capital requirements

**[P]** Task §14 requires these to be named. **Each is capital rather than cost because it must exist *before* the revenue it enables, cannot be recovered from a collection that has not happened, and is not discharged by anyone refusing to be paid.**

| # | **Hidden capital** | **What it actually is** | **Who supplies it today** | **Consequence if unsupplied** | **Corpus anchor** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **HC-1** | **Constitutive capital** | The cost of creating a legal person: counsel, drafting, filing, governance documents | **`NOBODY`** | **`SR-01` remains unresolved and every transfer in §XV.4 remains impossible** | `SR-01`, `LDR-U08`, **A-4** |
| **HC-2** | **Compliance capital** | PAN, registrations, a bank mandate, statutory enrolments, filing capability | **`NOBODY`** | **`A-N16` remains blocked; money cannot be received or paid at all** | **`A-N16`, 0 of 5 conditions met** |
| **HC-3** | **Working capital** | The gap between incurring a direct cost and collecting for it | **`NOBODY`** | **Priority 3 cannot be met; a venture that grows faster than it collects becomes insolvent while succeeding** | **X.3 determinant 4; P-6** |
| **HC-4** | **Wage-protection capital** | The buffer that makes priority 1 survive a weak month | **`NOBODY — priority 6 is behind four claims`** | **The shortfall rule §XI.5 fires in the first weak month, which is also the first month anyone was promised anything** | **XIV.3, XIV.4** |
| **HC-5** | **Human time capital** | The unpaid hours of the 11 roles performing L2 work while also doing L1 | **The role-holders, invisibly** | **Burnout, silent abandonment, and the control roles failing first — `D-6`, `D-8`** | **`B-09`; XII.2 FM-2** |
| **HC-6** | **Skills capital** | Sales, pricing, negotiation, delivery quality, financial literacy — none of which the architecture supplies | **`NOBODY`** | **Every venture is priced by guesswork and sold by people who have never sold** | **IX.4; `[S-B: §7.3]` four constraints** |
| **HC-7** | **Infrastructure capital** | Tools, materials, equipment, workspace, connectivity | **Non-cash resourcing in principle** | **L3 unfunded; a venture cannot deliver what it has sold** | **XII.1 L3; R-C** |
| **HC-8** | **Technology and AI capital** | Models, compute, licences, integration, maintenance, and the human skill to supervise them | **`NOBODY`** | **The AI Business Network is an architecture with no machinery — and Part VIII's matrix assumes capability that has not been funded** | **Part VIII; `[S-D: §30.2]`** |
| **HC-9** | **Trust and brand capital** | The acquisition cost the corpus measures: a first customer in a neighbourhood costs trust, time and delivery quality | **Nobody can buy it; it is earned slowly** | **No customers, therefore no revenue, therefore no priority 1, therefore no architecture in operation** | **IX.4; RT-21 City pipeline** |
| **HC-10** | **Governance and audit capital** | The cost of amendment, validation, audit, records and the professional advice this instrument keeps requiring | **`NOBODY — funding-map row 14`** | **The architecture decays silently; nobody detects it because detection is the unfunded function** | **XII.2 row 14; Part XXII** |
| **HC-11** | **Risk and insurance capital** | Cover for custody of goods, safety incidents, professional error, cyber and data, and employment liability | **`UNKNOWN`** | **A single incident exceeds the entire enterprise's capacity. **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL ONLY — NO COVERAGE IS ASSERTED`*** | **Standing constraint; `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **HC-12** | **Records and data capital** | The means of capturing the 17 record types with WHO → WHAT → WHEN → WHY → AMOUNT → AUTHORITY → EVIDENCE → STATUS — **explicitly not software** | **`NOBODY`** | **No records, therefore no verification, therefore no compensation, therefore no audit, therefore no architecture** | **Part XXI; EA-26 no software** |
| **HC-13** | **Mission capital** | The pre-surplus instrument that lets service operate before `S-6` | **`NOBODY`** | **The mission waits for commerce, which is the exact inversion of the organisation's purpose** | **Part XIII; R-A's limit** |
| **HC-14** | **Failure capital** | The cost of the 18 failure modes: containment, remediation, restitution, professional advice, and the time of the people who must decide | **`NOBODY`** | **Failure is absorbed by individuals personally, which violates `D-3` and ends the organisation's ability to fail safely** | **Part XVIII; `D-3`** |

### **The re-test verdict**

| Test | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Does the architecture require cash from members?** | **`NO — AND THIS IS RETAINED AS A PERMANENT PROHIBITION`** (`MLM-3`, `MLM-5`, `D-3`, J-11) |
| **Does the architecture require capital?** | **`YES — FOURTEEN KINDS, AND NONE OF THEM IS SUPPLIED`** |
| **Can the fourteen be met from the waterfall?** | **`NO.` The waterfall distributes a collection. Ten of the fourteen must exist before the first collection** |
| **Can they be met from a founder contribution?** | **Possibly — but that is `C_L1-a`, it is `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`, it triggers `AG-10` and Part XVI, and it creates a funder** |
| **Can they be met from donations?** | ***"Donations may be possible **after validation**"* — validation has not occurred, and restricted money carries an obligation to honour the restriction |
| **Can they be met by going slower?** | **`PARTLY.` Slowness converts HC-3, HC-4, HC-7 and HC-8 into HC-5 — time capital paid by members personally. **That is not a solution; it is a transfer from the organisation to the people it exists to protect, and `D-3` forbids requiring it*** |
| **Overall** | **`THE "ZERO-CAPITAL" CLAIM IS REJECTED AS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE ORGANISATION (J-01) AND RETAINED AS A PROMISE TO MEMBERS (MLM-3, MLM-5, D-3). THE ORGANISATION IS CAPITAL-HUNGRY AND CURRENTLY CAPITAL-FREE.`** |

## XVII.3 What low-capital can honestly mean

**[P]** The defensible version of the claim, stated as an architectural rule rather than a marketing line.

| Rule | **Content** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **LC-1** | **No member pays to join, participate, advance, be recognised or leave** | `MLM-3`, `MLM-5` — permanent |
| **LC-2** | **No venture is approved whose *direct* costs cannot be met from its own collection within a stated collection cycle** — the parameter is `[M]`, never invented | `HG-4`; Part XVIII `F-02` |
| **LC-3** | **Every venture is started at the smallest scale at which its unit economics can be observed** | `[S-B: §19.2–19.5]` four gates; RT-19 |
| **LC-4** | **Capability is acquired by resourcing, sharing and non-cash means before it is purchased** | R-C; XII.1 L3 |
| **LC-5** | **Where capital is genuinely required and genuinely absent, the correct act is to name the requirement and stop — not to proceed and transfer the cost to members** | **XVII.2 verdict; `D-3`; EA-24** |
| **LC-6** | **The architecture never describes itself as zero-capital. It describes itself as zero-member-fee and capital-dependent** | **`NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`, replacing `J-01`** |
