# PART XXIII — AI ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE

## XXIII.1 What AI is in this architecture

**[P]** Bound by **EA-15, EA-21, FA-1, FA-7**. **The founder's assertion is that Krytos is the *Virtual Brain*. Held as an assertion and never as a legal conclusion, that phrase describes a *function*: an arrangement that recommends, models, drafts, detects and summarises across the ecosystem. It does not describe a person, an employer, a counterparty, a signatory or an owner.**

| Question | **Answer** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **Is AI a legal person here?** | **`NO`** | **FA-1, FA-4, SEP-7; Rule K-4 `ASSERTION ≠ DOCUMENT`** |
| **Can AI hold money, own a venture, employ anyone or sign anything?** | **`NO`** | **XX.2 — every `SIGNS` cell involving money is `BLOCKED`; a system cannot be a signatory in any event** |
| **Can AI make a consequential economic decision?** | **`NO — PERMANENTLY, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** | **[S: Const. Art.12]** verbatim: *"AI may recommend; humans decide. AI cannot autonomously make consequential HR, wage, legal, safeguarding, safety, major fund or leadership decisions"* — `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`, not a proposal **[S-D:1161, 4529 `LDR-C05`]**; **J-21** |
| **Can AI be a role-holder?** | **`NO`. The AI & Business Development Manager is a human role that *uses* AI** | **[appendix.txt:2142–2168]** — the specification says the manager *"cannot … set wages alone … or let AI make consequential decisions"* |
| **Is an AI system authorised to be built?** | **`NO`** | **EA-21, EA-26; §18 of the delta-audit brief** — no AI system, dashboard, automation or platform absent an authorising gate. **No gate authorises one** |
| **What, then, is the AI Business Network today?** | **An architecture of human roles with an unfunded technology dependency** | **HC-8; Part VIII's lifecycle and matrix** |

## XXIII.2 The economic function boundary

**[P]** Part VIII's matrix carries the twelve corpus rows and the seven new economic functions. **This section states the boundary as two closed lists, because a list of permissions is safer than a principle.**

| AI **may** | **Why it is safe** |
|---|---|
| **Detect a pattern in records** — an accrual ageing, a concentration, a timing anomaly, a duplicate claim, a related-party connection | Detection is a computation over records the architecture already requires, and its output is a question, not an act |
| **Draft a proposal, a price, a scope, a contract clause or a report** | A draft has no effect until a human adopts it, and adoption is recorded (**AI-2**) |
| **Model a scenario** — what happens to the joint tier if a customer pays late; which solvency line is reached under stated assumptions | A model is a hypothesis with its inputs listed (**AI-1**). **It may not be presented as a forecast of entitlement** |
| **Reconcile arithmetic** — foot a period, check a waterfall allocation against the priority order, verify that a payment sheet follows its formula version | Arithmetic against a fixed rule contains no discretion. **The rule is the control, not the calculator** |
| **Summarise and translate** — a member-facing explanation of a shortfall, in plain language | Transparency is an obligation (**`D-2`**, **DR-7**) and a summary speeds it |
| **Flag a possible breach** of any rule in this instrument | Flagging creates a human task. **It never closes one** |
| **Classify provisionally** — an expense's likely category, a task's likely venture | **Provisional**, with a human decision recorded. **AI may flag; a human decides** (**XX.2**) |

| AI **may not** | **Why it is prohibited** |
|---|---|
| **Set or approve a final price** | A price is a commitment to a customer and a claim on a worker's compensation |
| **Form, accept, vary or terminate a contract** | Contract formation is a legal act requiring a legal person — **`SR-01`** |
| **Extend, refuse, forgive or pursue credit** | Credit is a decision about a person's obligation, and debt is the mechanism by which loyalty is manufactured (**`D-3`**) |
| **Classify an expense finally** | Classification moves money between pools and priorities — **AG-02** |
| **Draw, release, freeze or restore a reserve or float** | **Major fund decisions are expressly reserved to humans** — Art.12 |
| **Clear, waive or approve a related-party transaction** | **RP-7** — AI may flag the pattern; only a disinterested human may clear it, and none exists (**GF-5**) |
| **Decide to open, scale, pause or close a venture** | **HG-5**; closure ends people's income |
| **Compute, approve or alter compensation** | **Wage decisions are expressly reserved to humans** — Art.12; **J-21**; the formula does not exist (**GF-3**) |
| **Verify work** | Verification is the act on which priority 1 rests, and it must be performed by a person not paid from the work (**EL-2, COI-2**) |
| **Amend this architecture, or any rule in it** | **GD-14** — Tier-1: National plus professional validation plus the founder's formal amendment |
| **Produce a ranking, score, badge or comparison between people or teams** | **AG-15, J-18, MD-14** — and no such instrument may be built at all |

## XXIII.3 The eight AI fairness controls, applied to money

**[P]** Retained verbatim in substance from **[S-D: §30.3]**, each with its economic application.

| Control | **The corpus's rule** | **Its economic application** |
|---|---|---|
| **AI-1** | **Every AI output carries a confidence rating and its input list.** No output without provenance | **A modelled price, margin or solvency projection carries its inputs. An economic recommendation whose inputs are not listed may not be tabled at all** |
| **AI-2** | **Every consequential decision records the human decision-maker, the AI input considered, and whether it was followed.** Deviation is normal and is not flagged as an error | **REC-17 keeps the AI output and the human decision as separate linked entries (**F-18**). Deviating from an AI-suggested price is not an exception to be explained** |
| **AI-3** | **No AI output may be the sole basis for a consequential decision** *(Rule D-64: two independent signals)* | **Two independent signals are required for any price, any approval, any draw and any closure. **A model plus a model is not two signals***** |
| **AI-4** | **Human override is a single recorded step, requires no justification when it favours the subject, and requires justification only when it disadvantages them** | **Raising a worker's verified value above an AI-computed figure needs no justification. Lowering it does. **Asymmetric friction is deliberate***** |
| **AI-5** | **Prohibited inputs:** protected characteristics or proxies; religion; political affiliation; caste; place of birth; family relationships; **beneficiary status**; complaint history as a negative; social-media following; any sealed-category record | **§XXIII.4 extends this list for the economic domain** |
| **AI-6** | **Cohort-relative outputs only where the cohort assignment comes from records**, never from a model's clustering | **No model may cluster neighbourhoods into economic tiers. A model that discovers "low-capacity areas" has re-encoded disadvantage as a market fact** |
| **AI-7** | **Periodic disparity testing** across units and cohorts in aggregate, with small-cell suppression; **a disparity is an investigation trigger, not a finding** | **Compensation, approval rates, venture viability and accrual ageing are tested across neighbourhoods. **With one team, small-cell suppression means the test cannot yet run — which is recorded, not hidden***** |
| **AI-8** | **Model changes are versioned and recorded** like any standard, with the change reason and the affected decisions identifiable | **Every model version is tied to the payments, prices and approvals it influenced — the economic twin of **RT-16** and **AC-2**** |

## XXIII.4 The economic prohibited inputs

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`, extending **AI-5**. **No economic model, calculation, recommendation or record may use any of the following as an input.**

| # | **Prohibited input** | **Why** |
|---|---|---|
| **PI-1** | **Any count of people** — recruited, active, paused, in a team, in a downline, volunteering, attending | **`MLM-1`, AG-06** — the moment a count has economic weight, recruitment becomes the business |
| **PI-2** | **Beneficiary status, or any record of having received help** | **AI-5, BP-1 … BP-5** — service populations are not markets |
| **PI-3** | **Neighbourhood, locality, pincode or any proxy for the wealth of the people served** | **AI-6; AO-3** — a poor area is not a low-capacity market, and treating it as one is the discrimination the architecture exists to refuse |
| **PI-4** | **A person's unpaid hours, as a predictor of their willingness to work for nothing** | **`D-6`, VB-1 … VB-6** — it converts a measure of cost into a targeting criterion |
| **PI-5** | **Complaint, grievance, safeguarding or protection history as a negative economic signal** | **AI-5** — and it would make raising a concern economically dangerous |
| **PI-6** | **Historical revenue of a locality as a prediction of its capacity** | **Rule D-107: *"Historical bias is not a baseline."* Historical outcomes are inputs to disparity testing (**AI-7**), never labels for prediction** **[S-D:3511]** |
| **PI-7** | **Any sealed-category record, and any of the twelve `NM-1 … NM-12` categories** | **J-15** — permanent, no approval route |
| **PI-8** | **Social-media following, engagement or attention as a proxy for customer demand** | **IX.4** — attention is not a receipt, and pricing on it manufactures revenue that will not be collected |
| **PI-9** | **A related-party connection used to *favour* a counterparty** | **RP-T1 … RP-T5; AG-14** — detection may use the connection; benefit may not |
| **PI-10** | **Any invented number** | **EA-9, EA-10, Part XXVIII** — a model supplied with an unsupported parameter will return an unsupported conclusion with confidence attached |

## XXIII.5 The three AI-specific economic risks

| Risk | **Content** | **Control** |
|---|---|---|
| **AR-1 Rubber-stamping** | **The human defers because disagreeing requires effort** **[S-ST: F-29]**. In economics the cost is immediate: an adopted AI price becomes the venture's economics and nobody owns it | **AI-2's "whether it was followed" field · AI-3's two-signal rule · AI-4's free override in the subject's favour · REC-17's separation of the two entries · AU-12** |
| **AR-2 Confidence without capability** | **HC-8 is unfunded. A model that has never been paid for, maintained, validated or supervised produces confident output from stale inputs** | **AI-1's input list · AI-8's versioning · §XXI.1's prohibition on building anything · the honest statement that the AI Business Network currently has no machinery** |
| **AR-3 Automation of an unresolved decision** | **The most dangerous risk of all: a system that computes an answer to a question the governance has not decided. **GF-1's thirteen `UNASSIGNED` approval cells would be filled by whatever default the tool ships with***** | **`GD-01 … GD-15` must be decided by humans before any tool exists · **EA-24** — a metric may never be both the target and the judge · **F-18** treats the appearance of an AI decision as a failure mode in its own right** |

---

# PART XXIV — HUMAN DECISION ARCHITECTURE

## XXIV.1 The decisions that are human, and only human

**[P]** Bound by **HG-1 … HG-7, EA-15, J-21**. **Part VIII assigns the gates. This Part states the complete list of economic decisions reserved to humans, because a reserved list is enforceable and a principle is not.**

| # | **Reserved human decision** | **Gate** | **Current holder** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HD-1** | **Whether a venture enters the approval universe at all** | **HG-1, HG-2** | **`UNASSIGNED — GF-1`** |
| **HD-2** | **Whether a venture passes economic validation** | **HG-3** | **`UNASSIGNED`** |
| **HD-3** | **Whether a venture passes legal and compliance check** | **HG-4** | **`BLOCKED — SR-01; `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION``** |
| **HD-4** | **Whether a pilot may proceed, and on what it may spend** | **HG-4** | **`NOT AUTHORISED — EA-28; `GATE P0 = 0 of 10``** |
| **HD-5** | **Whether a customer's acceptance is genuine** | **HG-6** | HR & Verification — **and it may not be paid from what it verifies** |
| **HD-6** | **Whether work is verified and at what value** | **HG-6** | HR & Verification |
| **HD-7** | **Whether the joint tier can be met, and what happens if it cannot** | **HG-6, HG-7** | **`UNASSIGNED`** |
| **HD-8** | **Whether a reserve or float draw occurs** | **HG-7** | **`UNASSIGNED — GD-03`** |
| **HD-9** | **Whether a venture is restructured, paused, scaled or closed** | **HG-5** | **`UNASSIGNED`** |
| **HD-10** | **Whether a contribution is accepted, and on what terms** | **HG-7** | **`UNASSIGNED`** |
| **HD-11** | **Whether a related-party transaction proceeds** | **HG-7** | **`NO DISINTERESTED HOLDER EXISTS — GF-5`** |
| **HD-12** | **Whether mission money is spent, and on what** | **HG-7** | **`UNASSIGNED — GD-13`** |
| **HD-13** | **Whether founder compensation is set, paid or deferred** | **HG-7** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal) — CF-16`** |
| **HD-14** | **Whether any rule in this instrument is amended** | **Tier-1** | **National + professional validation + the founder's formal amendment — **`BLOCKED, no National layer exists`**** |
| **HD-15** | **Whether an audit finding is accepted, contested or escalated** | **HG-7** | Internal Auditor detects; **`UNASSIGNED` decides** |

## XXIV.2 The escalation ladder, and the fact that it has one rung

**[P]** The corpus's ladder is **PRIMARY ROLE → SUB-ROLE/DEPUTY → COORDINATORS → SUPPORTING MEMBERS**, above which sit Team, City, State and National. **The economic decision ladder requires a level above the decider for every contested act.**

| Level | **Exists?** | **Consequence** |
|---|---|---|
| **Team / Mohalla** | **Yes — eleven named roles** | Every decision below HD-14 originates here |
| **City** | **`NO`** | No level to escalate a contested verification, a concentration exception or a related-party question to |
| **State** | **`NO`** | No specialist pool, no second verifier, no out-of-team assessor |
| **National** | **`NO`** | **No body capable of a Tier-1 amendment — which means the priority order, the founder's position at priority 4 and reinvestment's place cannot be changed by anyone, at all, today** |
| **Founder** | **Yes — one person** | **Every unresolved decision routes here, and several of them concern the founder's own money. **`CF-16` is not a defect in this design; it is the honest description of a one-person governance layer***** |

> ### **`THE ESCALATION LADDER HAS ONE RUNG, AND THE PERSON STANDING ON IT IS INTERESTED IN SEVERAL OF THE DECISIONS.`**
>
> **This is `GD-01` and `GF-1` stated structurally. It is not solvable by an architecture document. It is solvable by **(a)** appointing a disinterested approver, **(b)** creating a level, or **(c)** accepting that certain economic acts cannot occur until one of the first two happens. **This instrument records the three options and selects none — EA-17, EA-24.***

## XXIV.3 The decision record

**[P]** **REC-17** carries every decision. **Four properties are non-negotiable:**

| Property | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **Named human** | **`AUTHORITY` names a person, never a system, never "the organisation", never "the team"** (**DR-3**) |
| **Options considered** | **A decision recorded without its alternatives cannot be audited, because nobody can tell whether the decider saw the trade** |
| **Reason and rule** | **Which rule in this instrument was applied — or an explicit statement that none applied and the decision was discretionary** |
| **Recusal** | **Who was interested, and whether they recused. **A recusal is recorded, never assumed** (**RP-2**)** |

## XXIV.4 No decision by default

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`. **The most common governance failure in a small organisation is not a wrong decision; it is the absence of one, discovered later as a fact on the ground.**

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **ND-1** | **Where a decision is required and none is made, the absence is recorded as a decision of status `UNRESOLVED`, with the date it became due and the person it was routed to** |
| **ND-2** | **An `UNRESOLVED` status blocks the act.** It does not permit the act to proceed on a default, a precedent, a tool's setting or an assumption |
| **ND-3** | **`GD-01 … GD-15` are the current population of ND-1 records.** Fifteen open decisions, each with a named route and none with a named holder |
| **ND-4** | **A default is never a decision.** Where a system, a template, a form or an AI output supplies a value because no human did, **F-18** applies |
| **ND-5** | **Silence is not consent, and delay is not refusal.** Both are recorded, and both are visible to the people they affect (**DR-7**) |
| **ND-6** | **Ageing applies to decisions as it does to accruals (**AC-5**): a decision that has been `UNRESOLVED` for longer than the stated period is escalated, and the escalation is recorded** |

## XXIV.5 Decision latency as an economic cost

**[P]** `NEW — CURRENT SEWAK ARCHITECTURE`. **Latency is not an administrative inconvenience; it is a cost with a location.**

| Act | **What latency costs** |
|---|---|
| **Venture approval** | The window in which the customer's need exists. **A decision that arrives after the need has been met elsewhere costs the acquisition, and acquisition is the scarcest resource in this architecture (IX.4)** |
| **Verification** | Priority 1's timing. **A worker whose work is unverified is a worker who is unpaid, and the delay is indistinguishable from a shortfall** |
| **Collection escalation** | The accrual's age. **F-03's exposure grows with every period of delay** |
| **Reserve draw** | The shock's size. **A trigger met late is a trigger not met** |
| **Related-party clearance** | The transaction, which simply does not happen — **the correct outcome where no disinterested approver exists, and a real cost nonetheless** |
| **Amendment** | **Everything downstream of the amended rule.** The priority order, the founder's position and reinvestment's place are all frozen today, and every economic design that depends on them is frozen with them |

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **DL-1** | **Every reserved decision carries a stated period within which it must be made or recorded as `UNRESOLVED`** — the period is `[M]`, never invented |
| **DL-2** | **Latency is measured per act and reported, not averaged** — an average conceals the one decision that blocked everything |
| **DL-3** | **Latency is never resolved by delegating the decision to a system** (**ND-4, AR-3**) |

---

# PART XXV — THE SEVEN CANDIDATE ECONOMIC MODELS

## XXV.1 The method, and the attribute set

**[P]** Bound by **EA-29**. **The corpus's own rule is adopted: *"no winner is chosen automatically. Each model is assessed against the source, then on eight criteria, and the recommendation is derived from the model rather than from preference"* **[S-B: §16]**. Seven candidate models are assessed, each on the **ten attributes the corpus itself uses at [S-B: §37.1]**, so that the assessment is comparable with the corpus's own and no new measuring stick is invented.**

| # | **Attribute** | **What it asks** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Description** | What the model actually is, in one statement |
| **2** | **Source basis** | Whether the current corpus supports it, and where |
| **3** | **Resilience** | What it survives, and what kills it |
| **4** | **Autonomy** | Who decides what the organisation does, and who can stop it |
| **5** | **Fairness** | Whether it treats a poor neighbourhood differently from a rich one |
| **6** | **Scaling** | What it scales with |
| **7** | **Control** | How strong the governance is, and what it degrades into |
| **8** | **Incentives** | What behaviour it rewards — tested against `I-01 … I-08` and Part XXVII |
| **9** | **Startup feasibility** | Whether it can begin at zero collection with eleven people and no entity |
| **10** | **Service continuity** | Whether the mission keeps running when the money stops |

| Model | **Name** | **Origin** |
|---|---|---|
| **CM-A** | **Central funding** | Corpus **[S-B: §37.1 Model A]** — already rejected at **J-20** |
| **CM-B** | **Local self-sustainability** | Corpus **[S-B: §37.1 Model B]** — already rejected at **J-19** |
| **CM-C** | **Hybrid with an explicit funding map** | Corpus **[S-B: §37.1 Model C, §37.3]** and **`B-26`** — adopted here as **XII.2** |
| **CM-D** | **Fully volunteer mission, no commerce** | **`NEW`** candidate, derived from corpus **[S-B: §16.1 Model A]** taken to its limit |
| **CM-E** | **Service volunteered, venture work paid** | Corpus **[S-B: §16.1 Model D]** — the labour-layer model the architecture already describes |
| **CM-F** | **Ecosystem model — Krytos as economic head** | **`NEW`** candidate, derived from **FA-1, FA-2, FA-6** held strictly as assertions |
| **CM-G** | **Member-owned venture cooperatives** | **`NEW`** candidate, derived from the founder-upside question at **XV.5** |

## XXV.2 CM-A — Central funding

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | Teams are funded from a central pool — donations, grants, founder capital, Krytos — and ventures are optional |
| **2 Source basis** | **Weak.** Donations *"may be possible after validation"*; the social-impact fund is surplus-dependent and barred from operating cost; **no central operating budget exists anywhere in the corpus** **[S-B: §37.1]** |
| **3 Resilience** | High for teams, **low for the centre — one donor decision ends everything** |
| **4 Autonomy** | **Low — *"he who pays decides."*** Donor intent and funder capture both apply |
| **5 Fairness** | High between teams, **low between donors' preferences and local need** |
| **6 Scaling** | **Scales with fundraising capacity, not with capability** |
| **7 Control** | Strong central control; **degrades into a grant-administration organisation** |
| **8 Incentives** | **Poor — no survival pressure, and dependency.** Maximises `I-03` and `I-08` |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **Feasible only if a funder exists — and none is named.** Adopting it would mean deciding to become a donor-funded organisation, which contradicts *"the organisation is not merely a donation-funded charity; legitimate business is part of the design"* |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Excellent — service is funded directly.** This is the one attribute on which CM-A beats everything else, and it is the reason `C_L1-a` remains an open option at **GD-06** |
| **Verdict** | **`REJECTED AS UNAVAILABLE — J-20.`** Not wrong in principle; **unfunded in fact**. Its single genuine advantage is carried into this architecture as **`MI-1`**, the pre-surplus mission instrument, rather than as an operating model |

## XXV.3 CM-B — Local self-sustainability

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | Each team must fund itself from its own ventures; the centre is funded by priority-5 fees |
| **2 Source basis** | **Strong in principle** — the waterfall is venture-receipts-based — **and contradicted in fact by the corpus's own arithmetic** |
| **3 Resilience** | **Low for teams**; high for the centre. Teams fail individually without systemic risk — which is a polite way of saying the failure is invisible |
| **4 Autonomy** | **High — but only for teams that survive** |
| **5 Fairness** | **`LOW — A POOR NEIGHBOURHOOD IS PENALISED FOR BEING POOR.`** This is the fairness failure the whole architecture exists to refuse, and it is why **AO-3** is an anti-objective |
| **6 Scaling** | **Scales only where demand exists — so it cannot reach the places the mission exists for** |
| **7 Control** | Weak centrally; strong local discipline from survival |
| **8 Incentives** | **Strong for revenue, destructive for mission.** Maximises `I-01`, `I-02` and `I-07` simultaneously |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **`NOT FEASIBLE.`** A standing-start team cannot reach healthy break-even in the target months; there is no cold-start instrument, and **XIV.4** shows why priority 6 cannot supply one |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Poor — service is the first thing cut** |
| **Verdict** | **`REJECTED — J-19.`** ***"Model B is not a policy choice but a description of failure"*** **[S-B: §37.3]**. **It is also the model the current architecture defaults into if `GD-02` is never decided — which is the practical reason `H-Q17` cannot be left open** |

## XXV.4 CM-C — Hybrid with an explicit funding map

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | **Service and governance funded centrally or by donation; venture economics local; cross-subsidy layered — and every cost layer mapped to a named source, with no blank cell** |
| **2 Source basis** | **Strongest in fact.** *"The source already **is** hybrid: ventures local, reserves national, materials and pipeline at City, specialists at State, standards and platform national"* **[S-B: §37.1]** |
| **3 Resilience** | **Medium-high — diversified sources; failure is local and supportable** |
| **4 Autonomy** | Medium-high — local venture choice inside central standards |
| **5 Fairness** | **Highest available — need-based support with published criteria** |
| **6 Scaling** | Scales with capability and with centre funding, rather than with either alone |
| **7 Control** | Balanced; **the corpus's own decision matrix already describes it** |
| **8 Incentives** | **Best available — venture discipline exists, and the mission is protected by the veto gates rather than by surplus** |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **Feasible only with a float and a named centre source.** ***"What it lacks is not a design but three named funding decisions"*** **[S-B: §37.3]** — and in the current architecture **none of the three is made**: the centre's pre-revenue source (**GD-02**), the float (**P-6**, proposed), and the service-continuity instrument (**`MI-1`**, proposed) |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Good — a time-costed floor plus a materials instrument** |
| **Verdict** | **`ADOPTED AS THE FUNDING LAYER OF THIS ARCHITECTURE.`** **The corpus's caveat is carried in full: *"Model C is the most complex of the three and therefore the most exposed to G-15 and G-16. Its controls — caps, terms, reconciliation, publication, one renewal maximum — are not optional extras. They are what distinguishes a hybrid from a dependency."* Those controls are **MD-05, AG-11, AG-12, REC-11, REC-12**** |

## XXV.5 CM-D — Fully volunteer mission, no commerce

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | Nearly all labour unpaid; no commercial venture at all; the organisation exists purely as a service body |
| **2 Source basis** | **Contradicted.** *"The organisation is not merely a donation-funded charity; legitimate business is part of the design"* is preserved as `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`. Priority 1 exists, and it exists to pay people |
| **3 Resilience** | **Low cash need, high churn risk.** Survives on mission commitment alone, and mission commitment is the one resource that cannot be replenished once spent |
| **4 Autonomy** | High internally; **wholly dependent externally on whoever funds the non-labour costs** |
| **5 Fairness** | **`THE WORST OF THE SEVEN.`** It asks the people nearest to the need — who are usually the least able to afford it — to work for nothing, indefinitely. **`D-8` exists precisely because some roles cannot be unpaid** |
| **6 Scaling** | **Scales with willingness to work for free, which is negatively correlated with the cost of living** |
| **7 Control** | Weak — no compensation means no compensation records, which means no verification discipline, which means no audit trail |
| **8 Incentives** | Removes the revenue incentives entirely **and** creates the worst one available: **the organisation's capacity becomes a function of who can afford to volunteer** |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **`FEASIBLE — AND IT IS WHAT IS ACTUALLY HAPPENING.`** Eleven people, no entity, no revenue, no funding source for eight of fourteen map rows. **CM-D is not a proposal; it is the current de facto state, and naming it is the point** |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Good in the short term, poor in the long term** — service continues until the volunteers stop, and there is no instrument to notice |
| **Verdict** | **`REJECTED AS A MODEL — BUT RECOGNISED AS THE PRESENT CONDITION.`** The architecture must be built so that CM-D is a **stage with an exit**, not a **destination without one**. **`F-10`, `F-12`, HC-5, VB-5 and `D-8` are the exit instruments** |

## XXV.6 CM-E — Service volunteered, venture work paid

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | **A rule rather than a mixture: mission work is volunteered; venture work is paid** |
| **2 Source basis** | **Strong — *"this is what the architecture describes."*** Priority 1 pays verified *venture* work; service is scheduled from verified and trained members with no payment line **[S-B: §16.1]** |
| **3 Resilience** | **Highest of the labour models — cash need scales with revenue: no revenue, no payroll, no insolvency** |
| **4 Autonomy** | High at the venture level; **the boundary between service and venture is set centrally and must be published** (**VB-5**) |
| **5 Fairness** | **Fair at the boundary, unfair across it.** A person doing mission work is unpaid while a person doing venture work is paid, and the two are often the same person in the same week — which is exactly why **MW-1 … MW-6** recording is mandatory and **AL-4** was adopted |
| **6 Scaling** | Scales with venture demand; **the service side does not scale with anything, because it is unfunded** |
| **7 Control** | **Strong — and stronger than any other model, because the rule is binary.** The audit question is not "was this fair?" but "was this venture work?" |
| **8 Incentives** | **Creates one specific distortion: an incentive to classify work as venture work.** Controlled by **AG-02**, **EL-3** and **AU-5** |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **`FEASIBLE.`** It requires no funder, no float and no entity — only a published boundary and honest records |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Depends entirely on `MI-1`.** Service is volunteered, so it continues as long as volunteers continue — which **`D-6`** requires to be measured rather than assumed |
| **Verdict** | **`ADOPTED AS THE LABOUR LAYER OF THIS ARCHITECTURE.`** **CM-C funds it; CM-E staffs it. Together they are the model this instrument has actually designed, and neither is new — both are the corpus's own recommendations, carried forward with their caveats intact** |

## XXV.7 CM-F — Ecosystem model: Krytos as economic head

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | Krytos holds the commercial activity, the brand, the technology and the upside; Sewak Sathi Sanstha delivers service and supplies labour |
| **2 Source basis** | **`NONE.`** It rests on **FA-1, FA-2 and FA-6 — three assertions**. **No constitutive document exists (FA-4), no legal work has been done (FA-5), and no relationship between the two is established anywhere in the corpus** |
| **3 Resilience** | **Unevaluable.** Resilience requires a counterparty, and there is none |
| **4 Autonomy** | **Would be low for Sewak Sathi** — the delivery body would depend on the commercial body for every rupee, with no instrument governing the transfer |
| **5 Fairness** | **`THE MOST DANGEROUS ATTRIBUTE OF THE SEVEN MODELS.`** Members' verified labour would generate value in one body and be compensated, if at all, by another — **which is the structural form of the thing `MLM-2`, `SEP-6` and Part XI exist to prevent** |
| **6 Scaling** | Scales with Krytos's commercial success, **which is undefined, unfunded and unconstituted** |
| **7 Control** | **`NONE AVAILABLE.`** Every transfer is a related-party transaction (**RP-T3**), every approval requires a disinterested approver who does not exist (**GF-5**), and the founder sits on both sides of every one (**SEP-1**) |
| **8 Incentives** | **Creates the strongest available incentive to move value out of the mission body and into the commercial body — and the architecture has no instrument to detect it, because there is no counterparty to record** |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **`IMPOSSIBLE.`** Two of the ten pairings at **XV.4** are not merely unapproved; **one side does not exist** |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Would be wholly contingent on a commercial body's willingness to fund it, with no restriction, no instrument and no obligation** |
| **Verdict** | **`REJECTED — NOT ON MERIT, ON EXISTENCE.`** ***No transfer of money, value, equity, IP, revenue or control between Krytos, Sewak Sathi Sanstha, the founder, any venture and any worker is legally permissible today (§XV.1).*** **The eight questions at XV.6 must be answered, in order, by professionals and by the founder, before this model can even be assessed. Until then it is a description of an intention, not an economic model** |

## XXV.8 CM-G — Member-owned venture cooperatives

| Attribute | **Assessment** |
|---|---|
| **1 Description** | Each venture is owned by the members who deliver it; they share its surplus directly; the organisation licenses the standard and takes a fee |
| **2 Source basis** | **`NONE — AND PARTLY CONTRADICTED.`** *"Leader equity, profit share or ownership stakes are prohibited"* **[S-D:2999]**. The prohibition is addressed to leaders, not to members, **but nothing in the corpus authorises member ownership either** |
| **3 Resilience** | High per venture — the owners bear the risk they create. **Low for the organisation, which would hold the brand and none of the economics** |
| **4 Autonomy** | **Highest of the seven** |
| **5 Fairness** | **`HIGHLY UNEVEN.`** Members in a wealthy catchment would own viable ventures; members in a poor one would own nothing. **The same fairness failure as CM-B, one level down** |
| **6 Scaling** | Scales with the formation of entities — **and entity formation is exactly what EA-20 prohibits this instrument from selecting** |
| **7 Control** | **Weak.** Ownership creates a claim that outranks every rule in this architecture, and the anti-MLM firewall would have to be re-proven against a structure in which recruiting a co-owner *is* recruiting someone whose income depends on you |
| **8 Incentives** | **`FAILS `MLM-1` AND `MLM-8` ON INSPECTION.`** In a cooperative, a member's income rises when the membership rises — not because a commission is paid, but because the economics of the venture genuinely improve with more owners. **The vector is real even where the intent is not, and `MLM-8`'s test would refuse it at G3** |
| **9 Startup feasibility** | **`NOT FEASIBLE.`** Requires entity formation, an ownership document, a valuation, a members' agreement, tax characterisation and a bank account — **six things that do not exist and five of which are `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **10 Service continuity** | **Poor — a venture owned by its workers has no obligation to anyone it serves** |
| **Verdict** | **`REJECTED.`** Not because member benefit is wrong — Part XI exists to secure it — but because **ownership is a legal structure, and no legal structure may be selected here (EA-20)**, and because **the incentive test it fails is the one that is permanent with no approval route**. **Worker benefit is achieved through priority 1 and the published formula, which require no entity, no equity and no ownership** |

## XXV.9 The determination

**[P]** Derived, not preferred — and it produces one architecture, not a menu (**EA-29**).

| Model | **Verdict** | **Ground** |
|---|---|---|
| **CM-A** | **`REJECTED AS UNAVAILABLE`** — one advantage carried forward as **`MI-1`** | **J-20; [S-B: §37.3]** |
| **CM-B** | **`REJECTED`** — and recognised as the **default** if `GD-02` is never decided | **J-19; AO-3; [S-B: §37.3]** |
| **CM-C** | **`ADOPTED — THE FUNDING LAYER`** | **[S-B: §37.3]; `B-26` → XII.2; AL-4** |
| **CM-D** | **`REJECTED AS A MODEL — RECOGNISED AS THE PRESENT CONDITION`** | **§XXV.5; F-10, F-12, HC-5** |
| **CM-E** | **`ADOPTED — THE LABOUR LAYER`** | **[S-B: §16.1 Model D]; VB-5; MW-1 … MW-6** |
| **CM-F** | **`REJECTED — NOT ON MERIT, ON EXISTENCE`** | **FA-4, FA-5; XV.1; XV.4; SEP-1 … SEP-8** |
| **CM-G** | **`REJECTED`** | **[S-D:2999]; `MLM-1`, `MLM-8`; EA-20** |

> ### **`THE CURRENT ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE IS CM-C FUNDED AND CM-E STAFFED.`**
>
> **Both halves are the corpus's own conclusions, carried forward with their caveats and their controls intact. Neither is new, and nothing in this Part invents a third model. What is new is the statement of what CM-C requires and does not have: **three named funding decisions, none of them made — the centre's pre-revenue source (`GD-02`), the float (`P-6`), and the service-continuity instrument (`MI-1`).** Until those three exist, CM-C is a design and CM-B is what happens.**

---

# PART XXVI — CONNECTION TO THE FIFTEEN ORGANISATIONAL SYSTEMS

## XXVI.1 The method

**[P]** Bound by task §20. **An economic architecture that is not connected to the systems around it will be overridden by them, and — more dangerously — will silently damage them. Each of the fifteen systems is examined in three directions: what the economy needs from it, what it needs from the economy, and **where the economic incentive damages it**. The third column is the one that matters, and it is filled for all fifteen.**

## XXVI.2 Systems 1 – 8

| # | **System** | **What the economy needs from it** | **What it needs from the economy** | **Where the economic incentive damages it** | **Control** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Governance and authority** | An approver for every act in **XX.2** — currently `UNASSIGNED` in thirteen of sixteen | A funding source for governance itself (**HC-10, GD-12, funding-map row 14**) | **An unfunded governance system is bypassed rather than obeyed. Every economic pressure in this architecture points toward acting first and recording later, because acting is how a collection happens** | **`GD-01`, `GD-14`, **ND-1 … ND-6**, AU-10** |
| **2** | **Membership and participation** | A verified record of who is a member and in what role class | Nothing — **membership must never be an economic transaction** | **The moment membership has economic value, recruitment becomes the business. This is the single shortest path from a mission organisation to an MLM** | **`MLM-1`, `MLM-3`, `MLM-5`, `MLM-8`, **T1**, **T4**, AG-06, AG-08** |
| **3** | **Leadership and succession** | A leader who performs verified leadership **work**, separately recorded | Compensation for that work, bounded and capped | **`I-06` — promotion rewards the leader who grows a team, and growing a team weakens the team. Economically, a leader differential tied to team size makes this worse than the corpus's version, because the reward becomes cash rather than status** | **LD-1 … LD-8, **MLM-2**, **MLM-9**, MW-1 … MW-6, AG-07** |
| **4** | **Protection and safeguarding** | A protection function that operates whether or not it is funded | A funding source that does not depend on the activity it constrains | **`I-03` at its most dangerous: protection is invisible work. A prevented incident produces no photograph, no count, no receipt and no revenue. **An economy that pays only for verified venture work systematically starves the function whose absence kills people*** | **`NM-3`, XII.2 row 9, **`MI-1`**, **BP-1 … BP-5**, EA-23 veto not weight** |
| **5** | **Human adoption and mass participation** | Participation that is free, voluntary and unbought | A reason to participate that is not a payment | **Any economic benefit attached to participation converts adoption into recruitment, and adoption metrics into headcount metrics — **`G-13`** volunteer-count inflation, which the corpus already identifies** | **`MLM-3 … MLM-6`, PI-1, **AO-2**, AG-06, J-18, MD-14** |
| **6** | **Krytos and the ecosystem** | A legal person, which does not exist | Nothing may flow to it today | **An intended economic head creates an incentive to route value toward it before it exists — in the form of work, IP, brand, records or member commitment. **The routing is invisible precisely because there is no counterparty to record*** | **XV.1's declaration, XV.4's matrix, SEP-1 … SEP-8, **RP-T3**, CM-F's rejection** |
| **7** | **The AI Business Network and venture lifecycle** | Eleven stages with human gates at every stage where money moves | Funded AI capability (**HC-8**), which does not exist | **`AR-3` — a lifecycle with thirteen `UNASSIGNED` approval cells will be filled by whatever default the tool supplies. **Automation does not remove an unresolved decision; it hides one*** | **HG-1 … HG-7, Part VIII's matrix, Part XXIII's two closed lists, **ND-4**, AU-12** |
| **8** | **Verification and HR** | Independence from what it verifies | A funding source that is not the work being verified | **`PC-07`: the verifier is unfunded, and the cheapest way to fund a verifier is to let them verify work they are paid from. **That is not a hypothetical; it is the default state of eleven people with no segregation available*** | **COI-2, **EL-2**, EL-5, AU-5, **AG-01**, F-11** |

## XXVI.3 Systems 9 – 15

| # | **System** | **What the economy needs from it** | **What it needs from the economy** | **Where the economic incentive damages it** | **Control** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **9** | **Funds and custody** | The four-hand separation: receive, approve, record, review | A mandate, an account and a signatory — **none exist** | **`A-N16` makes segregation impossible, and impossibility is resolved in practice by one person doing all four. **The damage is not the breach; it is the normalisation of the breach*** | **COI-1, EA-16, **X-7**, **X-8**, AU-4, F-11, **[appendix.txt:6925–6958]**** |
| **10** | **Warehouse and materials** | Custody of goods with an unbroken evidence chain | A funded handling and storage capability | **Category 7 is the only category that could fund warehouse capability, and it is blocked at Stage 2 — so custody of donated materials is performed by an unfunded role handling goods it cannot insure. **A loss falls on the person holding it*** | **XII.2 row 5, HC-11 as a **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`**, REC-05, **BP-3**** |
| **11** | **Communication, social media and reputation** | Truthful communication that never describes earning in terms of building a team | A funded capacity that produces attention but no receipts (**IX.4**) | **`T3` — an unfunded communications function under revenue pressure will describe the opportunity rather than the work, because the opportunity is what attracts participation. **Legitimacy capital is destroyed instantly and compounds slowly (**`I-07`**)*** | **`T3`, **AG-15**, PI-8, **J-18**, EA-14, the six free categories and `NM-1 … NM-12`** |
| **12** | **Volunteer management** | A record of who volunteered, on what, and whether they could decline | A measured cost of volunteering, and a published boundary | **`I-03` again: volunteer coordination is invisible work. **And `F-12` — the moment venture work needs hands, the volunteer function becomes a labour pool, which is `NM-10` and, if it is ever paid for, `J-13`*** | **VB-1 … VB-6, **NM-10**, MW-5, **`D-6`**, `D-8`, HC-5** |
| **13** | **Audit and accountability** | Twelve audits, ten achievable, one permanently external | A funding source and an authority to compel (**AF-5**) | **`I-08` and `G-12` — audit depends on people admitting weakness, and every economic mechanism rewards looking strong. **An auditor who can detect everything and compel nothing becomes a diary, and a diary is read by nobody*** | **AU-1 … AU-12, AF-1 … AF-6, **ND-6**, DR-7, anti-retaliation** |
| **14** | **Training and capability** | Capability in sales, pricing, delivery, financial literacy and supervision (**HC-6**) | A funded training line, which has no source | **The corpus's own finding: ***"training raises capability **and** expectations. If the role cannot meet the raised expectation — because there is no paid work, no progression, no recognition — the investment accelerates exit."*** An economy that trains people and cannot pay them is an economy that subsidises other employers** | **HC-6, XII.2 row 14, VB-6, **`D-9`**, **[S-B: §15.4]**** |
| **15** | **Grievance, appeal and anti-retaliation** | A route a person can use without economic consequence | Nothing — **access to a grievance route is one of the twelve `NM` categories and may never be monetised** | **`I-08` in its sharpest form: the person most likely to raise a grievance is the person most economically exposed — an unpaid volunteer, a worker with an accrual, a member awaiting verification. **A grievance route with an economic cost attached is a grievance route that is never used*** | **`NM-12`, PI-5, **AI-4**'s asymmetric friction, DR-7, **`D-7`**, anti-retaliation** |

## XXVI.4 The pattern across all fifteen

| # | **Finding** |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Thirteen of the fifteen systems need something from the economy that the economy cannot currently supply.** Only system 2 (membership) and system 15 (grievance) need nothing — **and both of those need nothing precisely because they must never be economic** |
| **2** | **The incentive damage is concentrated in one mechanism: the architecture pays for verified venture work and not for anything else.** Nine of the fifteen damaged systems are damaged by that single fact — protection, verification, volunteer coordination, audit, training, governance, communication, custody and grievance are all invisible work |
| **3** | **`I-03` is therefore the most important incentive failure in the current architecture**, more than `I-01` or `I-07`: *"visible service produces photographs, counts and social proof; invisible service — verification, follow-up, safeguarding, record-keeping, a prevented incident — produces nothing publishable"* **[S-B: §35.1]** |
| **4** | **The fix is not to pay for invisible work from revenue that does not exist.** It is `MI-1` for mission-critical invisible work, the funding map for governance-critical invisible work, and **honest disclosure (`D-2`) where neither is available** |
| **5** | **No system is damaged by the anti-MLM firewall.** Every control in Part IX is protective of all fifteen, which is the strongest available evidence that the firewall is correctly placed |

---

# PART XXVII — INCENTIVE SAFETY

## XXVII.1 The six tests

**[P]** Bound by **EA-22, EA-23, EA-24**. **The corpus's eight incentive failures `I-01 … I-08` **[S-B: §35.1]** are the population. Six tests are derived from them and applied to this architecture. A test that this architecture passes is marked `PASS`; one it passes only with a control is `PASS WITH CONTROL`; one it fails is `FAIL — EXPOSED`.**

| Test | **The question** | **Derived from** |
|---|---|---|
| **IST-1** | **Does any reward in this architecture vary with a count of people?** | **`I-06`, `MLM-1`, `G-13`** |
| **IST-2** | **Does any reward arrive before the value is delivered and collected?** | **`I-05`, `G-01`, `G-11`** |
| **IST-3** | **Does any reward favour visible work over invisible work?** | **`I-03`, `I-04`** |
| **IST-4** | **Does any reward favour easy work over necessary work?** | **`I-02`** |
| **IST-5** | **Does any reward penalise honesty about weakness?** | **`I-08`, `G-12`** |
| **IST-6** | **Does any reward subordinate the mission to revenue, or the long term to the short term?** | **`I-01`, `I-07`** |

## XXVII.2 The tests applied

| Test | **Where it bites in this architecture** | **Control** | **Residual exposure** | **Verdict** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IST-1** | **Nowhere in the design.** Priority 1 pays verified work; the leader differential is bounded and work-justified; recognition is not compensation (**XI.9**); headcount is an anti-objective (**AO-2**) | **MLM-1 … MLM-11, PI-1, T1, T4, AG-06, AG-08, J-09 … J-13** | **The only live route is CM-G's cooperative structure, which is rejected; and **AG-07**, the reconstructed differential, which requires a control because a prohibition creates its own evasion** | **`PASS — PERMANENTLY, NO APPROVAL ROUTE`** |
| **IST-2** | **In three places.** `V_invoiced` can be recorded before collection (**RR-2**); acquisition is rewarded immediately while collection arrives later (**`I-05`**); a float or reserve draw is money before value | **RR-2's two separate fields · **S-1 … S-6** as the only permitted health statement · AG-03, AG-04 · MD-05's cap, term and one-renewal maximum · LC-2's pre-approval test** | **The accounting basis is undecided (**RR-1**), and until it is decided a team can look solvent on invoices while being unable to pay its workers — **which is the corpus's own `I-05` finding, unresolved** | **`PASS WITH CONTROL — ONE DEPENDENCY OPEN: RR-1`** |
| **IST-3** | **Everywhere. This is the architecture's deepest incentive failure.** Nine of fifteen systems perform invisible work (**XXVI.4 finding 2**); protection, verification, audit, training and volunteer coordination produce nothing publishable and nothing payable | **`MI-1` for mission-critical invisible work · the funding map for governance-critical invisible work · **`D-6`**, which measures the cost of volunteering · AU-10, the only audit that detects a mandate performed unfunded · **VB-5**'s published boundary** | **`FAIL — EXPOSED.` The controls name the exposure and measure it; **they do not fund it.** Eleven people are performing nine systems' worth of invisible work with no source, and no architecture can change that without money it does not have** | **`FAIL — EXPOSED, NAMED, MEASURED, UNFUNDED`** |
| **IST-4** | **In category selection.** The cash-generating categories are the easy ones; the difficult needs sit in the cash-intensive categories that are blocked at Stage 0–2 (education, physical products) | **Priority assessment stays **need-based, not margin-based** · the seven categories are a closed universe, so an easy category cannot be invented · **HG-2** screens on mission fit before economics · EA-23's veto gates** | **Real but bounded: no category may be approved today at all (**Part VII: 0 of 7 approved**), so the incentive has nothing to act on yet. **It becomes live at the first approval*** | **`PASS WITH CONTROL — BECOMES LIVE AT FIRST APPROVAL`** |
| **IST-5** | **In every failure record.** Eighteen failure modes each require someone to admit that something has gone wrong, and every one of the six currently-open failures (**XVIII.4 finding 7**) is an admission about the architecture's own state | **`G-12`'s control retained: *"nobody is punished for reporting a failure"* · anti-retaliation · **DR-7**, which publishes failure records to the people affected rather than routing them upward only · **AI-4**'s asymmetric friction · **ND-1**, which makes non-decision a recorded fact rather than a personal failure** | **Structural: with one governance rung (**XXIV.2**), the person who admits a failure and the person who judges it are the same person or the founder. **Anti-retaliation cannot be guaranteed by a rule where there is no second party to enforce it*** | **`PASS WITH CONTROL — STRUCTURALLY WEAK AT ONE RUNG`** |
| **IST-6** | **In the priority order itself.** Priority 7 funds the mission from surplus, so commerce precedes mission in the sequence of money | **The separation of flows (**X-1 … X-8**) · **`MI-1`**, which exists precisely so that the mission does not wait for `S-6` · **BP-1 … BP-5** · the six free categories and **`NM-1 … NM-12`** as vetoes · **J-16**'s permanent rejection of paid priority service · **`I-07`**'s trust finding carried into PI-8** | **The ordering question at **X.6** is unresolved and is a Tier-1 amendment. If founder compensation remains at priority 4, a claim on the enterprise ranks ahead of the reserve that protects workers and ahead of the funding of the control system — **which is a long-term-over-short-term inversion at the top of the structure*** | **`PASS WITH CONTROL — ONE TIER-1 QUESTION OPEN: GD-08`** |

## XXVII.3 The incentive-safety conclusion

> ### **`FIVE OF SIX TESTS PASS, FOUR OF THEM ONLY WITH A CONTROL. ONE FAILS, AND THE FAILURE IS HONEST.`**
>
> **`IST-3` cannot be passed by an architecture with no money. It is the failure that the whole of Part XII's funding map, Part XIII's `MI-1`, `D-6`'s measurement of volunteering and AU-10's honesty audit exist to make **visible** — and visibility is what an architecture can do when funding is what it cannot do. Any instrument that claimed to have solved `IST-3` without a funder would be selling something.**
>
> **Two dependencies decide whether the passing tests stay passed: `RR-1`, the accounting basis (**IST-2**), and `GD-08`, the founder's position in the priority order (**IST-6**). Both are `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`. Neither is resolvable here.**

## XXVIII.2 The distinction that makes the declaration honest

**[P]** This instrument contains numbers. **They are all counts of its own structures, and none is an economic parameter. The distinction is stated because a document that claimed to contain no numbers at all would be either false or unreadable.**

| Kind | **Examples from this instrument** | **Permitted?** | **Why** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A count of the instrument's own structures** | Thirty rules · seventy-two decomposition items · eleven roles · four flows · seven categories · eleven lifecycle stages · six solvency lines · eight priorities · fourteen hidden capitals · eighteen failure modes · fifteen gaming vectors · seventeen record types · eight record fields · seven candidate models · ten model attributes · six incentive tests · twelve audits · fifteen open governance decisions · fifteen reserved human decisions · fifteen systems | **`YES`** | **These are the architecture's shape. They are required by the task, they are derived by enumeration rather than asserted, and none of them is a value anyone is paid, charged, targeted or measured against** |
| **An economic parameter** | A rate · a percentage · a cap · a multiplier · a budget · a target · a threshold · a reserve amount · a coverage figure · a price · a margin | **`NO`** | **EA-9. Every one is expressed as a named variable whose determination is assigned to a named process** |
| **A historical figure quoted from a source** | The illustrative percentages preserved at **[appendix.txt:6364–6369]** | **`NOT RESTATED HERE`** | **EA-10. They are classified below and are not reproduced, because reproduction invites adoption. They appear in `0` calculations in this instrument** |

## XXVIII.3 The classification of every numeric item this instrument touches

**[P]** Required by the standing delta-audit rule. **Every item is classified into exactly one of seven classes.**

| Class | **Items in this instrument** | **Count** |
|---|---|---|
| **`SOURCE BLUEPRINT FIGURE`** | The illustrative percentages preserved at **[appendix.txt:6364–6369]** and the figures quoted in the corpus's derivations. **Not restated. Not adopted. Now also *historical* under `FD-1`, which is a second and independent reason not to adopt them** | **`0 ADOPTED`** |
| **`ILLUSTRATIVE`** | — | **`0`** |
| **`PILOT TARGET`** | — **No pilot is created or authorised (EA-28)** | **`0`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL CONTROL`** | The priority order · the joint tier · the six solvency lines · the four flows and five pools · the eight prohibited crossings · the seven categories · the eleven stages and seven human gates · the eight governance functions · the eight record fields · the eleven anti-MLM rules · the four detection tests · the ten prohibited inputs · the six dignity-relevant controls | **Enumerated in Parts VI … XXI — `NO VALUE ATTACHED TO ANY`** |
| **`PROPOSED`** | **P-6** the seeded float · **`MI-1`** the mission instrument · **MLM-8 … MLM-11** · **AG-01 … AG-15** · the five new AI economic functions · the funding map · the shortfall rule's **SS-0** gate · **AC-2, AC-3** | **All `PROPOSED — REQUIRES APPROVAL`, none operative** |
| **`FINAL-APPROVED`** | — | **`0`** |
| **`REQUIRES VALIDATION`** | Every item in §XXVIII.4 | **Twenty-two** |

## XXVIII.4 The variable register — every parameter, and who determines it

**[P]** Where this architecture needs a value, it defines a variable and assigns its determination. **No variable is filled. `[M]` is the corpus's own notation: *"a value or relationship this model needs in order to run at all, which does not exist in the source. Always written as a named variable"* **[S-B:28]****

| **V-07** | **The reserve coverage integer N** | **XIV.3, GD-09** | Governance decision + professional validation | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-08** | **The founder compensation cap** | **XV.5, X.6, P-3** | **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED (beneficiary recusal)`** | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION + FOUNDER DECISION`** |
| **V-09** | **The float's cap, term and renewal count** | **MD-05, P-6, REC-12, AG-12** | The named source's terms + governance ratification | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-10** | **The statutory rates, bases and due dates** | **REC-10, F-05, JT-2** | Tax and labour professionals | **`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** |
| **V-11** | **The organisation service fee** | **XII.1 L2, priority 5** | Tier-1 amendment + professional validation | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-12** | **The social-impact contribution and its surplus conditions** | **XIII.2 R-A, priority 7** | Tier-1 amendment + professional validation | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-13** | **The decision-latency period** | **DL-1, ND-6** | Governance decision | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-14** | **The accrual-ageing escalation period** | **AC-5, ND-6** | Governance decision | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |

| **V-16** | **The point at which a role ceases to be volunteerable** | **`D-8`, VB-5, GD-10** | Founder decision + professional validation | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** |
| **V-17** | **The venture cost-to-collection constraint at approval** | **LC-2, HG-4, F-02** | Governance decision | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |

| **V-19** | **The `MI-1` source, restriction and allocation rule** | **XIII.3, GD-13** | Governance decision + professional validation on restricted-fund handling | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-20** | **The audit-sampling thresholds at each tier** | **AU-1 … AU-12** | Governance decision, once a population exists | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-21** | **Reinvestment's position and any cap upon it** | **X.7, GD-07** | Tier-1 amendment + professional validation | **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** |
| **V-22** | **Insurance cover, limits and exclusions** | **HC-11** | **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL ONLY — NO COVERAGE IS ASSERTED`** | **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |

> ### **`TWENTY-TWO VARIABLES. TWENTY-TWO EMPTY. NOT ONE FILLED BY THIS INSTRUMENT, AND NOT ONE LEFT UNASSIGNED.`**
>
> **This is the whole of the numbers discipline: **a parameter is not omitted, it is *located*.** An architecture that leaves a value blank invites someone to fill it in quietly; an architecture that names the value, names its determiner and names the evidence required makes the filling-in a governed act.**

# PART XXIX — PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REGISTER

## XXIX.1 The rule, and the relationship to the corpus's own register

**[P]** Bound by **EA-19, EA-20**. **The corpus holds fifty-two professional-validation items, of which nineteen were assessed as `FORMULABLE` and none as discharged. This register does not replace that one; it formulates the questions that this architecture specifically cannot answer, in the six fields the task requires: `QUESTION → WHY IT MATTERS → DECISION DEPENDENCY → PROFESSIONAL → EVIDENCE REQUIRED → STATUS`.**

| Standing rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **1** | **No item in this register is resolved by this instrument.** A formulated question is not an answer |
| **2** | **No item may be resolved by repetition, by a founder's assertion, by a corpus reference, or by the passage of time** *(Rules K-4, K-8, J-31)* |
| **3** | **The four verbatim markers are used wherever they apply:** `STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE` · `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL` · `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE` · `REQUIRES COUNSEL` |
| **4** | **Insurance is addressed only as a `QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`. No coverage is asserted, assumed or implied** |
| **5** | **The organisation may not pay for this advice today.** `A-N16` blocks disbursement and **`PP-04`** restricts instruction to the founder as a natural person. **Instruction is therefore a personal act, and that fact is recorded rather than worked around** |

## XXIX.2 The register — PVR-01 … PVR-11

| # | **QUESTION** | **WHY IT MATTERS** | **DECISION DEPENDENCY** | **PROFESSIONAL** | **EVIDENCE REQUIRED** | **STATUS** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PVR-01** | **What legal form, if any, can hold this architecture's obligations — and can it contract, employ, hold money and be sued?** | Every `SIGNS` cell in **XX.2** is `BLOCKED` without it. Sixteen economic acts, zero executable | **`SR-01`; `GD-01`; `GD-05`; the whole of Parts X … XXII** | **Indian corporate and governance counsel** | **A constitutive document, a registration and an opinion on capacity. **A Git repository, a domain, a brand usage or a founder statement is not evidence** *(Rule I-31)*** | **`OPEN — UNRESOLVED`** |
| **PVR-02** | **Are the people performing verified work employees, contractors, volunteers, or something the architecture has no word for?** | It determines whether priority 1 is a legal obligation or a promise, whether withholding applies, and whether **JT-2** is even coherent | **XI.3, XI.5, REC-06, REC-08, REC-10** | **Labour and employment counsel + a chartered accountant** | **A written classification per role class, with the tests applied** | **`OPEN — `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE` AND `REQUIRES COUNSEL``** |
| **PVR-03** | **Is partial payment of earned compensation lawful, and if not, what is the lawful alternative when the collection is short?** | **SS-0** is the gate on the entire shortfall rule. If partial payment is unlawful, the correct act is suspension of work, not pro-rata reduction — a materially different architecture | **XI.5, F-07, JT-1 … JT-5** | **Labour counsel** | **An opinion on minimum-wage and timely-payment obligations for the asserted jurisdiction and classification** | **`OPEN — BLOCKS THE SHORTFALL RULE`** |
| **PVR-04** | **Is an unpaid, accrued, recorded claim a debt — and against whom, when no legal person exists?** | **AC-3** currently requires that an accrual be described to the worker as a moral record. **Whether that description is accurate is not this instrument's to decide** | **XI.6, REC-09, F-03, F-07** | **Counsel + chartered accountant** | **An opinion on enforceability, and on the accounting treatment of an accrual with no obligor** | **`OPEN — `BLOCKED BY SR-01``** |
| **PVR-05** | **Is revenue recognised on collection or on invoice?** | It decides whether workers are paid in a delayed month, whether a team is above **S-1**, and whether **IST-2** passes. **RR-2** records both until it is answered, which is a workaround and not an answer | **RR-1, RR-2, REC-04, REC-16, IST-2, V-04** | **Chartered accountant** | **A stated accounting policy, with the basis and its consequences** | **`OPEN — `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION``** |

| **PVR-20** | **What are the record-retention periods for each of the seventeen record types?** | **DR-9** leaves it open because it depends on the entity form. **Retention is not a technicality: an accrual destroyed before its period expires is an unpaid worker with no proof** | **DR-9, V-15, REC-01 … REC-17** | **Counsel + chartered accountant** | **A retention schedule** | **`OPEN`** |
| **PVR-21** | **May the organisation instruct and pay professionals at all, before an entity and a mandate exist?** | It determines whether this entire register can be worked through by the organisation or only by the founder personally. **`PP-04` restricts instruction to the natural person; `A-N16` blocks payment** | **XXIX.1 rule 5, GD-12, HC-1, HC-10** | **Counsel** | **An opinion on capacity to instruct and to pay** | **`OPEN — THE ANSWER DECIDES WHO CAN ACT`** |
| **PVR-22** | **Does an unpaid control role carrying legal exposure — the four segregated hands, and the protection function — create personal liability for the person holding it?** | **`D-8`** requires a stated point at which a role ceases to be volunteerable. **Until this is answered, the architecture is asking four people to hold exposure that may be personal, for nothing, and is not telling them so** | **XI.7 D-8, VB-5, V-16, GD-10, XII.2 FM-2** | **Counsel** | **An opinion per role. **This is the most urgent item in the register that concerns a person rather than a structure***** | **`OPEN — `REQUIRES COUNSEL``** |

# PART XXX — PILOT ARCHITECTURE

## XXX.1 The status of any pilot

**[P]** Bound by **EA-28, CF-19's removal by FD-1**. **This instrument creates no pilot, authorises no pilot, budgets no pilot and sets no threshold for one.**

| Measure | **State** |
|---|---|
| **Is a pilot authorised?** | **`NO`** |
| **Gate status** | **`GATE P0 = 0 of 10` — 22 of 22 Tier-0 items outstanding** |
| **Pilot budget** | **`NONE — AND NONE MAY BE STATED` (EA-9, task §26)** |
| **Pilot thresholds** | **`NONE — AND NONE MAY BE STATED`** |
| **Is the old circularity still operative?** | **`NO — ANTECEDENT REMOVED BY FD-1.`** The circularity was *"the pilot needs the blueprint's economics; the blueprint's economics need the pilot's measurements."* **FD-1 removes the blueprint as a required source, so the circle is broken at its root. **This is the one thing `FD-1` genuinely unblocked***** |
| **Is a pilot therefore possible?** | **`NO — BLOCKED ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS.`** `SR-01` *(no legal person)*, `A-N16` *(no mandate, 0 of 5)*, `GD-01` *(no approver)*, **GF-3** *(no compensation formula)* and **PVR-01 … PVR-22** |

## XXX.2 What a pilot would have to measure

**[P]** Measurements, not targets. **Each is a question a pilot could answer that nothing else can, and each is stated without a threshold because a threshold stated now would be an invented number.**

| # | **Measurement** | **What it would settle** | **Which variable it fills** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PM-01** | **The actual interval between delivery and collection, per category** | Whether **LC-2** is satisfiable at all, and whether priority 3 is a funding rule or an allocation rule in practice | **V-06** |
| **PM-02** | **The actual direct-cost-to-collection relationship, per venture** | Whether a standing-start venture can reach **S-3** without a float | **V-17, P-6** |
| **PM-03** | **The real cost of acquiring a first customer in a neighbourhood, expressed in hours and in trust rather than in money** | Whether **IX.4**'s finding holds, and at what scale a City pipeline becomes necessary | **RT-21's dependency** |
| **PM-04** | **The hours of invisible work actually performed per period, by system** | **`IST-3` — the failure this architecture cannot fund.** AU-10 would have a population for the first time | **HC-5, HC-10, GD-02** |
| **PM-05** | **The tenure of a volunteer, and the organisation's own cost of processing one** | Whether **`D-6`**'s measurement changes any decision, and where **`D-8`**'s boundary falls | **V-16** |
| **PM-06** | **The number of periods the joint tier could be met from an actual collection** | Which solvency line a real team reaches — **X.8's only honest health measure** | **V-07** |
| **PM-07** | **Whether a mixed-work split can be recorded honestly by the person performing it** | Whether **MW-1 … MW-6** and **AL-4** work in practice or only on paper | **V-02** |
| **PM-08** | **Whether segregation of duties is achievable at eleven people, and if not, which compensating controls actually hold** | **F-11 — currently open and assumed unresolvable. A pilot could show whether it is** | **GD-11** |
| **PM-09** | **Whether the seventeen record types can be kept without software, at what cost in hours** | **HC-12, and §XXI.1's paper-only condition** | **DR-9** |
| **PM-10** | **Whether any of the seven categories can pass G1 … G4 with a real customer and no entity** | **Part VII's central open fact: 0 of 7 categories are approved today** | **V-18** |

## XXX.3 The pilot's conditions, if one is ever authorised

| Condition | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **PC-A** | **Paper-only.** No application, dashboard, ledger system, payment system, identity system, membership platform, AI system, automation, badge, ranking or leaderboard may be built for it (**EA-21, EA-26, §18 of the delta-audit brief**) |
| **PC-B** | **No new organisational layer.** A pilot may not create a City, State or National structure, and may not create a role that this architecture does not contain |
| **PC-C** | **No member pays anything.** **MLM-3, MLM-5, `D-3`, T4** — and a pilot participant is a member |
| **PC-D** | **No beneficiary is a customer.** **BP-1 … BP-5** |
| **PC-E** | **Nobody is punished for reporting a failure.** **`G-12`, IST-5** — a pilot in which failure is career-ending produces concealment instead of measurement |
| **PC-F** | **Every measurement in §XXX.2 is published to the participants,** including the ones that show the pilot failing |
| **PC-G** | **The compensation formula must exist and be published before anyone is recruited into the pilot** — **`D-1`, F-06** |
| **PC-H** | **The pilot may not be the evidence source for a document the pilot itself requires.** *(Rule J-31's principle, preserved: a reference to a document is not the document)* |
| **PC-I** | **A pilot measures. It does not approve a category, set a rate, authorise a payment, or amend a rule** |

## XXX.4 Success and failure conditions

**[P]** Stated architecturally, because a numeric success condition would be an invented target.

| Success condition | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **PSC-1** | **Every measurement in §XXX.2 has an answer, including the ones that are `zero`, `unknown` or `worse than expected`** |
| **PSC-2** | **No member paid anything at any point, and **T4** returns zero** |
| **PSC-3** | **No reward in the pilot varied with any count of people, and **T1** returns zero correlation** |
| **PSC-4** | **Every person who performed verified work can state what they earned, under which formula version, and what remains accrued** |
| **PSC-5** | **Every record type required by the pilot was kept, and every gap was recorded as a gap** |
| **PSC-6** | **At least one variable in **V-01 … V-22** moved from `REQUIRES VALIDATION` to a determined value, on evidence** |
| **PSC-7** | **No beneficiary was charged, solicited, converted or exposed** |

| Failure condition | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **PFC-1** | **Any money was received from a participant for participation** — **veto, immediate stop** |
| **PFC-2** | **Any reward varied with a count of people** — **veto, immediate stop** |
| **PFC-3** | **A payment was made with no published formula, or under a retroactively changed one** |
| **PFC-4** | **A record was created to satisfy the pilot rather than to record a fact** |
| **PFC-5** | **A measurement was withheld because it was unfavourable** |
| **PFC-6** | **Software, a dashboard, a ranking or an automation was built** |
| **PFC-7** | **A beneficiary became a customer, a data source or a testimonial** |
| **PFC-8** | **A person was made to bear a cost personally** — **`D-3`, X-8** |
| **PFC-9** | **A governance question was resolved by the pilot rather than referred** — **ND-4, EA-24** |

> ### **`SEVEN SUCCESS CONDITIONS, NINE FAILURE CONDITIONS, FOUR OF THEM VETOES. NOT ONE CONTAINS A NUMBER.`**

## XXXI.1 The implementation dependency chain

**[P]** Ordered, because an economic architecture that does not state its own sequence will be implemented out of order — and the most common out-of-order failure is spending before there is an approver. **No step may be performed before the steps above it. Nothing here authorises any step; the chain states what each step requires.**

| # | **Step** | **What it unlocks** | **Depends on** | **Who must act** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ID-1** | **Instruction of qualified Indian corporate and tax counsel on `SR-01` / `LDR-U08`** | Everything below. **Nineteen of twenty-two PVR items depend on it** | Nothing — **it is the first act available** | **The founder, as a natural person. The organisation may not pay: `A-N16`, `PP-04`, PVR-21** | **`AVAILABLE NOW — NOT TAKEN`** |
| **ID-2** | **Entity form determined by the full chain: `OPTION SET → CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS → PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION → HUMAN DECISION`** | Legal capacity | **ID-1** | Counsel advises; a human decides. **No entity is selected by this instrument (EA-20)** | **`BLOCKED ON ID-1`** |
| **ID-3** | **A constitutive document executed** | The end of **FA-4**, and with it the impossibility of the Krytos pairings | **ID-2** | The founder and counsel | **`BLOCKED ON ID-2`** |
| **ID-4** | **Registrations and identifiers obtained** | The ability to compute priority 2 at all | **ID-3** | Counsel and a chartered accountant | **`BLOCKED ON ID-3`** |

| **ID-6** | **A governance layer appointed: an approver who is not the beneficiary of what is approved** | Thirteen `UNASSIGNED` approval cells; **HD-1 … HD-15**; **GD-01** | **ID-1**, and a decision by the founder | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** — the three options at **XXIV.2** | **`OPEN — GD-01`** |
| **ID-7** | **Worker classification determined per role class** | Whether priority 1 is an obligation or a promise; **JT-2**; **SS-0** | **ID-1, ID-2** | Counsel and a chartered accountant — **PVR-02, PVR-03** | **`OPEN`** |
| **ID-8** | **The compensation formula specified, approved and published** | **REC-07, REC-08**; recruitment may lawfully begin (**`D-1`**) | **ID-7**, and **V-01 … V-03** | Founder decision + National approval + professional validation | **`OPEN — GF-3`** |

| **ID-12** | **The seeded float `P-6` sourced, capped and termed** | **XIV.4**'s cold start; **LC-2**; the first weak month | **ID-5**, **ID-6**, **V-09** | A named external source + governance ratification | **`OPEN — PROPOSED ONLY`** |

> ### **`EIGHTEEN STEPS. TWO ARE AVAILABLE TODAY — `ID-1` AND `ID-13`. NEITHER HAS BEEN TAKEN.`**
>
> **`ID-1` requires one person to instruct one professional, and it cannot be paid for by the organisation. `ID-13` requires the seventeen record types to begin being kept on paper, at a cost in hours that nobody is measuring. **Every other step is blocked behind one of these two.***

## XXXII.4 What happens next, and what this instrument refuses to do

**[P]** Bound by **EA-30** and **DA-31**.

| Refusal | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **1** | **This instrument creates no successor.** It does not generate a v1.3-L, a v1.4, an implementation plan, a budget, a pilot authorisation or a stage that the previous one did not earn |
| **2** | **It does not upgrade any gate.** `GATE P0 = 0 of 10` and 22 of 22 Tier-0 items remain outstanding. **Designing an architecture is not discharging a gate** |
| **3** | **It does not amend any register.** 0 registers amended, 0 gates moved, 0 figures adopted, `CLM-01` unused, `CF-22` open, `GC-01` `UNRESOLVED`, `A-N16` blocked at 0 of 5, the pilot not authorised |
| **4** | **It does not resolve `FD-1`.** The founder's reclassification of the blueprint is recorded and acted on as a working premise, marked **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE RATIFICATION`** as a controlled amendment. **Ratification is a human act that has not occurred** |
| **5** | **It does not declare the architecture complete.** It declares it **buildable**, which is a different word with a different meaning: the components exist, are labelled, are sourced and are consistent — and none of them can operate |
| **6** | **It does not soften the six open failures.** F-05, F-08, F-10, F-11, F-14 and F-17 are open today, before any revenue exists to conceal them. **That is the most useful thing in this instrument, and it is a description of the present, not a prediction** |

> ### **`THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE NEXT ACTION IS `ID-1`.`**
>
> **One person, one instruction, three questions that do not require an entity: is partial payment of earned compensation lawful; is revenue recognised on collection or on invoice; do the four unpaid control roles carry personal liability for the people holding them. **It cannot be paid for by the organisation. It requires no architecture, no revenue, no approval, no layer and no number. Everything else in Parts XXX and XXXI waits behind it.***

## XXXII.5 The closing principle

> ### **`FIRST MAKE THE INSTITUTION LEGALLY AND OPERATIONALLY DECIDABLE. THEN DECIDE. THEN VALIDATE. THEN IMPLEMENT. THEN PILOT.`**
>
> **This instrument is a design, and a design is the first of those five things wearing the clothes of the second. It makes the economic system *decidable* — every question is named, every authority is located or recorded as absent, every parameter is a variable with a determiner, every failure has a chain, every mechanism has a provenance and a label. **It does not decide. It cannot. Eleven people, no entity, no mandate, no approver and no formula are not a governance; they are the reason one is needed.***
>
> **What has been built is the economic system that belongs to the architecture that exists — not to the team that was described in a historical document, not to a structure that has not been constituted, and not to a set of numbers that nobody has approved. The mechanisms that survived scrutiny are retained and say so. Everything that belonged to the old team is rejected and says so. And where the current architecture has no answer, the instrument says **`REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`**, **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`** or **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** — which is the only honest thing an architecture can say about a question it does not have the authority to answer.**

---

**`VERDICT: CURRENT ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE — DESIGNED, NOT DEPLOYABLE`**
